# The ntm compatibility inventory

nhm is a drop-in for ntm (AGENTS.md rule 4). This file holds a row for
each ntm verb and each `--robot-*` flag at the tracked commit,
`9d5ed97796e37fa7b7c89bc3ba4087c144f0102c` (`NTMCommit` in
`internal/cli`), as ntm's release build has them (`-tags
ensemble_experimental`): 113 verbs, 2 hidden verbs and 152 robot flags.

The table has three parts:

1. **The callers' forms:** the forms that ACFS, its lessons, brenner and
   ru call (plan-v8 2.12). They come first.
2. **The other verbs,** by name. Where part 1 covers a verb only in part,
   part 2 has a row for the rest of ntm's form.
3. **The other robot flags,** by name.

Each deferred row names a child bead of nhm-2p6, the epic of the port
plan. The plan (order, sizes, the engines the rows share) is on nhm-syq.

Rules:

- Accepting a flag and ignoring its behaviour is not compatibility. A form
  is `implemented` only when its behaviour and output are ntm's and a test
  checks them.
- `deferred` names the bead that implements the form. `waived` names the
  operator's ruling.
- Upstream contract tests (ntm's own types and goldens) and caller tests
  (what ACFS, brenner and ru assert) are kept apart. Where a caller expects
  another shape than ntm's tracked type, the row says so.
- `nhm compatibility status` reads this table. Keep one row per line, and
  the disposition as the first word of the last cell.
- A robot row starts with its own flag, and no flag starts two rows:
  `--robot-capabilities` lists one command per robot row, and a robot flag
  that nhm does not answer gives its row's disposition as the hint.
- A **Gaps (rule 4)** note in an implemented row names what nhm does not
  do yet of that verb or flag, and the bead that does it.
- A path, default or `NTM_*` name that a row gives as ntm's is ntm's
  only. nhm uses its own (`<project>/.nhm/`, `~/.config/nhm/`, `NHM_*`;
  AGENTS.md rule 4), and so does the port of a deferred row. Where nhm's
  value is not ntm's, an implemented row says so.

## The callers' forms

| Form | Callers | Output | nhm | Test | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `--robot-status` | ACFS `swarm_status.sh`, `support.sh`; ru | ntm `StatusOutput`: `schema_id`, `schema_version`, `generated_at`, `system`, `sessions[]` of `StatusSessionHeader`, `summary`; extras under `_nhm`. ru's driver reads `.sessions[S].panes[0]` (`ru:13799`) and greps compact JSON for `"name":"ru_sweep_…"` (`ru:13612`); ntm 9d5ed977 has neither (`sessions` is a list, and its JSON is indented, as nhm's): ru's older shapes, for the RU fork's driver (the operator's F.7 ruling, question 2, 2026-10-13; nhm-hir). **With herdr down,** as ntm's `buildLiveStatus`, which swallows a failed collection: success, exit 0, `sessions: []`. `swarm_status.sh` passes on exit 0, and ru takes exit 0 as "robot mode available". nhm also names `herdr` in `degraded_sources`, so `overall_status` is `degraded`, and keeps herdr's error in `_nhm.sources.herdr`: a caller can tell herdr down from no sessions. **Not as ntm:** with no socket known (no `--socket` and no `HERDR_SOCKET_PATH`), `DEPENDENCY_MISSING`, exit 1. ntm always finds tmux's default socket. nhm has no default, so it cannot tell whether sessions exist, and an empty status would tell ru and `swarm_status.sh` that none do (nhm-fyq). **Gaps (rule 4):** `--robot-limit` or `--limit` and `--robot-offset` or `--offset` page `sessions[]` and add `pagination` and `_agent_hints` (the `--robot-limit` row, nhm-2p6.2); nhm answers `NOT_IMPLEMENTED`, exit 2, for the `--robot-*` names and `INVALID_FLAG` for `--limit` and `--offset`. ntm accepts `--bead-limit` and changes nothing for status; nhm refuses it with `INVALID_FLAG` (deferred: nhm-2p6.3) | `internal/cli/robot.go`, `internal/core/snapshot/robot.go`, `internal/core/snapshot/snapshot.go` | `TestRobotStatus`, `TestRobotStatusAndSnapshotWithNoHerdrServer`, `TestRobotStatusAndSnapshotWhenHerdrAnswersBadly` | implemented |
| `--robot-snapshot` | robots that bootstrap | ntm `SnapshotOutput` subset: `schema_id`, `ts`, `sessions[].agents[]` with `pane` = slot `N.M`, `summary`, `alerts`, `active_incidents`, `replay_window` (unsupported). **With no herdr server** (its socket is gone or refuses), as ntm's with no tmux server: success, exit 0, `sessions: []`, `herdr` in `degraded_sources` and herdr's error in `_nhm.sources.herdr`. If herdr is not installed either (`HERDR_BIN_PATH`, else `herdr` on `PATH`), `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` "herdr is not installed", exit 1, as ntm's "tmux is not installed"; nhm looks for herdr only when no server answers. **Not as ntm:** a herdr that answers badly or late gives `DEPENDENCY_MISSING`, where ntm's failed list gives `INTERNAL_ERROR`. Both exit 1, and no ACFS or ru script reads this code. With no socket known, `DEPENDENCY_MISSING`, as for `--robot-status` (nhm-fyq). **Gaps (rule 4):** `--since=RFC3339` switches ntm to the delta form, `SnapshotDeltaOutput{success, timestamp, ts, since, changes[]{type, session, pane, data}, truncated}`; a bad time is `INVALID_FLAG` "Use an ISO8601/RFC3339 timestamp". The delta form needs a change log. `--bead-limit` (default 5) sets the ready and in-progress beads per category in the snapshot's work section. `--robot-limit` or `--limit` and `--robot-offset` or `--offset` page `sessions[]` (the `--robot-limit` row, nhm-2p6.2). nhm refuses `--since`, `--bead-limit`, `--limit` and `--offset` with `INVALID_FLAG`, and the two `--robot-*` paging flags with `NOT_IMPLEMENTED`. nhm has no `work` section: ntm fills it from the work view that `work-snapshot` prints (ready, blocked and in-progress beads, summary, triage, graph, verification), which needs nhm-2p6.21's work engine (deferred: nhm-2p6.3) | `internal/cli/robot.go`, `internal/core/snapshot/snapshot.go` | `TestRobotSnapshot`, `TestRobotStatusAndSnapshotWithNoHerdrServer`, `TestRobotStatusAndSnapshotWhenHerdrAnswersBadly` | implemented |
| `--robot-activity=S` | ru | ntm `ActivityOutput`: `agents[]` with ntm's state enum. ru reads `.panes[0].activity` (`ru:13728`), and its driver passes a pane id and reads a top-level `.state` (`ru:13863`, `13931`); ntm 9d5ed977 answers neither shape: ru's older shapes, for the RU fork's driver (the operator's F.7 ruling, question 2, 2026-10-13; nhm-hir). **Gaps (rule 4):** ntm reads `--panes` (N, W.P or %N; a bad selector is `INVALID_FLAG`) and `--activity-type`, with `--type` as the shared fallback. nhm defines neither, so both fail with `INVALID_FLAG` "flag provided but not defined" (deferred: nhm-2p6.3) | `internal/cli/robot.go` | `TestRobotActivity` | implemented |
| `--robot-tail=S --lines=N` | ru | ntm `TailOutput`: `panes{slot: {type, state, lines[]}}`. **Gaps (rule 4):** ntm also reads `--panes` (N, W.P or %N; a bad one is `INVALID_FLAG`) and `--fresh` (a live capture per pane, not the observer's buffer). nhm defines only `--lines`, `--socket`, `--no-mail` and `--robot-format`, so `--panes` and `--fresh` are `INVALID_FLAG`. nhm reads each agent's lines live, so it can accept `--fresh` as is; `--panes` takes the slot `N.M` or a herdr pane id, as for `--robot-send` (deferred: nhm-2p6.3) | `internal/cli/robot.go` | `TestRobotTail` | implemented |
| `--robot-capabilities` | brenner, robots | ntm `CapabilitiesOutput`: `commands[]` with unsupported ones marked `unavailable` and a reason. **Gaps (rule 4):** ntm reads `--capability-command`, `--capability-category`, `--capability-search` and `--capability-compact`; nhm refuses all four with `INVALID_FLAG`. ntm's `CapabilitiesOutput` also has `surfaces[]`, `attention`, `filter` and `pagination_contract_violations`, and its release build lists 146 commands with their parameters and examples. nhm lists one command per `--robot-*` row of this file, with no parameters, and also lists the global modifiers that have rows (`robot-format`, `robot-limit` and the others) as commands (deferred: nhm-2p6.2) | `internal/cli/robot.go` | `TestRobotCapabilities` | implemented |
| `--robot-format=json` | every robot call | JSON; `auto` is JSON; `toon` is refused with `INVALID_FLAG`. **Agent types** are ntm's `agentTypeString`: opencode is `oc` in every read, in `--robot-spawn` and in `spawn --assign`'s agent id. pi, which ntm does not know, is listed as `pi` in every read, as ntm lists a registered plugin agent type; ntm reads a pi pane as `user` and skips it in activity, health and wait, but nhm's pi is an agent that nhm spawned. The operator kept this reading (2026-10-23; nhm-y7q): activity, health and wait watch pi agents. **Exit codes** are ntm's `ExitCodeForResponse`: 2 only for `NOT_IMPLEMENTED`, 1 for every other error. With no herdr, every robot call answers `DEPENDENCY_MISSING`, exit 1, except `--robot-status` and `--robot-snapshot`, which answer as ntm's do with tmux down (their rows; nhm-fyq). ntm answers `SESSION_NOT_FOUND` for a session read there, because it cannot tell a stopped tmux from a missing session; nhm can. **Upgrade edge:** a `--robot-spawn --spawn-assign-work` or `spawn --assign` rerun, with the same `--op-id`, of an opencode assignment made before nhm-i3k fails closed: the recorded type `opencode` no longer matches `oc`, so the item fails with the claim conflict and nothing is sent twice (nhm-i3k). **Gaps (rule 4):** ntm takes the format from the flag, else `NTM_ROBOT_FORMAT`, else `NTM_OUTPUT_FORMAT`, else `TOON_DEFAULT_FORMAT`, else the config's `[robot.output] format`. nhm reads none of these sources: with `NTM_ROBOT_FORMAT=toon`, ntm writes TOON and nhm writes JSON. For a bad value ntm's `INVALID_FLAG` hint is "Use json, toon, or auto"; nhm's error says "nhm writes json only" and its hint is "use --robot-format=json". nhm enters robot mode only when the first argument is a `--robot-*` flag; ntm takes robot flags in any position. `nhm --lines=50 --robot-tail=S` and `nhm --panes=2 --robot-send=S …` print "nhm: unknown command" and the usage on stderr, exit 2, not a JSON error (deferred: nhm-2p6.2) | `internal/cli/robot.go`, `internal/integrations/kinds/kinds.go` | `TestRobotFormat`, `TestRobotReadsTypeOpencodeAsOcAndListPi`, `TestRobotDependencyMissingExitsOne` | implemented |

| `--robot-spawn=S --spawn-dir=R --spawn-cc=N --spawn-cod=N [--spawn-agy=N] --spawn-no-user --spawn-safety --spawn-wait [--ready-timeout=Ns] --timeout=60s --robot-format=json [--dry-run] [--spawn-prompt=T] [--trust-folder] [--ignore-capacity]` | ACFS `swarm_launch.sh`, newproj `screen_success.sh`, ru, brenner | ntm `SpawnOutput`: `success, session, working_dir, effective_project_key, dry_run, assignments: [], admission.decision, agents[]\|would_create[] {type, pane: "N.M", name, title, ready, error}, stagger.schedule[]`; the admission is the robot profile (unknown quota refuses unless `[quota] admit_unknown` names the pool; a red or unknown capacity refuses unless `--ignore-capacity`); no kickoff unless `--spawn-prompt`. A refusal is ntm's: `RESOURCE_BUSY`, `spawn admission refuse: <reason>` and the admission's hint; a dry run reports the decision and succeeds, as ntm's does (nhm-2p6.4.12). `--spawn-safety` refuses a session that exists with ntm's `INVALID_FLAG`, sentence (`nhm kill` for `ntm kill`) and hint, and looks again just before it makes the session. As ntm, nhm waits for the agents to be ready only with `--spawn-wait` or a prompt; without them it answers once every agent started (`ready` false, `success` true unless a start failed), and after a failed wait it sends no prompt (nhm-2p6.4.10; ACFS newproj adds `--spawn-wait`: acfs-9hkh.106). The wait is `--spawn-timeout` (or its deprecated alias `--ready-timeout`), else `--timeout`, else ntm's 30 s; a bare number is seconds. **Difference:** nhm adds `--trust-folder`, spelled as `nhm spawn --trust-folder`; ntm 9d5ed977 has no trust flag on any verb. It answers the kind's first-run folder-trust dialog (Claude Code, Codex), and nothing is trusted without it. ntm has no such flag: without `--spawn-wait`, its robot spawn returns at once and leaves the agent at the dialog, for the user or ntm's `--robot-answer-dialog`. With `--spawn-wait` or `--spawn-prompt`, ntm waits for ready, and nothing answers the dialog there either. nhm's flag answers it during that wait; without the flag an agent at the dialog ends unresolved, and without the wait it stays at the dialog, as in ntm. ACFS newproj passes it after the user consents to the new folder (nhm-cvg). **Difference:** nhm adds `--ignore-capacity`, spelled as `nhm spawn --ignore-capacity` (nhm-1pq); the spawn row says what it does. ntm 9d5ed977 has no flag that overrides its robot spawn's admission. ru's space form `--robot-spawn S --dir D --prompt P` (`ru:13762`) is refused with `INVALID_FLAG`, as ntm 9d5ed977 refuses it (no `--dir` flag): ru's older shape, for the RU fork's driver (the operator's F.7 ruling, question 2, 2026-10-13; nhm-hir). `--spawn-assign-work` has a row of its own. **Specs, names and labels (nhm-2p6.4.2):** `--spawn-cc`, `--spawn-cod`, `--spawn-grok` and `--spawn-omp` (and nhm's `--spawn-pi`) take `count[:model[:effort]]` (effort also as `model@effort`), `--spawn-oc` `count[:model]` (an effort is dropped at launch), `--spawn-agy` a count only (its model is pinned); a bare integer is a count, 0 too. A bad spec, a negative count or no agent is `INVALID_FLAG` with ntm's words and hint. The agents start in ntm's launch order: cc, cod, gmi, agy, grok, omp, oc, then nhm's pi. Each agent's `name` is ntm's: the `--spawn-names` values in order, then `<type>-<NATO word>` (`claude-alpha`); its `title` is ntm's pane title, `<session>__cc_1` (nhm's herdr pane label stays the Mail name and the model); `agent_mail_name` is the Agent Mail name, and its lowercase is the herdr name (ACFS `swarm_launch.sh` moves to it: acfs-9hkh.105). `--spawn-label=L` makes the session `<session>--L`; a session with `--` or a bad label is `INVALID_FLAG` (ntm's `config/label.go`, `internal/core/sessionlabel`). `--spawn-prompt-file` reads the prompt from a file or `-` (ntm's `loadRobotMessageInput`), and with `--spawn-assign-work` it goes ahead of each work prompt. `--spawn-claude`, `--spawn-codex` and `--spawn-opencode`, which ntm does not have, are unknown flags. **Stagger (nhm-2p6.4.10):** `--spawn-stagger-mode=none\|fixed\|smart` and `--spawn-stagger-delay` (0 to 5m; `[spawn] stagger_mode` and `stagger_delay` in nhm's config, then none and 30s) pace the prompts, `--spawn-prompt` or the work prompts, in launch order: the agent at order i gets its prompt i×interval after the first, and the answer's `stagger{mode, interval_ms, provider, warning, schedule[]{pane, agent_type, order, delay_ms, scheduled_at}}` is that plan, present only when the spawn sends prompts. smart takes the strictest provider's delay in the batch (15 s Anthropic, 10 s OpenAI, 8 s Google, else 10 s) or its learned delay in the project's `.nhm/rate_limits.json`, which nhm does not write yet (ntm learns it on its send path). A bad mode or delay is `INVALID_FLAG` with ntm's words and hint. **Gaps (rule 4):** `--spawn-gmi`: nhm has no gemini kind (deferred: nhm-2p6.4.4). `--spawn-preset=NAME`, a recipe of `--robot-recipes` in place of counts (deferred: nhm-2p6.7) | `internal/cli/spawn.go`, `internal/core/spawn`, `internal/core/sessionlabel` | `TestRobotContract`, `TestRobotSpawnRefusesWithoutObservations`, `TestRobotSpawnAgentSpecModelEffort`, `TestRobotSpawnAgySpecRefusesModel`, `TestRobotSpawnLabel`, `TestRobotSpawnPromptFile`, `TestRobotSpawnNames`, `TestParseSessionLabel`, `TestValidateLabel`, `TestRobotSpawnNoWaitWithoutSpawnWait`, `TestRobotSpawnStagger`, `TestSpawnNoWait`, `TestSpawnKickoffAllReady`, `TestResolveSpawnStaggerModes`, `TestResolveSpawnStaggerSmartProviderPriority`, `TestPacerHoldsEachSlotToItsOffset`, `TestRobotSpawnSafety`, `TestRobotReadyWait`, `TestSafetyLooksAgainBeforeItMakesTheSession`, `TestRobotSpawnTrustFolder`, `TestTrustFolder`, `TestRobotSpawnIgnoreCapacity`, `TestRobotSpawnIgnoresCapacity`, `TestAdmission` | implemented |
| `--spawn-assign-work [--strategy=S \| --spawn-assign-strategy=S]` (with `--robot-spawn`) | ACFS newproj `screen_success.sh` | ntm's consented one-shot assignment. Before anything is created, in a dry run too, the strategy (`top-n`, `diverse`, `dependency-aware`, `skill-matched`), the `[assign]` policy (nhm's config and the project's `.nhm/config.toml`, where ntm reads its `.ntm/config.toml`, merged by ntm's rules) and bv's actionable plan are checked; a refusal is `INVALID_FLAG`, `STALE_WORK_COORDINATION`, `TIMEOUT` or `INTERNAL_ERROR`. After the agents are up, each gets at most one task: claimed with br's compare-and-set as `<Mail name>/nhm-<key>` (ntm's actor is `<agent>/ntm-<key>`; a claim that an older nhm wrote with `/ntm-` stays its agent's), recorded in ntm's assignment ledger, and delivered through the send core, one operation per attempt, on the `--robot-spawn` row's stagger schedule (nhm-2p6.4.10); `--spawn-prompt` (or `--spawn-prompt-file`) prefixes each work prompt. CASS history and CM rules (best-effort, queried by the bead's title, labels and description) go above each fresh work prompt before its intent is recorded, and are reported as `cass_injection` and `memory_injection`: CASS unless `--no-cass`, when `--with-cass` or nhm's `[cass] context` asks for it; CM when `--with-memory` or nhm's `[memory] cm` asks for it (they stand in for ntm's `[cass.context] enabled` and `[memory] send_injection`, as for `--robot-send`); a replay or a later attempt sends the recorded prompt and queries nothing, and nhm records what the agent got under the delivering operation, with the bead. The answer adds `mode: "orchestrator"`, `assign_strategy` and ntm's `assignments[]`; an agent without a claimed and sent task makes it `ASSIGNMENT_FAILED`. A rerun replays the ledger, and a delivery whose outcome is unknown is never sent again. A rerun of the same `--op-id` (nhm's own: ntm's robot spawn has no `--op-id`) answers `OPERATION_IN_PROGRESS` and sends nothing while the first run still holds the operation's lock, and `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` for other choices (the strategy, `--spawn-prompt`, `--with-cass`, `--no-cass`, `--with-memory`, or no `--spawn-assign-work`). Once the first run is over, the rerun assigns as a first run does if that run reached none of its agents, by spawn --assign's rule (nhm-88n, nhm-vcf); otherwise it claims and sends nothing and replays the ledger, and an agent that the first run did not reach gets no work, as ntm gives an agent without work no prompt, and makes the answer `ASSIGNMENT_FAILED`. Differences from ntm: the preflight refuses ntm's `--require-reservation` and `--reservation-paths` with `INVALID_FLAG` (nhm reserves no files for agents; nhm-2p6.4.3); `--spawn-require-reservation` and `--spawn-reservation-paths`, which ntm does not have, are unknown flags, as in ntm; no webhooks. **Gaps (rule 4):** ntm's config turns CASS on by default (`[cass.context] enabled = true`); nhm's `[cass] context` is `off` by default, so a work prompt gets CASS only when the config or `--with-cass` asks for it (the config's keys and defaults: deferred: nhm-2p6.10) | `internal/cli/spawn.go`, `internal/cli/spawn_assign.go`, `internal/core/spawnassign`, `internal/core/assignment`, `internal/integrations/bv` | `TestRobotSpawnAssignPreflightComesFirst`, `TestRobotSpawnAssignReservationFlagNames`, `TestRobotSpawnAssignNoCass`, `TestRobotSpawnAssignReplayAfterACrash`, `TestRobotSpawnAssignReplayAssignsNoNewWork`, `TestRobotSpawnAssignReplayLockAndFingerprint`, `TestAssignClaimsAndDeliversOneTaskPerAgent`, `TestAssignKnownUnsentRetriesAsANewAttempt`, `TestAssignUnknownOutcomeIsNeverResent`, `TestDispatchNoActuationNeedsNoOperation`, `TestAssignEnrichesWorkPromptAndReplaysWithoutRequery`, `TestAssignEnrichedRetryKeepsItsPreparedContext`, `TestClaimLeavesBrWritableAfterBvRead` | implemented |
| `--robot-assign=S [--beads=a,b] [--strategy=simple\|balanced\|speed\|quality\|dependency]` | — (no ACFS, lessons, brenner or ru call; the operator's ruling, 2026-10-23, nhm-y55) | ntm `AssignOutput`: `success, session, strategy, generated_at, recommendations[] {pane_id, pane_target, agent_type, model, assign_bead, bead_title, priority, confidence, reasoning}, blocked_beads[] {id, title, blocked_by[]}, idle_agents[], unassignable_beads[], summary, _agent_hints, work_source_mismatch`; a list is `[]`, never null, as ntm's encoder writes it. `agent_type` is ntm's short type here (`cc`, `cod`, `agy`, `oc`), as ntm's session observer gives it, not `agentTypeString`'s. It reads only: it claims, mails and sends nothing, and writes no ledger file. The steps and refusals are ntm's: the session, trimmed (`SESSION_NOT_FOUND`), its project (`INTERNAL_ERROR`), the `[assign]` policy (nhm's config and the project's `.nhm/config.toml`, where ntm reads its `.ntm/config.toml`; `INVALID_FLAG`), the strategy (`INVALID_FLAG`, ntm's hint), then bv's actionable plan (`STALE_WORK_COORDINATION` with its receipt, `DEPENDENCY_MISSING`, `TIMEOUT`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`) and br's in-progress and blocked lists. The strategies are ntm's: `simple` (the default) gives the next ready bead to the next idle agent; the others run ntm's matcher. **herdr in tmux's place:** the session is a workspace (its label or id). Its project is the project of most of its bound agents, as for `nhm mail`. Its agents are that project's bound agents, in slot order. An agent is idle when the send core's delivery gate is ready and the instance's assignment ledger holds no active assignment for its binding. `pane_id` is the herdr pane id, `pane_target` the slot `N.M`. **Differences:** ntm's `_agent_hints.suggested_commands` name `ntm --robot-bulk-assign`, which nhm does not have, so nhm suggests no command. The spawn sends no kickoff of its own: an agent with a bead gets the kickoff (`--prompt`), `--init-prompt` and its work prompt in one delivery, since a working agent takes no second prompt; every other agent gets the kickoff and `--init-prompt` after the assignment, except an agent whose work prompt went or may have gone (a claimed bead, unless the ledger shows its dispatch surely failed). `--with-agent-name` puts ntm's ``You are agent `NAME`. It reads no modifiers. It reads system facts (tmux present, free disk, load), every tmux session and pane, the user's too (activity time and the last 20 lines: `no_output_5m` after 300 s, `error_state_detected`, `interactive_gate: <gate>`), and bv and br (drift, bottlenecks, keystones, priority, counts). JSON `HealthOutput`: `checked_at, system {tmux_ok, disk_free_gb, load_avg}, sessions {<name>: {healthy, agents {<pane key>: {responsive, output_rate, last_activity_sec, issue}}}}, alerts[], bv_available, bd_available, error, drift_status, drift_message, top_bottlenecks[], top_keystones[], ready_count, in_progress_count, blocked_count, next_recommended[], dependency_context`. On herdr: `tmux_ok` means herdr answers; the sessions are herdr workspaces, with agents keyed by the slot `N.M` (ntm's key is a bare index in one window: record it); herdr's blocked is the interactive gate; nhm's limit recogniser covers the rate-limit half of `error_state_detected`; herdr gives no output time, so `last_activity_sec` is -1, `output_rate` `unknown`, and no `no_output_5m`. | `spawn <session> --cc=N[:MODEL] --cod=N --agy=N …`, `add <session> …` | lessons, brenner | one line per agent (name, kind, model, slot, pane, status) and the operation id; `--json` (the root's flag) gives nhm's envelope. **Parser, label and exit (nhm-2p6.4.5):** spawn and add are on the shared parser, with ntm's flag and argument errors and exit 1 for every error, a refusal and the admission's too (they were 2 and 3; ACFS's rehearsals change with acfs-w2m3); the error is `Error: <msg>` on stderr. `-l\|--label L` is ntm's goal label: the session `<session>--L`; a session name with `--` or a bad label is refused with ntm's words. nhm's tab label is `--tab-label`. brenner's `spawn <thread> --cc=1 --cod=1 --agy=1` parses. ru's `spawn S --cc=1 --no-user` (`ru:1464`) parses: ntm's `--no-user` keeps no pane for the user, and nhm keeps none, so the flag changes nothing (the operator's F.7 ruling, question 2, 2026-10-13; nhm-hir). **Agent specs (nhm-2p6.4.1):** each agent flag takes ntm's spec: `N`, `N:model`, `N:model:effort`, or `N:model@effort` for a kind with an effort flag (claude, codex, grok, omp, pi); N is at least 1 (`count must be at least 1, got 0`). Repeated flags add up, each with its own model and effort, and the agents start in the order of their flags, as ntm's do. agy refuses a model with ntm's words (its model is pinned). grok, omp and opencode take `--model`, grok `--effort` and omp `--thinking`, as ntm launches them; an effort for a kind without an effort flag is dropped at launch. nhm's own rule: a model or an effort starts with a letter or a number, because each is an argv value of its own (ntm's charsets allow `-` first). nhm refuses, as ntm 9d5ed977 does, ACFS's `spawn S --count N` (`swarm_simulation.sh`) and several sessions in one call (the web lesson data): their row. **Order and stagger (nhm-2p6.4.10):** as ntm does, every agent starts first, then the spawn waits for them to be ready, then the kickoffs go out on the stagger schedule: the prompts are paced, not the starts. `--stagger-mode smart` takes ntm's provider delay (the `--robot-spawn` row), and `agents[].delay_ms` is each kickoff's offset from the first. **Layout:** ntm tiles all the session's panes in one tmux window (tmux's `tiled`). nhm puts up to `--per-tab` (6) agents of a project in one tab (nhm-4c4), as an even grid in reading order, also across adds: 6 is 2 rows of 3, slots N.1 to N.3 on top (the operator, nhm-cxh). A partial tab of 6: 2 panes are a third and two thirds of the width; 4 have the first column cut in two. N has the integer square root of N rows and as many columns as N needs (4 is 2x2, 9 is 3x3); a new pane takes the first cell that no pane holds as its own, so it fills a retired agent's gap. A tab off the grid (nhm 0.1.8 to 0.1.10, by hand, another `--per-tab`) is not joined. **Guards (pi, which ntm does not know; nhm-dyr):** nhm guards a pi with its own pi extension, as Claude gets the dcg hook through `--settings`: `-e <instance dir>/pi/nhm-guard-<hash>.ts --extension-policy permissive` (the one profile that lets the extension run dcg without a prompt), and `PI_EXTENSION_HOOKS_FAIL_CLOSED=1` in the pane, so a hook that fails blocks the call, and a hook that hangs (pi quarantines it after 5 s) blocks it and every later call of that pi. The extension does not load without that env, so a pi in a pane that nhm did not create does not start. The extension sends each bash command to `dcg test --robot --dialect posix` and blocks it unless dcg allows it. It blocks pi's hub, debug and subagent tools, and each tool that xdev runs but bash (screened) and pi's tools that start no program, because they may start programs that dcg cannot screen. The permissive policy, pi 0.8.0's default, applies to every extension that pi loads. A `!` shell command that a person types at pi's prompt runs with no hook that can stop it; `send` refuses one (nhm-ubi). rch and Agent Mail's stop hook are unsupported for pi. Without dcg, a pi spawn is refused unless `--allow-unguarded`, and a pi recycle is refused. nhm adds no `--yolo`, so pi asks before each tool call; whether a guarded pi gets it is the operator's decision. **Capacity (nhm's own; nhm-1pq):** a capacity provider (`[providers] capacity` in nhm's config, ACFS's `acfs capacity --guard --json`; none by default) that answers red refuses the spawn, and so does one that fails with `--require-observations` (the robot profile). `--ignore-capacity` admits both, for this spawn, with the provider's answer as a warning; it covers no quota or guard refusal, and `--force` covers no capacity. | `send <session> [prompt] (--all \| --cc \| --cod \| --agy \| --pane=N \| -t KEY [--bead ID] [--var k=v] [--file F]) [--dry-run] [--no-cass] [--with-memory=false]` | lessons, brenner | one line per target and the operation id; `--json` gives nhm's envelope with the receipts. `--with-memory` and `--with-cass` put CM's rules and CASS's hits before the prompt, best-effort (nhm.memory, acfs-9hkh.21); the CASS duplicate check is on by default (`--no-cass-check` or `--no-cass` turns it off); `--thread` sends the thread's mail with the prompt and wakes the targets (nhm.wake). As ntm's send, it types into a busy agent (`--when any`, the default; brenner's broadcast right after a spawn); an agent at a dialog (herdr's blocked) and an unsafe pane are refused. For a busy agent, `submitted` means herdr wrote the prompt and its Enter; Claude Code queues it (other kinds not checked; ntm types the same way); `state_after: working` is the turn that was already running, and `--wait` answers `ambiguous` for it, never `complete`. nhm's no-interrupt send stays as an explicit choice: `--when ready` refuses a busy agent unless `--force`. nhm's palette and the menu's send type into a busy agent too, as ntm's palette does. nhm's wake nudges and the assignment loop keep that gate (the operator's rulings, 2026-10-13; nhm-r0v). **Difference from ntm (the operator's rulings, 2026-10-24; nhm-ubi, nhm-gb0, nhm-mowp):** nhm refuses a send whose prompt, for an agent whose kind has a shell escape, starts with that escape (white space and control characters before it do not count) or holds a control character other than tab, newline and carriage return, such as `ESC [201~`, the end of a bracketed paste. Every kind has `!` until the sandbox proves that it runs no such prompt. Typed through `nhm send` in the sandbox, Claude Code 2.1.310, Codex 0.164.0, pi 0.8.0 and omp 18.12.6 ran a prompt that starts with `!` as a shell command at once, with no approval question, and dcg never screened it; omp also runs a `$` line as Python, so nhm refuses a prompt that starts with `$` to it too. opencode 1.19.4 and agy 1.4.6 took a pasted `!` as a prompt, but ran a `!` that came after `ESC [201~` as a typed key. grok 1.0.67 ran none of these forms, so it has no escape, and nhm types a `!` or a control character to it as given. A kind from config keeps `!`, and an escape it names (`shell_escape`) is one more. The check reads each target's exact prompt, template and enrichment included, and refuses the whole send before its claim, a dry run too: exit 2, with each agent, its kind and why. The palette, the menu, templates, `watch`, kickoffs and recycle's prompt go through it; CM and CASS text and recycle's handoff keep no control character but tab and newline, so nhm's own text refuses no send. A `!` on a later line goes out as typed. Before nhm types into an agent's input box (a send's prompt, a dry run too, wake's nudge and recycle's exit command), it reads the pane's last lines: herdr types the text after what the box holds, so a `!` that a person left there turns it into a shell command (proved with Claude Code 2.1.310, Codex 0.164.0 and pi 0.8.0 for a typed or pasted `!`, also one typed while the agent works, and for Codex and pi a space and then `!`; nhm-gb0, nhm-mowp). nhm refuses that target, and says why, when the box shows Claude Code's bash mode (the box starts with `!`, or its footer says `! for shell mode`), Codex's shell mode (`Shell mode` in its footer, or a composer that starts with `!` after white space) or a pi input box that starts with `!` after white space. The receipt is `refused`, with `shell_draft` (`screen_unread` when herdr cannot read the box); `--robot-send` lists the target as failed, wake skips the nudge and keeps the mail pending, recycle refuses before its claim and reads the box again right before its exit command, and the other targets get their prompts. An ordinary draft, a shell command in the transcript, a reply that quotes these marks and Claude's `?` shortcuts panel refuse nothing. Not covered: omp, opencode, agy and kinds from config, whose screens nhm does not read (their Cannot lists say so), and a `!` that a person types after nhm's read. **Gaps (rule 4):** before it delivers, ntm's send runs a redaction check on the prompt, set by `[redaction]` (default `warn`) and the root `--redact` and `--allow-secret`; nhm's send runs none (deferred: nhm-2p6.20). ntm's send also checkpoints the session before an interactive broadcast, writes analytics and audit entries, and stores its history entry redacted, with ntm's types and pane keys: the `checkpoint`, `analytics`, `audit` and `history` rows hold those gaps. ntm's other send flags are in the `send` row of part 2 (deferred: nhm-2p6.5) | `internal/cli/send.go`, `internal/core/send` | `TestSendVerb`, `TestSendWithMemoryAndFeedback`, `TestSendThreadWakesTheTargets`, `TestSendTypesIntoABusyAgentNotABlockedOne`, `TestSendRefusesAShellEscape`, `TestUISendRefusesAShellEscape`, `TestSendRefusesAShellEscapeForEachKind`, `TestSendToAKindWithoutAnEscape`, `TestSendRefusesATargetWithAShellDraft`, `TestClaudeShellDraft`, `TestCodexShellDraft`, `TestPiShellDraft`, `TestADraftInTheInputBoxHoldsTheNudge`, `TestRecycleTypesNoExitCommandIntoAShellDraft` | implemented |
