An agent's first read prints its last `--tail` lines (20); later reads take 100 lines, as ntm's. `--cc`, `--cod`, `--gmi` and `--agy` keep those kinds; `--pane` keeps one agent and wins over them. Agents that start or stop join or leave the watch, as ntm lists its panes each poll: herdr's agent list is read each round, and the model is built again only when it changes. Without `--activity`, a read that fails ends the watch (exit 1, `failed to capture pane output`); with it, the read is skipped. `--bead ID` prints `[HH:MM:SS] [<Name>] mention: line` for each new line that names the bead (a whole word, in any case; all of an agent's first read), and `bead status: S` when br show in the session's project gives a new status, every 30 s or every `--interval` given (`unknown (lookup error: …)` when br fails). `--pattern GLOB --command TEXT` watches the session's project as ntm does, with fsnotify (ntm's watcher and version, v1.10.1): recursive, without ntm's ignored names (`.git`, `node_modules`, …), a folder made during the watch watched too, and ntm's 250 ms trailing debounce: when a file that matches changes (a GLOB with a slash matches the path from the root, one without the file's name), it prints `File changed: P` (not with `--no-color`, as ntm), `Triggering command on N pane(s)...` and each failure, and sends TEXT to the agents. `--json`: ntm's NDJSON frames (`event`, `timestamp`, `session`, and `pane`, `pane_id`, `agent_type` (ntm's `cc`, `cod`, …), `lines`, `bead`, `text`, `status`, `path`, `delivered`, `failed`, `error`): `watch_started`, `pane_output`, `bead_mention`, `bead_status`, `file_change`, `dispatch`, `dispatch_error`. Not as ntm: herdr's agents, not tmux panes (the user's shell is not one), each named after its mail name, herdr name or pane id (ntm's pane title); without a session, the calling pane's workspace (ntm also infers one from the working folder or the only session); no colours, and times in UTC (ntm's are local); `--pane` takes a name, the slot N.M or a pane id, and a tmux index is refused (exit 2); an agent's new lines follow logs -f's rule (nhm-rnx): a pane's last 10 lines are its live region (the prompt box, status line and spinner that a working agent redraws in place) and a line prints only once it is above them, so an idle agent's last few lines wait for its next output; a line prints when its text is not in the last read (each copy counted), so a redrawn line prints once per change and a line whose exact text is already in the window does not print again (ntm's last-line diff reprints a redrawn window); an agent that joins prints its `--tail` lines (ntm's, its whole 100-line read); the project of `--bead` and `--pattern` is the one the session's bound agents share (for an inferred session without one, the working folder); only a change to a matching file holds back the debounce (ntm's waits for 250 ms without a change to any file), and the bursts that come during a send go as one, after it (ntm runs each burst's handler at once); inotify takes one watch per folder (ACFS's tree takes about 9,300), and when it has none left (`fs.inotify.max_user_watches`), at the start or for a new folder, nhm releases its watches, says so once on stderr and polls the project (ntm leaves the folders past the limit unwatched and says nothing); when fsnotify cannot start, nhm says so once on stderr and polls (ntm polls every `--interval`); the polling: a scan waits `--interval`, or five times the last scan's time when that is longer (ACFS's 43,000 paths take about 1 s a scan, so a change there is seen up to about 5 s late), and a matching change sends TEXT once, after a scan that sees no change to a matching file; `--command` goes through nhm send's path (core send, the owner lock, receipts) to the bound agents in any state, the calling pane left out, and an unbound agent is a failure of its own (ntm types into every target pane, the user's too); a send that fails as a whole gives a `dispatch_error` frame with its error. Why a 10-line live region (nhm-rnx, read-only on 8 working Claude panes, 16 reads 2 s apart, counts only): the old overlap rule printed the whole 20-line read in each round while the agent worked; a live region of 5 or 8 lines still printed the spinner in nearly every round (13 to 17 extra lines in 15 rounds); 10 lines hid it (0 to 2); a 20-line read cut by 10 missed 0 to 15 lines per pane that a full-height read printed, and a 30-line read missed 0 to 2. nhm's extras: agents named after the session (a mail name, herdr name, slot or pane id), `--count N` (stop after N rounds; with `--pattern`, N bursts of changes, or N scans when it polls) and `--socket` | `internal/cli/watch.go`, `internal/cli/logs.go`, `internal/core/watcher/watcher.go` | `TestWatchPrintsEachLineOnce`, `TestWatchJSONFrames`, `TestWatchFilters`, `TestWatchReadFailure`, `TestWatchAgentsJoinAndLeave`, `TestWatchSessionFromCallingPane`, `TestWatchBead`, `TestWatchFilesNotify`, `TestWatchFilesSendsCommand`, `TestWatchFilesFallsBackWhenWatchesRunOut`, `TestWatcherMatchHoldsOnlyMatchingPaths`, `TestWatcherWatchesNewDirectory`, `TestWatcherOutOfWatches`, `TestWatchRefusals`, `TestFilterPanesCanonicalizesAliases`, `TestEmitWatchJSONFrame_Framing`, `TestFreshLinesCountsTexts` | implemented (the operator's ruling, 2026-10-23: ntm's form, nhm's selection as an extra; nhm-zac; fsnotify with the polling as the fallback, the operator's ruling 2026-10-23, nhm-98l) |
| `view [session]`, `tile`, `v` | the web palette lesson (`ntm view <session>`, "alias: ntm tile"); the web lesson data | ntm's view on herdr: take the zoom off in every tab of the session's workspace (`pane.zoom` off in each tab that the snapshot's layouts show zoomed, through its focused pane, so the tab's focus stays, as ntm's tiling keeps the active pane), then focus the workspace, as `nhm attach` does. Without a session, the calling pane's workspace; outside herdr, name the session (exit 2). `✓ Zoom off in every tab of '<session>' (tabs: N, zoomed before: M); focused it; open herdr to see it`; `--json` gives nhm's envelope with the tabs and the ones unzoomed. Not as ntm: herdr lays out the panes itself, so no tiled layout is applied (nhm's spawn grid stays); herdr has no client to attach, so the focus shows in every herdr client, and outside herdr there is no session picker. Changes no agent, so it runs under either owner. **Gaps (rule 4):** with no session, ntm infers it from the cwd, takes the only session or shows a picker, and it matches a given name by prefix; nhm takes only the calling pane's workspace and exits 2 outside herdr. ntm's `--json` is `SuccessResponse {success, message}` (`tiled layout applied to 'S'`); nhm's is its envelope. ntm's unknown session is `SESSION_NOT_FOUND` with a hint on stderr, exit 1 (JSON `ErrorResponse`); nhm exits 2 with no hint line (deferred: nhm-2p6.3) | `internal/cli/view.go` | `TestViewTakesTheZoomOffAndFocuses` | implemented (the operator's ruling, 2026-10-23; nhm-k9d; it supersedes the F.7 question 3 relays of 2026-10-13) |
| `diff [session] <pane1> <pane2> [-u] [--code-only]` | the web palette lesson; the web lesson data | ntm's diff: the last 1000 lines of each pane, without control sequences, compared by ntm's `ComputeDiff` (ported, with ntm's diff-match-patch library): `Comparing A vs B:`, `Lines: N vs M`, `Similarity: P%`; `-u` adds `Diff:` and the patch text; `--code-only` compares the fenced code blocks only (ntm's parser, ported), each as `// <language>` and its content. A pane is a slot `N.M`, a herdr pane id, an Agent Mail or herdr name, or a kind (its first agent), as `nhm zoom` takes it; a tmux pane index is refused (exit 2). Without a session, the calling pane's workspace. `--json`: nhm's envelope with ntm's `DiffResult` fields (`pane1, pane2, lines1, lines2, similarity, diff`). Not as ntm: a pane's name is its Agent Mail name, else its pane id (ntm's is the tmux pane title); a session or pane that nhm cannot resolve exits 2 and a failed read exits 1, as nhm's other verbs, where ntm exits 1 for every error. **Gaps (rule 4):** ntm also takes a pane by the end of its tmux title: `cc_1` matches the pane titled `myproject__cc_1`, so `ntm diff cc_1 cod_1` compares the panes titled `…__cc_1` and `…__cod_1`. nhm takes `cc_1` only as an agent's name, so the form finds no pane unless an agent has that name (exit 2); it needs a herdr counterpart of ntm's `<type>_<n>` names (deferred: nhm-2p6.3) | `internal/cli/diff.go`, `internal/core/panediff` | `TestDiffComparesTwoAgents`, `TestComputeDiff_*`, `TestCountLines`, `TestParse*`, `TestNormalizeLanguage` | implemented (the operator's ruling, 2026-10-23; nhm-k9d; it supersedes the F.7 question 3 relays of 2026-10-13) |
| `checkpoint save <session> -m M`, `config agents\|theme\|model` | the web palette lesson; the web lesson data (`ntm checkpoint save <session> -m`) | nhm answers `unknown command` (exit 2). ntm's checkpoint captures the panes, their scrollback and the git state in a store of its own, which nhm does not have. ntm 9d5ed977 has no `config agents`, `theme` or `model` (its `config` has `init`, `path`, `set`, `show`, `diff`, `get`, `edit`, `reset` and `project`): the lesson's own words | — | — | waived: the operator's ruling (2026-10-23; nhm-k9d): lesson differences; it supersedes the F.7 question 3 relays of 2026-10-13 |
| `serve`, `web` (the web UI) | — | — | — | — | waived: F.19 (2026-10-12) |

## The other verbs

The first row is ntm's root flags, which every verb takes. The verbs follow by name.

| Form | Callers | Output | nhm | Test | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `--json`, `--config F`, `--no-color`, `--ssh H`, `--redact MODE`, `--allow-secret`, `--profile-startup`, `-V` (ntm's root flags, on every verb) | — | ntm's root flags reach every verb, before or after it: `--json` (machine output), `--config F` (default `~/.config/ntm/config.toml`), `--no-color`, `--ssh user@host` (run on a remote host), `--redact off\|warn\|redact\|block` with `--allow-secret` (the redaction mode of this call), `--profile-startup` (startup timings). The root also takes `-V`/`--version` (one line, `ntm version X`). ntm exits 1 for a usage error and for most errors (`Error: <msg>` on stderr, or the robot error envelope under `--json`); a few paths keep exit 2 (`NOT_IMPLEMENTED`, `cass` without cass, `health` with an error, a failed `repo sync`), as their rows say. **On herdr:** nhm's root is ntm's cobra root with pflag (cobra 1.10.2, pflag 1.0.10), so the root flags parse before or after any verb (nhm-2p6.1.1). `--json` reaches every verb. `--no-color` sets nhm's `NHM_NO_COLOR` for the call, where ntm sets its `NTM_NO_COLOR`; nhm's theme and styles read `NHM_NO_COLOR`, `NHM_THEME`, `NHM_BACKGROUND`, `NHM_BG`, `NHM_ANIMATIONS`, `NHM_REDUCE_MOTION`, `NHM_ICONS` and `NHM_USE_ICONS` in place of ntm's `NTM_*` names, and bv `NHM_BV_TIMEOUT`. `-V`/`--version` prints `nhm version X`. `--ssh H` runs the whole call on H, robot flags too: `ssh [-t] -- H nhm <args without --ssh>` (`-t` when stdin and stdout are terminals), and ssh's exit is the call's. The call goes over once cobra has parsed it, so a `--ssh` that is another flag's value stays that value (a legacy verb's own flags are not known: there only the root's value flags count), and `--help` and `--version` answer here, as ntm's do; an empty `--ssh` is none, as in ntm (nhm-2p6.1.7). A robot call takes the root flags in front of its first `--robot-*` flag (nhm-2p6.1.9) and after it (nhm-2p6.1.10; a word after a robot flag that takes a value is that value, as pflag reads it): `--config` takes effect for it; `--json`, `--no-color` and `--profile-startup` change nothing that it writes (ntm prints no profile after a machine call); `--redact` and `--allow-secret` give the robot `NOT_IMPLEMENTED`, exit 2. `--config F` replaces the path of nhm's config file for the call (`NHM_CONFIG`, made absolute): the kinds, spawn's capacity, `[assign]`, the keys, the enrichment, `[lifecycle] bead_dbs` and reap's keep list read F; an empty `--config` is none, as in ntm. F's kinds take effect before the parse (the kind registry reads each config file once; Run parses the call once to find `--config`), so spawn, add and robot spawn take F's kind flags (nhm-2p6.1.7); robot spawn parses the call first to find a `--config` after its robot flag (nhm-2p6.1.10). `--profile-startup` prints ntm's profile on stderr after a command that succeeded (ntm's profiler, ported: the `cli_init` span and the verb's, the phases, the top spans and the recommendations), and nothing for a `--json` call, as ntm's PersistentPostRun does. An error that no verb writes is `Error: <msg>` on stderr and exit 1, or under `--json` the robot error envelope on stdout (`INVALID_FLAG` for a usage error, with ntm's hints), as ntm's `Execute` does; a verb on the shared parser gets ntm's flag errors, combined short flags (`-iv`, `-C3`) and ntm's did-you-mean. **Gaps:** `--redact` and `--allow-secret` are refused until nhm-2p6.20 (`Error: --redact is not in this nhm yet …`, exit 1; under `--json`, `NOT_IMPLEMENTED`, exit 2). A verb that its bead has not moved to the shared parser keeps its own flags (Go's `flag`: `-name` for a long flag, no combined short flags) and its exit codes (2 for a refused form; nhm-2p6.1.4). After a robot call's `--robot-*` flag, a root flag parses as the robot flags do, with Go's `flag` (`-json` too, and Go's text for a bad value). **Rulings (the operator, 2026-10-24):** `--ssh H` runs the whole call on H (`ssh [-t] H nhm <args without --ssh>`); `--config F` replaces the path of nhm's config for the call, and the schema follows nhm-2p6.10; exit 1 for every error, ntm's behaviour everywhere | `internal/cli/root.go`, `internal/cli/flagerrors_ntm.go`, `internal/cli/ssh.go`, `internal/cli/robot_root.go`, `internal/cli/config_flag.go`, `internal/profiler/profiler.go` | `TestRootFlagsBeforeTheVerb`, `TestRootFlagsAfterTheVerb`, `TestRootJSONBeforeTheVerb`, `TestRootFlagNotBuiltIsRefused`, `TestRootErrorUnderJSON`, `TestCombinedShortFlags`, `TestShortFlagWithValue`, `TestFlagErrorHints`, `TestPassThroughVerbArguments`, `TestSSHRunsTheWholeCallOnTheHost`, `TestSSHAfterTheParse`, `TestStripSSHKeepsAFlagValue`, `TestSSHExitCodePassesThrough`, `TestSSHEmptyOrMissing`, `TestConfigFlagSetsNHMConfig`, `TestConfigPathHonoursNHMConfig`, `TestConfigFlagAddsItsKindFlags`, `TestDefaultRereadsANewConfigPath`, `TestProfileStartupPrintsTheProfile`, `TestRobotCall`, `TestRootFlagsInFrontOfARobotFlag`, `TestRootFlagsAfterARobotFlag`, `TestConfigAfterARobotFlag` | deferred: nhm-2p6.20 (`--redact` and `--allow-secret`) |
| `activity [session] [--cc] [--cod] [--gmi] [--agy] [--grok] [--omp] [--pane P] [-w\|--watch] [--interval MS]` | — | ntm finds the session when none is given. It classifies each agent pane from its screen and its output marks. Text: `Session: S`, a table `Win Pane PaneID Agent State Velocity Duration`, then a box `N total, N available, N busy, N problems`. The type flags (booleans; there is no `--oc`) and `--pane` (a name or an index) filter the agents. `-w` refreshes every `--interval` milliseconds (2000); with `--json` it writes NDJSON, one frame per tick. JSON: `{success, session, captured_at, agents[]{pane (int), window_index, pane_id, agent_type, state, confidence, velocity, duration, state_since}, summary{STATE: n}, error}`; an error writes `success:false`, exit 1. **Gaps:** nhm needs the session. The type flags, `--pane`, `--watch`, `-w` and `--interval` are unknown flags. The text is a table `SLOT TYPE STATE DISPATCH`, with no velocity or duration. The JSON is nhm's envelope around ntm's robot `ActivityOutput`: `pane` is the slot string, and velocity, duration and `state_since` are missing. **On herdr:** `snapshot.ActivityFor` maps herdr's states to ntm's enum, and the filters apply to its result; the watch can reuse status's watch loop. herdr gives no output timing: velocity and duration need state-change times, which nhm could keep from herdr's events | `internal/cli/status.go` | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.3 (session reads) |
| `add <session> [--cc\|--cod\|--gmi\|--agy\|--grok\|--cursor\|--windsurf\|--aider\|--oc\|--omp\|--ollama N[:model]]… [-l\|--label L] [--cass-context Q] [--cass-context-days N] [--cass-context-limit N] [--no-cass-context] [--prompt T]` | — | ntm adds agents to a session that exists. `--label L` targets the session `<session>--L`. The specs are spawn's, but `--ollama` takes a spec here (an int on spawn). ntm's admission (pressure and slots) holds through the loop. ntm makes a checkpoint first when `[checkpoints] before_add_agents` asks for one, and takes the next pane indices from the live panes and the Agent Mail registry. A prompt goes only with `--prompt`. Text: `Adding N agent(s) to session 'S'...`, then `✓ Added N agent(s) (total M panes now)`. JSON: `AddResponse{generated_at, session, added_claude, added_codex, added_gemini, added_antigravity, added_grok, added_ollama, added_cursor, added_windsurf, added_aider, added_opencode, added_omp, total_added, new_panes[], agent_mail, seat_skips, admission}`; a failure writes `agentLifecycleFailureResponse`. A missing session: `session 'S' does not exist (use 'ntm spawn' to create)`, exit 1. **Gaps:** nhm's add is its spawn with `Add`; its specs are ntm's (nhm-2p6.4.1). `--gmi`, `--cursor`, `--windsurf`, `--aider` and `--ollama` are unknown flags; the `--cass-context` flags are spawn's (nhm-2p6.4.6); `-l` targets `<session>--L` (nhm-2p6.4.5). The text and the JSON are ntm's (nhm-2p6.4.9; ACFS's readers: acfs-9hkh.108): `AddResponse` with the admission, nhm's result, scope, operation id and warnings under `_nhm`, and nhm's agent lines on stderr; there are no `seat_skips`. The session is looked up as ntm's add looks it up: `session "S" not found (available: …)`, a unique prefix in text, the exact name under `--json`; a session gone after the lookup is ntm's `session 'S' does not exist (use 'nhm spawn' to create)`. A prompt goes only with `--prompt`, as ntm's (no `[prompts]` defaults for add), and alone, as spawn's; with none, add does not wait for the agents, except with `--assign` or `--trust-folder` (nhm-2p6.4.6). nhm makes no checkpoint (nhm-2p6.23), admits by quota, guards and capacity (exit 1), and always adds new panes and cleans up after a failure, as its spawn does (the operator, 2026-10-24). As ntm's, add runs in the session's folder (nhm-2p6.4.9): the one project of the session's active agents, where ntm takes the one project of its live panes' current folders; agents of two projects refuse in ntm's words (`resolve live project for session "S": panes span multiple project roots: A (panes); B (panes)`, exit 1); a session with no agent takes the cwd's work tree, as ntm then takes the cwd. nhm's `--project` still names the folder, and `add --assign` keeps the cwd's work tree. With `--assign`, the text is spawn's and the JSON keeps nhm's envelope (nhm-2p6.4.11). nhm also takes spawn's other flags (`--assign`, `--stagger-mode`): a superset, without the `[spawn]` defaults, and without spawn's `--safety` and `--stagger`. **On herdr:** core/spawn with `Add`; `pane.split` joins the grid | `internal/cli/spawn.go`, `internal/cli/spawn_output.go`, `internal/cli/spawn_output_ntm.go`, `internal/core/spawn` | `TestAddJSONIsAddResponse`, `TestAddText`, `TestAddFindsTheSession`, `TestAddRunsInTheSessionFolder` | deferred: nhm-2p6.4 (spawn and add) |
| `adopt <session-name> [--cc P] [--cod P] [--gmi P] [--agy P] [--grok P] [--cursor P] [--windsurf P] [--aider P] [--oc P] [--omp P] [--ollama P] [--user P] [--auto-name] [--dry-run] [--by-window]` | — | ntm's adopt turns the panes of a tmux session that ntm did not make into ntm agents. Each type flag takes panes as `0,1,2`, a range `0-5` or `window.pane`. `--auto-name` (default true) retitles each pane as `S__cc_1`. `--by-window` takes each window's sole pane for exactly one type flag and refuses a window with more panes. It refuses no pane, a repeated spec, a bare index that two windows share and a pane given twice. It sets each pane's title and agent type and registers an Agent Mail identity for each agent pane (best effort; none for `--user`). Text: `✓ Adopted session 'S'` or `[DRY RUN] Would adopt session 'S'`, the totals, the counts by type and one `[idx] old → new` line per pane; a failure prints `✗ Failed to adopt session: <err>`, exit 1. JSON `AdoptResult {success, session, adopted_panes[], total_panes, agents{}, dry_run, error?}`. ntm's own example `adopt S --by-window --cc` fails in ntm (`--cc` needs a value; `--cc=` works). **On herdr:** nhm's `adopt` binds one existing Agent Mail identity to one pane, another job, and refuses with exit 2. herdr detects each pane's agent, so a type flag is an override at most. Per pane: `core/spawn`'s identity step, `bindings.Adopt` and the pane label; `window.pane` is the slot `tab.pane`; `--by-window` takes each tab's sole pane. **Rulings:** ntm's form always has a type flag or `--by-window`, and nhm's form never has one: may one `adopt` serve both, chosen by those flags? | `internal/cli/cli.go`, `internal/cli/bindings.go` | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.11 (pane lifecycle) |
| `agents list\|show <type>\|stats\|recommend -t T [--type K] [-f F] [-l L] [--tokens N]`, `agents profiles\|ls`, `agents profile\|get` | — | ntm's capability profiles of its four agent types (claude, codex, gemini, antigravity), with performance from ntm's assignment ledgers and the model from the config. `list`: `TYPE MODEL CONTEXT SPECIALIZATIONS`; `--json` a list of `AgentProfile {type, model, context_budget, specializations, preferences, performance}`. `show <type>`: `Agent Profile: <type>` and its sections; an unknown type is `unknown agent type: x`, exit 1. `stats`: `AGENT TASKS SUCCESS AVG TIME LAST ACTIVE` (`-` when not measured); `--json` a map of type to performance. `recommend` (`-t/--title` required; `--type` task, bug, feature, epic or docs) scores the four types: `Task Analysis` … `Recommendation: <type> (score: N)` and `All Scores:`; `--json` `{task, recommended, scores[{agent, result}]}`. Bare `agents` prints the help, exit 0. **On herdr:** no herdr part. nhm has ntm's `AggregateOutcomes` (`internal/core/assignment`) and agent types; ntm's profile seed is not ported. Models come from nhm's kinds. The four types are fixed: pi and nhm's config kinds get no profile | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.7 (spawn catalogs) |
| `analytics [--days N] [--since YYYY-MM-DD] [--format text\|json\|csv\|prometheus] [--sessions]` | — | ntm sums its event log (`~/.config/ntm/analytics/events.jsonl`, kept 30 days) over the last `--days` (30), or from `--since`, which wins. Text is a summary; `--format` also takes `json`, `csv` and `prometheus` (or `prom`), and any other word gives text, with no error; `--json` forces JSON. JSON: `AnalyticsStats{period, total_sessions, total_agents, total_prompts, total_chars_sent, total_tokens_estimated, agent_breakdown, sessions[], error_count, error_types}`; the breakdown is by ntm's short types (`cc`, `cod`); `--sessions` adds a row per session. No log file gives empty stats, exit 0; errors exit 1. Writers: spawn, add, send and robot send, and the resilience monitor (crash, restart). **On herdr:** no herdr call. nhm writes no event log: add a writer of ntm's event lines to the spawn core (spawn and add), the send core, and recycle and reap, then port the reader | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.19 (history and analytics) |
| `approve <token>`, `approve list [--limit N] [--offset N]\|deny <token> [--reason R]\|show <token>\|history` | — | ntm keeps approval requests in its SQLite state. `approve <token>` approves one (no token: help, exit 0), `deny` refuses one (`--reason`), `show` prints one, `list` the pending ones (`--limit` 0 is all), `history` the decided ones. The approver is `$NTM_USER`, else `$USER`, not checked. Each decision also goes to the audit log. With an slb session in the environment, a request is also queued in slb, and its requester cannot approve it. Only `locks force-release` writes requests (when the policy's `force_release` is `approval`); an approval is used once. JSON: `ApprovalResult{success, id, action, resource, status, error}`; `list`: `ApprovalsListOutput{success, pending[], count, total_matches, has_more, pagination, _agent_hints}`. Errors exit 1. **On herdr:** no herdr call: an approvals table in nhm's store and an slb integration. It is of use only with `locks force-release` (nhm-2p6.16) | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.20 (safety and policy) |
| `assign [session] [--auto] [--strategy S] [--beads a,b] [--limit N] [--agent T] [--template impl\|review\|custom] [--template-file F] [--pane P] [--prompt T] [--clear IDS] [--clear-pane P] [--clear-failed] [--reassign ID] [--to-pane P] [--retry ID] [--retry-failed] [--watch] [--reserve-files] [--dry-run]` | — | ntm gives ready beads to the idle agents of a running session. Its other flags: `--cc-only`, `--cod-only`, `--gmi-only`, `--to-type`, `--ignore-deps`, `--force`, `--auto-reassign`, `--stop-when-done`, `--watch-interval`, `--delay`, `--idle-threshold`, `--timeout`, `--quiet`, `--verbose`, `--repo`, `--with-cass`, `--no-cass` and `--with-memory`. The flags pick the mode, in this order: clear (`--clear`, `--clear-pane`, `--clear-failed`), the `[assign]` policy check, `--reassign`, `--retry` or `--retry-failed`, `--watch`, `--pane` (needs `--beads`), then the plan: ntm's selection over bv's triage. `--strategy`: `balanced` (the default, else the config's), `speed`, `quality`, `dependency` or `round-robin`; another name is refused. Text: `Task Assignment Recommendations`, `Strategy: S`, the counts, one line per assignment with its reason, then the blocked and skipped beads. The plan runs with `--auto`, else after `Execute all assignments? [y/N]` on stdin (EOF cancels); `--dry-run` only clears `--auto`. The run prints as `spawn --assign` does. `--reserve-files` (default true) reserves in Agent Mail the paths that each bead names. `--json`: ntm's `AssignEnvelope` {command, subcommand, session, timestamp, success, data, warnings, error{code, message, details}}, not `--robot-assign`'s type; codes such as INVALID_FLAG, CLAIM_CONFLICT, TARGET_BUSY, PANE_NOT_FOUND. **On herdr:** the session is a workspace, its agents are its bound agents in slot order (as for `--robot-assign`), a pane is a slot `N.M` or a herdr pane id, and `--watch` reads herdr's agent states and br. It reuses spawnassign's selection, the assignment ledger and the send core. **Rulings:** `--reserve-files` defaults to true, but nhm reserves no files for agents; ntm's persona filter for the templates; webhooks. | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.9 (work distribution) |
| `attach [session-name]`, `a` | — | ntm's form of attach. With a session, ntm updates the session's Agent Mail activity and attaches (switch-client inside tmux, attach-session outside). A name matches by prefix; an ambiguous prefix is an error. An unknown session prints `Session 'S' does not exist.`, `Available sessions:` and the list, then asks `Create 'S' with default settings?` and runs create (10 panes), or exits 0. With no session it runs ntm's list. It prints nothing of its own on success. `--json` does not attach: it prints `SessionResponse {session, exists, attached?}`, and a missing session is `exists:false`, exit 0. **On herdr:** nhm's attach focuses the workspace (`workspace.focus`); `attached` is the workspace's focus. Gaps: the alias `a`; the prefix match; the unknown-session flow (nhm exits 2 with `no such workspace`); `--json` gives nhm's envelope `{workspace_id}` and focuses; no Agent Mail activity update. **Rulings:** the bare form shows nhm's agent list today; it follows the ruling on `list` | `internal/cli/attach.go` | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.3 (session reads) |
| `audit show <session> [--since D] [--until D] [--type T] [--limit N] [--offset N]`, `audit search <pattern>\|verify <session>\|export <session> [--format json\|csv] [-o F]\|list` | — | ntm's audit log is one file per session, writer and day under `$XDG_DATA_HOME/ntm/audit/`. Each line is an `AuditEntry` (`timestamp, session_id, event_type, actor, target, payload, metadata, prev_hash, checksum, sequence_num`), chained by SHA-256. Every ntm command writes a start and an end entry; send, spawn, kill, approvals, saved sessions and others write their own. `show` filters by time (RFC3339 or `1h`, `7d`), `--type` and `--limit` (100); `search` by `--sessions`, `--type`, `--actor` (`user`, `agent`, `system`), `--target` (a glob), `--days` (30) and `--limit` (50). JSON: `AuditQueryOutput{success, entries[], count, total_matches, has_more, scanned, scan_capped, duration_ms, pagination, _agent_hints}`. `verify` checks the chain (`{success, session, verified, files[], error}`); a broken chain fails, exit 1. `list` lists the files; `audit` alone prints help. Errors exit 1. **On herdr:** no herdr call. nhm's operations table is not an audit log. Add a writer with ntm's line format and chain, called once per command by nhm's dispatcher and by the cores that ntm audits; without the writers, `show` finds nothing | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.19 (history and analytics) |
| `beads` | — | A stub that points to br. It prints one line, `ℹ Beads are managed by the br CLI. Run 'br --help' for usage.` (the icon has colour only on a TTY without `NO_COLOR`), and exits 0. It takes and ignores any arguments, and `--json` changes nothing. Its help lists `br ready\|list\|show\|create\|close\|sync --flush-only`. ntm's bead work is in its robot flags (`--robot-beads-list`, `--robot-bead-*`), not in this verb. **On herdr:** no herdr part and no engine: a few lines in `internal/cli` | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.13 (br and bv wrappers) |
| `bind [-k\|--key KEY] [--overlay] [--show] [--unbind]` | — | ntm binds a tmux key to its palette: `bind-key -n KEY display-popup -E -w 80% -h 70% "ntm palette"` in the running server, and the same line in `~/.tmux.conf` under `# NTM Command Palette`. The key is F6, or F12 with `--overlay` when `--key` is not given; it must match `^[a-zA-Z0-9\-\^]+$`, else `invalid key format: …`. `--overlay` binds a 95% by 95% popup of `ntm dashboard --popup --inferred <session>` under `# NTM Dashboard Overlay`. Text: `✓ Bound F6 in current tmux server`, `✓ Added F6 binding to ~/.tmux.conf`, `Press F6 in tmux to open the command palette.` `--show` prints `✓ Found binding:` and the line, or `→ No F6 binding found in tmux.conf` and `Run ntm bind to set it up.`; `--unbind` removes the binding. No JSON. Errors exit 1. **On herdr:** nhm's `bind` (the `palette`, `bind` row) writes herdr's `[[keys.command]]` from `[keys]` in nhm's config. The operator's ruling (2026-10-24, nhm-2p6.6): ntm's flags with nhm's defaults, so a bare `bind` binds `[keys]` (prefix+a, prefix+d, prefix+m) and no F key. `-k`/`--key K` binds K to the palette as well, and `--overlay` binds the board (ntm's dashboard overlay) to F12, or to K, with ntm's key check (`invalid key format: … (allowed: a-z, 0-9, -, ^)`) and ntm's lines: `✓ Bound K in herdr's config PATH`, then `Press K in herdr to open the command palette.` (or `the agent board`). The key stays in nhm's block, where its comment names `nhm bind --key` or `--overlay`, so a later `nhm bind` (as `acfs update` runs it) keeps it; a new key takes an old one's place. `--show --key K` prints ntm's `✓ Found binding:` and the binding, or `→ No K binding found in herdr's config`; `--unbind --key K` removes K alone and records no opt-out (`✓ Removed K binding from PATH`), and refuses a key that `[keys]` sets. **Not as ntm:** `bind` stays on its own parser, because its `--config` names herdr's config, which the root's `--config` (nhm's config) would shadow; so its refusals keep nhm's text and exit 2 (ACFS reads exits 2 and 5 of `nhm bind`: nhm-2p6.1.4, acfs-w2m3). `--key` does not go with `--print-client` | `internal/cli/bind.go`, `internal/ui/bind.go` | `TestBindKeyFlag`, `TestBindOverlay`, `TestBindRefusesBadKey`, `TestBindShowAndUnbindKey`, `TestBlockKeysAndExtras`, `TestMergeExtras` | implemented |
| `bugs list\|notify\|summary\|watch [path]` | — | UBS findings for a path (default `.`). `list [--severity critical\|warning\|info] [--limit 20] [--all]` reads `<path>/.ntm/scan_cache.json` (1 h), else runs `ubs --format=json <path>` (60 s) and saves the cache; with neither filter, critical and warning only. Text: `Bug Findings (N total)`, per finding an icon, the message, `file:line:col` and a `💡` hint, then `Critical: N  Warning: N  Info: N`, or `✓ No issues found`; `--json` `{findings, totals, path}`. `summary` reads the cache only: `--json` `{path, totals, healthy, timestamp}`; no cache: a hint, exit 0 (JSON: `success:false`, exit 1). `notify [--project KEY] [--dry-run]` mails each reservation holder its findings (`[Scan] N issues in …`) and a digest to the other agents; with Agent Mail down it still prints `✓ Notified agents …`. `watch [--session S] [--interval D] [--once] [--force] [--verbose]` needs `[bugs] push_routing = true` or `--force` (else `PERMISSION_DENIED`, exit 1). Each tick (5m) nudges the holder's idle pane about new findings (10 m cooldown per pane), mails the rest as a digest, and files an attention item when Agent Mail is down; text `[ts] findings=N new=N nudged=N deferred=N digested=N`, `--json` one `bugsWatchTick` per tick. Without ubs: text `UBS not installed. Install: …`, exit 0; JSON `DEPENDENCY_MISSING`, `available:false`, exit 1. **On herdr:** the holder's pane is nhm's binding (agent name to pane), idle is herdr's state, and the nudge goes through `core/send`; fingerprints and cooldowns in `internal/store`, the outage item in the attention feed (`core/snapshot`). A new `internal/integrations/ubs` holds ntm's scanner | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.15 (ops tools) |
| `cass status\|search <query>\|insights\|timeline\|preview <prompt>` | — | Wraps the cass binary (`[cass] binary_path` overrides it; 30 s timeout). `status` runs `cass status --json`: `CASS Index Status`, or cass's `StatusResponse`. `search <query> [--agent A] [--workspace W] [--since D] [--limit 10] [--offset 0]` runs `cass search --json`: `Search Results (N of M)`, or `SearchResponse {query, limit, offset, count, total_matches, hits, _meta, aggregations}`. `insights [--since 7d]` aggregates a `*` search by agent and workspace: `Agent Insights (Since 7d)`, or `Aggregations`. `timeline [--since 24h] [--group-by hour\|day\|none]`: `Activity Timeline (24h): N sessions` and a table, or `TimelineResponse`. `preview <prompt> [--max-results 5] [--max-age 30] [--format markdown\|minimal\|structured] [--max-tokens 500]` shows what a send would inject: the keywords, the hits, the block and `Token estimate: ~N tokens`; `--json` `{success, query, filter, keywords, total_hits, filtered_out}`. `search` and `preview` take one argument: quote a phrase. Without cass: text `CASS is not installed.` and install lines, exit 2; JSON `NOT_IMPLEMENTED` with a `hint`, exit 2. Other failures are `INTERNAL_ERROR`, exit 1. Bare `cass` prints the help, exit 0. **On herdr:** no herdr part. `internal/integrations/cass` runs `search --robot` today; it needs `status`, `timeline` and a `--json` search with ntm's options and types. `preview` needs ntm's filter and inject formats (ntm's keyword extraction is ported). `nhm memory <query>` is close to `preview`, in nhm's frame | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.14 (knowledge tools) |
| `changes [session]` | — | ntm lists the recent file changes of the cwd's project, newest first, from the change ledger in ntm's state database; a session argument filters. ntm's session monitor writes the ledger: every 15 s it reads `git status` in each working tree and gives each change to the session, or to a worktree's agent. Text: `Recent File Changes`, a rule, one row per change (`A`, `D` or `M`, the path, the agents, a conflict mark, the age); `No file changes recorded.` with none. `--json`: a bare array of `RecordedFileChange` {timestamp, session, agents[], change{path, type, before, after}}. Outside the session's project it shows nothing for that session. **On herdr:** nhm has no recorder. It needs a sampler (the maintain loop or the watcher) and a file-change table in nhm's store. A pane's cwd and binding give the agent only when agents have their own worktrees. | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.16 (Agent Mail and files) |
| `checkpoint save <session> [-m M] [--scrollback N] [--no-git]`, `checkpoint list\|show\|restore\|delete\|verify\|export\|import` | — | ntm captures a session to `~/.local/share/ntm/checkpoints/<session>/<id>/`: each pane's launch spec and scrollback (`--scrollback` 1000 lines), the layout, git (branch, commit, dirty counts, a patch; `--no-git` skips it), the assignment store and a bv triage summary. `restore <session> [ref]` (an id, prefix, name, `last`, `latest` or `~N`) builds the tmux session again with fresh agents (`--as`, `--dry-run`, `--inject-context`, `--attach` and more). `list`, `show`, `delete`, `verify`, `export` (`tar.gz` or `zip`) and `import` manage the store. Save: `✓ Checkpoint created: ID`; JSON `{id, session, created_at, description, pane_count, working_dir, has_git, assignments_count, assignments, bv_summary, …}`. Restore JSON: `{success, session, source_session, checkpoint_id, panes_restored, context_injected, …}`; a failure names its `stage`. Errors exit 1. ntm also checkpoints by itself, on by default: before an interactive broadcast `send` and before an `add` of 3 or more agents (10 kept per session). The lessons' `checkpoint save <session> -m M` keeps its own waived row. **On herdr:** `layout.export` gives a tab's split tree, `pane.read` (`source: recent`) the scrollback that herdr keeps, the bindings the kinds, models and native sessions. Restore is `workspace.create` and `layout.apply`, then nhm's spawn core per agent and the send core for the context. nhm's herdr client has neither layout call yet | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.23 (save and restore) |
| `claim <bead-id> [--agent A] [--ttl D]`, `claim check <bead-id>`, `claim list` | — | ntm writes a soft claim as a file, `.ntm/claims/<id>.json` {agent, bead_id, claimed_at, expires_at}, in the cwd's project; it calls no br, Agent Mail or tmux. The id takes letters, digits, `_` and `-`. An active claim refuses (`bead X is already soft-claimed by A until T`); an expired one moves to `.ntm/claims/expired/`. `--agent` defaults to `AGENT_NAME`, then `NTM_AGENT_NAME`, then `USER`, else an error; `--ttl` is 5m and must be over 0. A bead named `check` or `list` cannot be claimed. Text: `claimed: X by A until T`; check gives `unclaimed: X` or `expired: X was claimed by A until T`; list gives one tab-separated line per claim or `no active soft claims`. `--json`: `SoftClaimCheckResult` {success, bead_id, state, claim}; list `SoftClaimListResult` {success, claims[]}. Errors exit 1. **On herdr:** no herdr call. A straight port that keeps `.ntm/claims`, so ntm and nhm see the same claims. It is not the br claim of `spawn --assign`. | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.16 (Agent Mail and files) |
| `cleanup [--dry-run] [--max-age H] [-v] [--force]` | — | Deletes stale ntm temp files from the top level of the temp directory: 11 name patterns such as `ntm-lifecycle-*`, `ntm-prompt-*.md`, `ntm-mail-*.md`, `.handoff-*.tmp` and `rotation-*.tmp`, older than `--max-age 24` hours, or all of them with `--force`; a directory goes whole. Arguments are ignored. Text: ` NTM Cleanup` (or `(Dry Run)`), the entries by pattern with `✓`, `✗` or `○`, then `Deleted: N files/directories (size)` and `Skipped: …`, or `✓ No stale NTM temp files found in /tmp`. In a dry run a recent entry also shows `✓`. `--json` `cleanupResponse` (with the robot time fields) `{dry_run, max_age_hours, results[{path, type, size_bytes, age, age_hours, mod_time, deleted, error, pattern}], total_files, total_size_bytes, deleted_files, deleted_size_bytes, skipped_files, error_count}`. Exit 0, also when a delete fails. **On herdr:** no herdr part. nhm's temp names differ (`nhm-bind-*`, `nhm-fit-*`, `nhm-test-*`): the port matches nhm's names. **Rulings:** match ntm's names too, to clean a host that ran ntm? | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.10 (config and setup) |
| `codex palette-state\|preflight\|replace-goal\|wait-goal-engaged [session] [--session S] [--pane N] [--lines N] [--select replace\|cancel] [--timeout S] [--interval-ms N]` | — | ntm's codex group reads one Codex pane and classifies its screen; bare `codex` prints the help, exit 0. `palette-state` (`--session` required): idle, slash_palette_open, goal_palette_primed, dialog_open or unknown. `preflight`: a state (codex-live, goal-completed, usage-limit, shell-no-codex, …) and `recommended_action` proceed, wait, refuse, respawn or alternate_pane. `replace-goal --select replace\|cancel` (default replace): cancel sends Escape; replace sends `1` and Enter only when the modal is interactive and shows that the old goal is closed, else it refuses. `wait-goal-engaged --timeout 10 --interval-ms 500`: polls the screen to engaged, engaging, dialog_stuck, unconfirmed or respawn_required. `--pane` is a tmux index (0); `--lines` 500. Text is a labelled block (`State:`, `Action:`, `Markers:`). JSON: robot envelopes with `PaletteStateResult {state, session, pane, provenance_hash, captured_lines, markers_matched[], timestamp_source}`, `CodexPreflightResult`, `CodexReplaceGoalResult` and `CodexWaitGoalEngagedResult`. An error is a JSON envelope on stdout even without `--json`, exit 1: `INVALID_FLAG`, `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `PANE_NOT_FOUND`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`, `RESOURCE_BUSY`, `PROMPT_SEND_FAILED`, `TIMEOUT`. **On herdr:** `pane.read` (visible, or recent N). herdr's blocked does not say which dialog and has no goal counters, so port ntm's classifiers. The keys go through a core function beside interrupt (`core/wait`), not a second send path. `--pane` and the JSON `pane` map to the slot | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.12 (agent screens) |
| `completion bash\|zsh\|fish\|powershell` | — | Prints cobra's completion script for the shell on stdout (fish with descriptions). At TAB time the script calls ntm's hidden `__complete` command for verbs, flags and values. It takes exactly one argument; an unknown shell is `Error: unsupported shell: x`, exit 1. `shell`'s script loads it (`source <(ntm completion zsh)`). **On herdr:** no herdr part. nhm's dispatcher is not cobra (a `flag` set per verb), so nhm has neither the generator nor `__complete`. The port needs a table of nhm's verbs, subcommands and flags (or its own `__complete`) and its own four scripts | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.10 (config and setup) |
| `config init\|path\|show\|diff\|get <key>\|edit\|reset --confirm\|validate [--all] [--fix]\|migrate [--dry-run]`, `config set projects-base <path>`, `config project init [--force]` | named in brenner's spec `specs/cockpit_runbook_v0.1.md` (`config project init`); no script calls it | Manages ntm's TOML config (`--config`, else `NTM_CONFIG`, else the XDG path). `init` writes the defaults and refuses an existing file. `path` prints the path. `set projects-base <path>` is `set`'s only child. `show` prints the merged TOML (global and the project's `.ntm/config.toml`); `--json` a map of chosen sections. `diff`: `Configuration differences (N):`; `--json` `{count, diffs[{key, path, default, current, source}]}`. `get <key>` reads a dotted path (`--json` `{key, value}`). `edit` creates the file if absent and runs `$EDITOR`, else `vi`. `reset` needs `--confirm` (else exit 1), deletes the file and writes the defaults. `validate` checks the config, project, recipes, personas and policy files: `ValidationReport {success, valid, error, results[], summary}`, exit 1 when not valid. `migrate` removes dead keys and keeps a `.bak.<unix>` backup: `{path, clean, dry_run, backup_path, removed_count, changes, …}`. `project init` makes `.ntm/` in the cwd (`config.toml`, `palette.md`, `templates/`, `pipelines/`, `personas.toml`), registers the project with Agent Mail (`ensure_project`) and writes its `agent_mail` keys. Bare `config` prints the help, exit 0. **On herdr:** no herdr part. nhm's config is not ntm's schema: each package reads its own tables of nhm's `config.toml`; nhm reads only `[assign]` and spawn's `[defaults]` of the project's `.nhm/config.toml` (never ntm's `.ntm/config.toml`) and has no `projects_base`. `path`, `edit`, `reset` and `project init` port as they are. **Rulings:** which file `init`, `show`, `diff`, `get`, `validate` and `migrate` serve (ntm's, or nhm's tables in ntm's shapes), and what `set projects-base` does without a `projects_base` | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.10 (config and setup) |
| `conflicts [session] [--since D] [--limit N]` | — | ntm lists the paths that different sets of agents changed. It reads the same ledger as `changes`, for the cwd's project. `--since` defaults to 24h; a bad or non-positive value falls back to 24h silently. `--limit` defaults to 50 (0: all). Severity is `critical` with 3 or more agents or with edits within 10 minutes, else `warning`. Order: newest `last_at` first, then path. Text: `Conflicts Detected`, `The following files were modified by different agent sets:`, then `[WARNING]` or `[CRITICAL]` and the path, and one line per change (agents, session, age); `No conflicts detected.` with none. `--json`: a bare array of `Conflict` {path, changes[], severity, agents[], last_at}. **On herdr:** as `changes`: it needs nhm's file-change recorder. It is not `coordinator conflicts`, which is a subcommand of another verb. | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.16 (Agent Mail and files) |
| `context build [--agent cc\|cod\|gmi\|cursor\|windsurf\|aider] [--bead ID] [--task T] [--files G]`, `context show <pack-id>\|stats\|clear`, `context inject <session> [--files F] [--pane N\|--all] [--build\|--pack ID] [--max-bytes N] [--dry-run]` | — | `build` makes a context pack for the current session's project from bv triage, CM, CASS, `ms` skills and the named files (inside the project); `--agent` picks XML or Markdown. The pack goes to ntm's SQLite state. Text: `Context Pack: ID`, `Agent Type:`, `Token Count:`, then a `✓` or `✗` line per part; JSON `ContextPackFull{id, bead_id, agent_type, repo_rev, correlation_id, created_at, token_count, rendered_prompt, components}`. `show` prints a stored pack. `stats` and `clear` act on a cache inside the process, so `stats` always shows 0 (`{cache_size, cache_keys}`) and `clear` changes nothing. `inject` sends files (default `AGENTS.md`, `README.md`, `.claude/project_context.md`) or a pack to one pane (`--pane`, an index) or all. It checks the whole batch first; a pack is refused for a pane that is not an agent's, or when too large, and never cut. `--task` and `--bead` need `--build`; `--pack` excludes them. JSON: `ContextInjectResult{success, session, injected_files, total_bytes, truncated, panes_injected, deliveries[], mode, selected_files, warnings, …}`; on a failure it prints the JSON, then exits 1. It writes no history entry. Not `rotate context`. **On herdr:** the builder reuses nhm's CM, CASS and bv integrations (no `ms` one yet) and needs a pack table in nhm's store; `inject` sends through the send core (receipts) to a slot or pane id | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.14 (knowledge tools) |
| `controller <session> [--agent-type T] [--prompt FILE] [--no-prompt]` | — | ntm's controller launches one coordinating agent in the session. `--agent-type` (default cc) takes cc, cod, gmi, agy, cursor, windsurf (ws), aider, oc, omp or ollama; another word is `unknown agent type`. It reuses pane 1 of the first window when that pane is a plain shell, else it splits a new pane. It titles the pane `controller_<type>`, types the launch command, waits 2 s and types the prompt. The default prompt names ntm's robot verbs; `--prompt FILE` is a Go template with `{{.Session}}`, `{{.AgentList}}` and `{{.ProjectDir}}`; `--no-prompt` sends none. It makes no Agent Mail identity and has no ready gate. Text: `✓ Controller agent launched in session 'S'`, then the pane, agent type, prompt and the count of agents. JSON `ControllerResponse {generated_at, session, pane_id, pane_index, agent_type, prompt_used?, agent_count, agent_list?}`; a failure is `ErrorResponse`, exit 1. **On herdr:** `core/spawn` adds one agent of the kind to the workspace (in a bare shell pane, by `process_info`, or in the grid's next cell), and `core/send` sends the rendered prompt as its kickoff, with a receipt. `pane_index` is the slot's M. `--robot-controller-spawn` uses the same builder. **Rulings:** nhm's spawn always makes an Agent Mail identity, and ntm's controller makes none: does nhm's controller keep the identity? | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.11 (pane lifecycle) |
| `coordinator status\|digest\|conflicts\|assign\|run [session] [--send] [--once]`, `coordinator enable\|disable <feature> [--interval D]`, `coord` | — | The session is optional. `status`: `Coordinator Status: S`, `Agents: N total, M idle`, a table `Pane Type Status Context Idle For`, `Configuration`, `Runtime`; `--json` `{session, timestamp, agent_count, idle_count, agents{}, runtime{}, config{}}`, states GENERATING, WAITING, THINKING, ERROR, STALLED, UNKNOWN. `digest`: `DigestSummary` (agent counts, alerts, bv's `work_summary`, deadlocks); `--send` mails it to `human_agent`. `conflicts`: overlapping Agent Mail file reservations, `{session, conflicts[], count}`. `assign`: `ntm assign`'s engine and flags. `run` claims the session, pauses the monitor's coordinator and runs a cycle every 5 s until a signal (`--once`: one cycle): lease upkeep always; mail nudge, context rotation, caam failover, conflict notify and negotiate, and auto-assign only when on. Auto-assign sends the work as Agent Mail with `ack_required`, not to the pane. `--json` `{success, session, once, auto_assign, assignments[], monitor_yielded, error_code, error}`. `enable` and `disable` write `[coordinator]` keys: `auto-assign`, `digest` (`--interval`, at least 10 s), `conflict-notify`, `conflict-negotiate`, `mail-nudge`. Errors exit 1. **On herdr:** `core/snapshot` observes (idle or done is WAITING, working is GENERATING); `core/assignment` and `core/spawnassign` hold the ledger and claims; `core/wake` does the nudge; `nhm maintain` can host the loop; `assign` follows nhm-2p6.9. **Rulings:** where the loop runs; whether nhm takes Agent Mail file reservations (leases, `conflicts`, `conflict-*`); context % and caam failover have no herdr or nhm part | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.26 (coordinator) |
| `copy [session[:pane]] [--last N] [--pattern RE] [--code] [--headers] [--all\|--cc\|--cod\|--gmi\|--agy] [--output F] [--quiet]`, `cp`, `yank` | named in brenner's spec `specs/cockpit_runbook_v0.1.md` (`ntm copy "$THREAD_ID"`); no script calls it | ntm's copy reads pane output and puts it on the clipboard. The target is the pane after `:` (an index, `%N` or a pane id suffix), else the type filters, else the active pane (else the first). `--last` (`-l`, 1000, above 0) lines per pane; `--pattern` (`-p`) keeps the matching lines; `--code` keeps code blocks only (headers off unless `--headers`); each pane gets a header `═══ <title> (pane N) ═══`. Redaction applies (`--redact`, `--allow-secret`; block mode refuses). `--output` (`-o`) writes a file (it makes the folder) in place of the clipboard. Text: `✓ Copied N lines from M pane(s) to clipboard` (or `to file (PATH)`); warnings go to stderr. JSON `copyResult {source, panes[], lines, bytes, destination, pattern?, code?, output_path?, redaction?}`. Errors exit 1 as plain text, also with `--json`: `--last must be positive`, `no matching panes found`, `no content captured (check filters)`, `invalid pattern regex`, `clipboard backend unavailable`. **On herdr:** `pane.read` recent N (as `nhm logs`); the active pane is herdr's focused pane; the type filters are kinds from the snapshot; code blocks from `core/panediff`. A herdr pane id holds a colon (`w1:p3`), so the pane part after `:` needs a definition (slot, name or pane id suffix). nhm has no clipboard code: port ntm's backends (pbcopy, wl-copy, xclip, xsel, WSL, the tmux buffer). nhm's redaction has no modes yet | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.8 (pane reads) |
| `create <session-name> [--panes N] [--label L]` | — | ntm's create makes a tmux session of N plain shell panes in the project folder under the projects base. `--panes` (`-p`): 0 or less takes the config default, 10. `--label` (`-l`) makes the name `PROJECT--L`; a project name with `--` is refused. A missing folder asks `Create it?` (JSON makes it silently; `Aborted.` exits 0). ntm runs its pre-create hooks (a failure aborts) and post-create hooks, and writes its audit log. A session that exists prints `Session 'S' already exists` and attaches (JSON `created:false, already_existed:true`). Text: `Creating session 'S' with N pane(s)...`, `Created session 'S' with N pane(s)`, then it attaches. JSON `CreateResponse {generated_at, session, created, already_existed?, working_directory?, pane_count, panes[]}`. Errors exit 1. **On herdr:** `workspace.create` and `pane.split` N−1 times (nhm's tabs and grid place them); a session that exists gets the focus, as attach. `nhm new` is the nearest (one workspace, cwd = the project, an existing label refused). herdr has no history limit; nhm has no audit log, and nhm's hooks are herdr event hooks. **Rulings:** the project folder (ntm's projects base, or nhm's `--project` and cwd); a session that exists (attach, as ntm, or refuse, as `nhm new`); ntm's create hooks and audit log (port or waive) | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.11 (pane lifecycle) |

JSON: `HistoryListJSON{entries[], total_count, showing, has_more, pagination, _agent_hints}`, each entry `{id, ts, session, targets, prompt, source, template, success, error, duration_ms}`. `show` takes an index (1 is newest) or a unique ID prefix. `history replay` is `replay`. `stats`: `{total_entries, success_count, failure_count, unique_sessions, file_size_bytes}` and the file's location. `clear` asks `[y/N]` unless `-f`; `--before` prunes by age. `prune --keep` 10000. Writers: send (an entry per prompt group, failures too), robot send (the caller's message), the palette and replay; a session in privacy mode writes nothing, and prompts are stored redacted (`[redaction]`, default `warn`). **On herdr:** no herdr call. nhm's send core writes nhm's history file for every send, but not as ntm: another path, raw prompts, nhm's kind names (`claude`) for ntm's types (`cc`), slots or pane ids for pane keys, one entry per send, no encryption or privacy mode | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.19 (history and analytics) |

Bare `hooks` prints help, exit 0. install writes `pre-commit` (the default) or `post-checkout` (others: "not yet implemented") and moves a foreign hook to `<hook>.backup`. The pre-commit script (marker `NTM_MANAGED_HOOK`) runs `br sync --flush-only` when `.beads` exists, `ntm guards check --staged`, `ntm hooks run pre-commit`, then the backup. uninstall restores the backup (`--restore`, default true). status: `Git Hooks Status`, then per hook type `installed (ntm)`, `installed (other)` or `not installed`, with `(backup exists)`. run pre-commit: UBS on the staged files (`--timeout` 120 s, 0: none). guard install and uninstall call Agent Mail's `install_precommit_guard` and `uninstall_precommit_guard` for the cwd. JSON: install {success, hook_type, path}, uninstall {success, hook_type, restored}, status {repo_root, hooks_dir, hooks[]}, run {success, passed, …}. `Hook already exists. Use --force to overwrite.` exits 0 in text mode and fails in JSON mode. **On herdr:** no herdr call. New: a hook manager whose script runs the nhm binary; ubs and br as tools. nhm's herdr event hooks are another thing. **Rulings:** the marker strings, as for `guards`. | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.17 (git and worktrees) |

Text: `✓ Initialized NTM project in <dir>/.ntm`, an Agent Mail line, `ℹ Created N directories/files`, `✓ Installed git hooks: pre-commit, post-checkout` or a warning (`not a git repository, skipping hooks`). `--json` `{success, project_path, ntm_dir, created_dirs, created_files, agent_mail, hooks_installed, non_interactive, force, no_hooks, agent_mail_warning, hooks_warning}`. `--non-interactive` shows only in the JSON. `init zsh\|bash\|fish`, with no such directory, runs `shell` (the old name: keep it for old rc files). **On herdr:** no herdr part. Agent Mail through `internal/integrations/agentmail`; nhm already reads the project's `.nhm/config.toml`, its own in the place of ntm's `.ntm/config.toml`. The hook calls `guards check` (nhm-2p6.16) and `hooks run` (nhm-2p6.17); nhm's `internal/integrations/guards` is the agent-hook guard, not ntm's reservation guard. **Rulings:** the binary that the hook calls (`ntm` or `nhm`), as for `shell` | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.10 (config and setup) |
| `internal-monitor <session>` (hidden) | — | Every `ntm spawn` and robot spawn starts it as a detached process (`NTM_DISABLE_INTERNAL_MONITOR` turns it off), and `spawn` prints `Session monitor started (pid: N)` or `(auto-restart enabled, pid: N)`. `swarm --auto-rotate-accounts` starts it in account-rotation mode. It takes one session and no flags, and refuses remote tmux. It holds a lease per session and runs until the session ends: health checks, crash detection and auto-restart (with `[resilience] auto_restart`); the session timeline every 10 s; context pressure to the attention feed; the `cm serve` and `am serve-http` daemons; a pane archive every 30 s; the coordinator host; a `git status` sampler every 15 s for `changes`, `conflicts` and `--robot-status`; at the end a `session_ended` webhook and a summary in `.ntm/summaries/`. It writes only to its log. **On herdr:** herdr already sees agent state and pane exit. `nhm maintain` (one per herdr server) is the nearest host; auto-restart is `core/lifecycle` recycle; the timeline goes to `internal/store`. Users need its effects, not its name. **Rulings:** whether to waive the name; which effects nhm carries (nhm has no auto-restart policy, daemon supervision, archiver, file sampler, end-of-session summary, webhook or caam rotation) | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.26 (coordinator) |
| `interrupt <session> [--tag T]` | ru (`ru:13596`, `ru:13893`: `nhm interrupt S --json`, the exit code); ACFS rehearsal E17 (`--name`) | ntm sends each agent pane its interrupt key, whatever the agent's state: Ctrl+C, or Escape for omp. It skips antigravity and the user pane. `--tag` (repeatable or a comma list; one match is enough, in any case) keeps the panes with a matching tag. One session argument (`accepts 1 arg(s), received 0`). Text: `Sent Ctrl+C to N agent pane(s)`, or `Sent interrupt to N agent pane(s) (Ctrl+C: a, Escape: b)`; a failed pane is ntm's error after that line (`interrupt partially failed (a interrupted, f failed): pane I: why; …`), exit 1. JSON: `InterruptResponse{generated_at, success, session, interrupted, failed, skipped, targeted_panes[], panes[]{pane, index, pane_id, agent_type, status, error}, error, error_code}`, with `PARTIAL_INTERRUPT` or `INTERRUPT_FAILED`; `success:false` exits 1; an error is ntm's `{"success":false,"error":…}` on one line, exit 1. A pane's tags end its herdr pane label (`IcyKnoll opus[frontend,api]`, set with `herdr pane rename`), as ntm reads them from the end of the tmux pane title. `pane` is the slot's M, or the slot `N.M` when the agents span tabs. nhm's extras: the selectors `--name`, `--pane` (the slot), `--type` and `--project` (one that matches no agent is an error), `--include-self`, `--op-id` (a repeated id sends nothing: `operation ID was claimed before (STATUS); nothing sent`, `_nhm.existing`), `--socket`; `_nhm` holds the operation, its status and each agent's result. **Differences:** nhm sends no key to a kind whose key it has not checked (opencode and grok, where ntm sends Ctrl+C): that pane fails with the reason (asked of the operator, nhm-2p6.5.1). | `kill [session] [-f\|--force] [--pane SEL] [--tag T] [-p\|--project P] [--summarize] [--no-hooks]` | — | ntm asks for a yes (a TUI confirm on a TTY, else y/N on stdin). Then it stops the session monitor, kills the session, ends the panes' left-over processes (SIGTERM, then SIGKILL), and releases every Agent Mail file reservation of the session's agents. `-f` skips the question; under `--json` ntm never asks. `--pane SEL` removes those panes and keeps the session; it refuses `--project`, `--tag` and `--summarize`. `--tag` kills only the panes with a matching tag and keeps the session. `--project P` kills every session of a base project after one confirm; then no session is given. `--summarize` writes a session summary (pane captures and git diff) first. Text: `Killed session 'S'`, `Killed N pane(s) matching tags`, `No panes found matching tags.` or `Aborted.` (exit 0); with `--pane`, `Removed pane P (target, type)` and `N pane(s) remain in session 'S'`. JSON: `KillResponse{generated_at, session, killed, message, summary}`; with `--pane`, ntm's robot kill-pane output. **Gaps:** without `--yes` or `--force`, nhm refuses (`give --yes`, exit 2), also under `--json`. It never asks, so a `y` on stdin does not kill. `--pane`, `--tag`, `--project`, `-p`, `--summarize` and `--no-hooks` are unknown flags. nhm ends no processes and releases no reservations (it retires each identity). Text: a `retired <Name>` line per agent, then `closed workspace <id>`; JSON: nhm's envelope with `KillResult`. **On herdr:** core/lifecycle's `KillWith` and `workspace.close`; a confirm popup or y/N on stdin in place of ntm's TUI; `--pane` is retire by slot or pane id; `--tag` needs pane tokens; `--project` loops over the project's workspaces; `--summarize` needs a summary engine (pane reads and git diff) | `internal/cli/retire.go`, `internal/core/lifecycle` | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.5 (send family) |

[y/N]:` on stdin. Stray arguments are ignored. | `models list\|pull <model>\|remove <model>\|recommend`, `models ls`, `models rm\|delete` | — | Manages Ollama models on `--host` (else `NTM_OLLAMA_HOST`, else `OLLAMA_HOST`, else `http://localhost:11434`). `list` (`/api/tags` and `df -k .`): a `NAME SIZE MODIFIED FAMILY PARAMETERS` table, `Host:`, `Model count:`, `Total size:`, `Disk free:`, a warning below 5 GB free and `Cleanup hint: oldest model(s): …`; `--json` `{host, model_count, total_size_bytes, disk_free_bytes, disk_low, cleanup_suggestions, models[]}`. `pull <model> [--timeout 30m]` streams `/api/pull` progress. `remove <model>` calls `/api/delete` after a y/N prompt (`-y/--yes` or `--json` skips it; a no is `Error: aborted`, exit 1). It takes no arguments. On first use it binds F12 (or `--bind-key`) with `tmux bind-key` and adds the binding to `~/.tmux.conf`; a failure warns `⚠ Could not auto-setup F12 binding: …`. `--attention-cursor` goes to the dashboard. | `pipeline run <file> -s S [--var k=v] [--var-file F] [--dry-run] [-b] [--start-from STEP] [--from-state RUN]`, `pipeline lint\|status\|list\|cancel\|resume [--background] [--iteration N] [--step-id ID]\|cleanup\|exec` | — | ntm's step engine for a YAML or TOML file (`schema_version: "2.0"`, `name`, `vars`, `settings`, `steps[]`). `run` needs `-s` and a live session; `--var-file` is a JSON object; `-b` starts `__pipeline-worker`; `--from-state` needs `--start-from`. Steps run as a graph (`depends_on`, `after`; 4 at a time): agent steps (a pane by index, agent type or route; paste, Enter, a submit check, 3 stable idle polls; the answer is the screen delta), shell commands, templates, branches, loops, foreach, `bead_query` (br), Agent Mail steps, and notices by desktop, webhook or mail. Runs are `run-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS-xxxx` in `.ntm/pipelines/`. Text: progress lines, then `Pipeline completed successfully!` or a failure line. `--json` on run, status, list, cancel: `PipelineRunOutput`, `PipelineStatusOutput`, `PipelineListOutput`, `PipelineCancelOutput`. `lint <file>` checks without tmux (`PARSE_FAILED`, `VALIDATION_FAILED`). `resume <run>` (`--mode continue\|restart-failed\|force-iter`, `--keep-state`, `--max-resume-age`, `--on-roster-change`), `cleanup --older D [--dry-run]`, legacy `exec <session> --stage "type[:model]: prompt"`. Every failure exits 1. **On herdr:** `core/send` delivers and never sends an unknown outcome again; `core/wait` waits for idle; a herdr pane read gives the answer; a pane is a slot `N.M` or a pane id. The parser, state, run control and worker are plain Go. Route `least-loaded` needs context %, which herdr does not give: fall back to herdr's state | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.25 (pipeline and workflows) |
| `__pipeline-worker -- <project-dir> <run-id>` (hidden) | — | ntm's detached worker for `pipeline run --background` and `pipeline resume --background`. No user types it: the launcher starts ntm again with the root `--config` and `--ssh`, then `--` and exactly two arguments. The worker reads `.ntm/pipelines/background/<run>.json` (version, token, expiry, executor config, vars, resume flag; at most 8 MiB), writes `<run>.claimed`, waits for the parent's token on stdin, writes `<run>.ready` and runs the executor under run control. Its stdout and stderr go to `<run>.log`. The parent waits up to 15 s for it. It prints nothing to the user (errors and usage are silenced). **On herdr:** no herdr part. nhm needs it only if it keeps ntm's detached-worker design; a port may use an internal entry point of its own. **Rulings:** whether to waive the name while `pipeline --background` keeps ntm's behaviour | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.25 (pipeline and workflows) |

Text only: `Agent Plugins:` with `NAME ALIAS DESCRIPTION`, `Command Plugins:` with `NAME USAGE DESCRIPTION`, or `No plugins installed.`; `--json` is ignored. A bad plugin directory is `Error: load agent plugins from …`, exit 1. Bare `plugins` prints the help, exit 0. ntm also runs each command plugin as a top-level verb, with its arguments as given and `NTM_CONFIG_PATH`, `NTM_VERSION`, `NTM_JSON=1` under `--json` and `NTM_SESSION` (the current tmux session). **On herdr:** ntm's agent plugins are nhm's config kinds (`[kinds.*]`, `internal/integrations/kinds`), which have no description or alias today. Errors exit 1. It also looks for `dcg`. Text: `Preflight Check`, `Preview Hash:`, `Size: N bytes, ~T tokens`, `DCG Available:`, a `✗`, `⚠` or `ℹ` `[ID] message` line per finding, then `✓ Preflight passed` or `✗ Preflight blocked`; `--preview` prints the first 500 bytes. The imbalance score is stddev/mean. By default it skips user panes and an unknown pane at index 0; `--all` (`-a`) takes them too. Some types refuse an automated relaunch. `--dry-run` lists the panes; else it asks first (`--force`, `-f`, skips). Per pane: tmux respawn-pane, the pane's recorded launch command, a 15 s ready gate.
