An agent pane is idle when the ledger shows no active task, it is not the user's or unknown, and its last task ended more than `--idle-threshold` (2m) ago; ntm reads no screen. Suggestions come from completed beads (`Review the changes for bead X: "T"`) and from the cwd's last `--commits` (5) commits (`Review commit <8>: "S"`). `--filter` works as for `rebalance` (no agy). Text: `📋 Review Queue for 'S'`, `Idle Agents: N` and the agents, `Pending Reviews: N`, `🔄 Suggested Assignments:` with rows `N. <agent> <- <prompt> (<source>)`. `--send` asks `Send these prompts? (y/n)`, types each prompt into its pane, then prints `Sent N review prompts (M skipped)`; with JSON it is refused. JSON `ReviewQueueResponse` {generated_at, session, idle_agents[], suggestions[] {agent, pane, agent_type, prompt, source, source_ref}, sent, skipped}; errors {success, session, error}. **On herdr:** the agents are the workspace's bindings; idleness stays ledger-only, as ntm's; `--send` goes through nhm's send core (rule 5), which adds receipts. | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.9 (work distribution) |
| `rollback <session> [checkpoint-id] [--last] [--dry-run] [--no-stash] [--no-git] [-f]` | — | ntm puts the session's work tree back to a checkpoint (an id, prefix, `last`, `~1` or `~N`; an id or `--last` is required). It interrupts the session's agents (Ctrl+C), stashes a dirty tree (`git stash push -m ntm-rollback-<ts>`, unless `--no-stash`), checks out the checkpoint's commit (detached) and applies its saved patch; a failed checkout pops the stash. It restores no panes. Without `-f` it asks; `--json` does not ask. Dry run: `Rollback Preview` and the planned actions; JSON `{dry_run, checkpoint, session, created_at, description, pane_count, actions[]}` (`stash_current_changes`, `apply_git_patch`, `checkout_commit_<8>`). Done: `✓ Rollback complete`; JSON `{success, checkpoint, session, stash_name, rolled_back, git_commit}`. Errors exit 1. **On herdr:** interrupt through nhm's interrupt core; the work tree is the workspace's project (bindings), where ntm asks tmux for the pane's path; git by exec. It needs nhm's checkpoint store first (see `checkpoint`) | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.23 (save and restore) |
| `rotate [session] (--pane P\|--all-limited) [--account A] [--preserve-context\|--force] [--dry-run] [--timeout D]`, `rotate context history [--last N] [--failed]\|pending\|confirm <agent-id> [--action A] [--minutes N] [--retry]\|stats\|clear`, `rotate lock <provider> <account>\|unlock <provider>\|status` | — | `rotate` restarts one pane's agent (`--pane`: N, W.P or %id) or every rate-limited one (`--all-limited`, found by typing `/usage` into each agent pane) on another account. The default is manual: it exits the agent, waits for a shell prompt, asks the user to switch the account in the browser and press Enter, then starts the agent again. `--account` names the account (else `[rotation]` suggests one); `--preserve-context` runs Claude's `/login` and a prompt to continue (Claude only); `--force` kills and starts again; `--timeout` 300 s. The output is boxed text, `✓ Rotation complete!`; `--json` changes nothing. `rotate context` works on the context rotations that ntm's coordinator queues: `history`, `pending`, `confirm` (`-a rotate\|compact\|ignore\|postpone`; a failed confirm exits 1), `stats`, `clear`; each has a JSON type. `lock`, `unlock` and `status` pin accounts in `<data-dir>/.ntm/account_pins.json` (`--data-dir`, default the cwd); `status --json`: `{pins, caam_safe_restore, global_rotation_safe, safe_restore_required, caam_capability_error}`. The verb does not switch through caam; the coordinator's failover does. Errors exit 1. **On herdr:** the restart is nhm's recycle with a pause for the browser switch; `--force` is recycle with a kill; `--all-limited` uses nhm's quota integration, not `/usage` typing. Pins are file I/O. `rotate context` needs the coordinator's rotator (nhm-2p6.26) | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.18 (accounts, quota and context) |
| `safety status\|blocked [--hours N]\|check <command…>\|simulate [command] [--step S]\|install [-f]\|uninstall`, `safety claude-hook` (hidden) | — | ntm checks a shell command against its policy (see `policy`). `check` joins its arguments; for a match that is not allow, `dcg` can turn approve into block. JSON: `CheckResponse{generated_at, command, action, pattern, reason, policy, dcg}`, action `allow`, `block` or `approve`; block and approve exit 1. `claude-hook` reads Claude Code's PreToolUse JSON on stdin, runs the same check and exits 2 to refuse; it logs a refusal to `~/.ntm/logs/blocked.jsonl` and to ntm's SQLite state (`blocked` and `metrics` read them). `blocked` lists the last `--hours` (24). `simulate` runs commands or `--step`s through the policy. `install` writes `git` and `rm` wrappers in `~/.ntm/bin` (they run `ntm safety check … --json --hook`), the hook script under `~/.claude/hooks/PreToolUse/` and its entry in `~/.claude/settings.json`; `uninstall` removes them. `status --json` reports the policy path, the rule counts and the wrapper and hook states. ntm's spawn also gives each Claude agent the policy hook through `--settings` (`[safety] claude_policy_hook`, on by default). **On herdr:** a hand port of the policy check (pure Go); the hook and wrappers call `nhm safety`; the caller is the herdr pane (`HERDR_PANE_ID`, the bindings), not `TMUX_PANE`; the spawn core adds the hook to Claude's `--settings` beside nhm's guard rules. Test `install` in a container: it changes host files. **Rulings:** the policy file, as for `policy` | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.20 (safety and policy) |
| `save [session-name] [--output DIR] [--lines N] [--all\|--cc\|--cod\|--gmi\|--agy]`, `dump`, `export` | — | ntm's save writes each pane's output to a text file. `--output` (`-o`, `./outputs`) is the folder; `--lines` (`-l`, 2000) per pane; with no type flag it takes every pane, user shells too. The file is `<session>_<title or pane_N>_<YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS>.txt` (local time), mode 0644, redacted, with a header (session, pane title and index, command, capture time, line count). Text: one `  ✓ <path>` per file, then `✓ Saved N file(s) to DIR`. No JSON: `--json` prints the same text. Errors exit 1: `no matching panes found`, `no content captured`, `no files saved`, an unknown session. **On herdr:** `pane.read` recent N for each pane of the workspace, agents and plain shells; the title is herdr's pane label; the command is `pane.process_info`'s foreground process or the agent kind. `--robot-save` is another feature. **Rulings:** the time in the file name: local, as ntm (rule 4), or UTC (rule 8)? | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.8 (pane reads) |
| `scale <session> [--cc N] [--cod N] [--gmi N] [--agy N] [--dry-run] [--force]` | — | ntm's scale sets the number of agents of each type in a session. Only the type flags given count; none is `no target counts specified (use --cc, --cod, --gmi, or --agy)`, and a negative count is refused. It plans spawns and kills (the highest index first); no change prints `Already at target counts. No changes needed.`. `--dry-run` prints the plan. In text mode a scale-down asks first (`--force`, `-f`, skips); in JSON mode it does not ask. Up runs ntm's add per type (a skipped account seat is an error); down is a plain tmux kill-pane; then a tiled layout. Text: the plan, `  Spawned N cc agent(s)`, `  Terminated <title>`, `Scaling complete. Current state: cc=N, cod=N, gmi=N, agy=N`; `Errors encountered:` exits 1. JSON `ScaleResponse {generated_at, session, before, after, actions[]{type, agent_type, count, agents[]}, success, dry_run?, errors?, error_code?}`; a failure is the JSON error envelope, exit 1. **On herdr:** up is `core/spawn` add (admission, identity, binding, kickoff); down is `core/lifecycle` retire, the highest slot first; the layout is `core/arrange` or herdr's own. nhm's kinds are more than ntm's four flags. **Rulings:** ntm kills a working agent and keeps nothing; nhm's retire keeps the transcript and refuses a working or blocked agent: what does a scale-down do then? | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.11 (pane lifecycle) |
| `scan [path] [--only L] [--exclude L] [--ci] [--fail-on-warning] [-v] [--timeout S] [--staged] [--diff] [-w\|--watch] [--notify] [--create-beads] [--update-beads] [--min-severity S] [--dry-run] [--analyze-impact] [--hotspots] [--priority-report]` | — | Runs `ubs --format=json` on a path (default `.`, made absolute) with `--only=`, `--exclude-langs=`, `--ci`, `--fail-on-warning`, `-v`, `--staged` and `--diff` (`--timeout 120` s). On request: `--create-beads` files br beads for findings at or above `--min-severity warning` (no duplicates: a signature in the description; `--dry-run` previews), `--update-beads` closes fixed ones, `--notify` mails agents (only with critical or warning findings), and `--analyze-impact`, `--hotspots`, `--priority-report` add analysis (bv's graph when installed). `-w` scans again on file changes (500 ms debounce), clears the screen per run, drops the three analysis flags and never ends. Text: `Scanning <path>...`, `Scan Results`, the counts, `Language Breakdown:`, a verdict, then the bead and analysis blocks. `--json` `{success, scan, beads_created, beads_updated, impact_analysis, hotspots, priority_report}`; a failure adds `error` and `error_code` (`SCAN_FAILED`, `SCAN_SETUP_FAILED`, or `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` with `available:false`). Exit 1 on critical findings (`scan found critical issues`), on warnings with `--fail-on-warning`, on a bead or notify failure, or without ubs (`✗ UBS not installed`, `Error: ubs is not installed`). ubs exit 2 or a report with `status` error or partial is an error, never an empty scan; ubs exit 3 is "nothing scanned". `scan` never writes `bugs`'s cache. **On herdr:** no tmux part. ubs through `bugs`'s new `internal/integrations/ubs`; br through `internal/integrations/bv` (`RunBd`); `-w` through `internal/core/watcher` | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.15 (ops tools) |
| `scrub [--path P]… [--since D] [--format text\|json]` | — | ntm scans its own files for secrets and reports them; it changes nothing. The roots are each `--path`, else the config file's directory and `~/.ntm`, those that exist. It walks them and skips `.git`, symlinks and binary files (so its SQLite state); `--since` keeps the files changed in that time. Text: `Scrub roots:` and the findings. JSON: `scrubResult{roots, files_scanned, findings[]{path, category, start, end, line, column, preview}, warnings}`; a preview is 40 bytes of context, redacted again. `--json` wins over `--format`; another format word is an error. Findings exit 0; errors exit 1. The scan is ntm's redaction with `[redaction]` from the config. **On herdr:** no herdr call. The default roots become nhm's own: its config directory, its data directory (history) and its state directory (SQLite, skipped as binary). It needs nhm's redaction at ntm's level (see `redact`) | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.20 (safety and policy) |
| `search <query> [-s\|--session S] [-a\|--agent A] [--since D] [-n\|--limit N] [--offset N]` | — | `cass search` with other defaults (`--limit 20`). `-s/--session` is an ntm session name, which ntm maps to its project directory (`projects_base/<session>`), or an absolute path; the search keeps to that workspace. It takes one argument: quote a phrase. Output and errors as `cass search`: the text table, or `SearchResponse` with `--json`; without cass, exit 2 (`NOT_IMPLEMENTED` in JSON). `audit search`, `history search` and `work search` are other verbs' subcommands. **On herdr:** no herdr part; it rides on the `cass search` port in `internal/integrations/cass`. nhm has no `projects_base`: a session maps to the project of most of its bound agents, the rule of the mail inbox | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.14 (knowledge tools) |
| `send <session> [prompt...] [--cc[=V]] [--cod[=V]] [--gmi[=V]] [--agy[=V]] [--grok[=V]] [--oc[=V]] [--omp[=V]] [--include-user] [-p\|--pane N] [-s\|--skip-first] [--tag T] [--project P] [-f\|--file F] [--prefix T] [--suffix T] [-c\|--context F] [--base-prompt T] [--base-prompt-file F] [--cass-check-days N] [--cass-similarity X] [--loop-mode] [--smart] [--route R] [--distribute] [--dist-strategy S] [--dist-limit N] [--dist-auto] [--batch F] [--delay D] [--confirm-each] [--stop-on-error] [--broadcast] [--agent N] [--priority-order] [--randomize] [--seed N] [--pace-dispatch] [--clear-input] [--codex-goal] [--force-non-interactive] [--no-hooks] [--panes SEL] [--cass-check]` | — | ntm types the prompt (the arguments, else stdin from a pipe) into the agent panes; with no selector, into every agent pane but the user's. `--cc=V` and the other type flags filter by model or persona. `--project P` sends to every session of a base project. `--smart` or `--route R` picks the pane; `--distribute` sends bv's triage work to idle agents after a confirm; `--batch F` sends a file of prompts; `--codex-goal` drives Codex's `/goal` flow on one `--pane`. `--prefix`, `--suffix`, `--context F` (`path:10-50`) and the base prompt (its flags, else `[send]`) add text. `--clear-input` sends Escape and C-u first. The CASS duplicate check asks y/N on a TTY, warns without one, and fails under `--json` unless `--force-non-interactive`. Text: `Sent to N pane(s)`; `No matching panes found` exits 1; a dry run lists `Would send N prompt(s):`. JSON, on one line: `SendResult{success, session, prompt_preview, targets[], delivered, failed, routed_to, dispatch_pacing, cass_injection, blocked, warnings, error_code, error, …}`; `SendDryRunResult` with `--dry-run`. **Gaps:** of the flags above, nhm takes only the type flags but `--gmi` (as booleans: `--cc=opus` exits 2), `--pane` and `--file` (not `-p` or `-f`), and `--project` (a scope filter); the rest are unknown flags. Without a selector nhm sends to the bound agents but the caller. It reads stdin only with `--msg-file -` or `--file -`. Its duplicate check only warns. It has none of ntm's dcg check of a prompt to a non-Claude agent, checkpoint before a broadcast, or prompt history. The text is a line per target and `operation <id>`; no match exits 2; the JSON is nhm's envelope. **On herdr:** the send core; tags in pane tokens; variants from the binding; routing on herdr's states; distribute on spawnassign and bv; batch as a loop; `--clear-input` and `--codex-goal` as herdr keys | `internal/cli/send.go`, `internal/core/send` | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.5 (send family) |
| `session-templates list\|show <name>`, `templates`, `st` | — | Session templates: 11 builtins (refactor, feature, bug-hunt, documentation, migration, …), the project's `.ntm/templates/` (from the cwd) and `~/.config/ntm/templates/`, `*.yaml` or `*.yml`, with inheritance to a maximum depth. `list`: `Available Session Templates` by source, `name  description` and `[N agents, tags: …]`, then `Total: N template(s)`; `--json` `SessionTemplatesListResult {templates[{name, description, source, tags, agent_count}], total}`. `show <name>` checks the template and prints its description, source, tags, total agents, one line per type `Claude (cc) x N [model: …]` (cc, cod, gmi, agy, cursor, windsurf, aider, ollama), the initial prompt (cut), CASS, Beads and Options; `--json` `SessionTemplateShowResult {name, description, source, tags, agents, prompts, cass, beads, options}`. Errors carry suggestions, exit 1. The bare verb prints the help, exit 0. Nothing else in ntm reads session templates (`spawn --template` reads workflow templates). **On herdr:** a catalog, no herdr part. nhm's `internal/core/templates` holds prompt templates (ntm's loader, ported, but read from `.nhm/templates` and `~/.config/nhm/templates`), not the session-template schema. The alias `templates` sits beside ntm's `template` verb (prompt templates), which nhm does not answer yet (the `template` row) | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.7 (spawn catalogs) |
| `sessions save [session] [--name N] [--overwrite]`, `sessions list [--archived]\|show <name>\|restore <saved>\|resume <saved>\|delete <name> [-f]\|archive <name>\|unarchive <name>`, `sessions restore-archived` | — | `save` captures a tmux session to `~/.ntm/sessions/<name>.json`: windows, panes, layout, launch specs, git branch, remote and commit, and each pane's provider session id, found in the agents' own session stores. With no argument it takes the current session, else it asks. `list`, `show`, `delete`, `archive` and `unarchive` (alias `restore-archived`; the `archived/` folder) manage the files. `restore <saved>` builds the topology again with fresh agents: `--launch` starts them, `--prompt` needs `--launch`; also `--name`, `-f`, `--attach`, `--skip-git-check`, `--theme`. `resume <saved>` builds it and resumes each agent's own session, through casr when present, else the agent's `--resume <id>` (`--native` forces that). Text names the file, or the panes and agents restored or resumed; `--json` gives each subcommand's state or list. Saves, restores and deletes go to the audit log; `--robot-save` writes the same files. Errors exit 1. Unlike `checkpoint restore`: no scrollback and no git patch. **On herdr:** save is `layout.export` per tab, the bindings (kind, model, native session id, resume argv) and git; herdr's `pane.report_agent_session` keeps a `resume_argv` per pane, and nhm's kind registry knows the resume flags. restore and resume are `workspace.create` and `layout.apply`, then spawn or `admin recover resume` per binding (casr optional). herdr restores its own session after a server restart; `nhm admin restore` then checks the bindings again | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.23 (save and restore) |
| `setup [-w\|--wrappers] [--hooks] [-f\|--force]`, `setup status`, `project-init` | — | Sets up the cwd for ntm. With an existing `.ntm/` and no `--force`: `✓ NTM already initialized at …`, exit 0. Else it makes `.ntm/{logs,pids,cache,bin}`, `.ntm/config.yaml` (which nothing in ntm reads), `.ntm/policy.yaml` and `AGENTS.md`, adds `.ntm/` to an existing `.gitignore`, and runs `br init --json` when br is on `PATH`. `--wrappers` (PATH wrappers for git and rm) and `--hooks` (Claude Code PreToolUse hooks) run `safety`'s install. | `shell zsh\|bash\|fish [--no-agent-aliases]` | — | Prints a shell script on stdout: the agent aliases `cc`, `cod` and `gmi` from the config's agent commands (left out with `--no-agent-aliases`); 16 verb aliases (`cnt` create, `sat` spawn, `qps` quick, `ant` add, `bp` send, `int` interrupt, `rnt` attach, `lnt` list, `snt` status, `vnt` view, `znt` zoom, `cpnt` copy, `svnt` save, `ncp` palette, `knt` kill, `dnt` deps; fish uses `abbr`); the completion load (`source <(ntm completion zsh)`, the same for bash; fish pipes `ntm completion fish` into `source`); a session completer for the aliases that parses `ntm list`; F6 runs `ntm palette` (zsh in tmux: a `tmux popup` of 80%). | `spawn <session> [--cc\|--cod\|--gmi\|--agy\|--grok\|--cursor\|--windsurf\|--aider\|--oc\|--omp N[:model[:effort]]]… [--local\|--ollama N] [--local-model M] [--local-host H] [--local-fallback] [--local-fallback-provider P] [--prompt T] [--with-agent-name] [--cass-context Q] [--cass-context-days N] [--cass-context-limit N] [--no-recovery] [--stagger[=D]] [--stagger-mode M] [--stagger-delay D] [-l\|--label L] [--create-dir] [--pane-env K=V] [--safety] [--verify-boot] [--auto-restart] [--privacy] [--allow-persist] [--no-hooks] [--no-cass-context]` | — | ntm makes the session in the folder `projects_base/<session>`; `--label L` makes `<session>--L` in the same folder, and a name with `--` is refused. Repeated specs add up, and a spec takes `N:model:effort` or `N:model@effort`. With no agent flag, `[project_defaults]` gives the counts. ntm starts every pane at once; in a session that exists, it splits only the missing panes. A prompt goes only with `--prompt` or a `[prompts]` default, after the CASS context and the recovery prompt. The stagger paces the prompts, not the starts (mode `none`, `--stagger-delay` 30s, `[spawn]` defaults). ntm exits 0 once the prompts are out (1 on an error), and leaves what it made after a failure. Text: step lines and, on a TTY, a "What's next?" footer. JSON: `SpawnResponse{generated_at, session, created, working_directory, panes[], agent_counts, stagger, agent_mail, recovery, profile_set, seat_skips}`; a failure writes `agentLifecycleFailureResponse`. **Gaps:** of the flags above, nhm takes only `--cc`, `--cod`, `--agy`, `--grok`, `--oc`, `--omp`, `--prompt`, `--label`, `--safety`, `--create-dir`, `--stagger`, `--stagger-mode`, `--stagger-delay`, `--pane-env`, `--verify-boot`, `--no-hooks`, `--cass-context`, `--cass-context-days`, `--cass-context-limit`, `--no-cass-context`, `--with-agent-name`, `--privacy` and `--allow-persist`; the rest are unknown flags. `--pane-env`, `--verify-boot` and `--no-hooks` are ntm's (nhm-2p6.4.7): the pane env fills `{project}`, `{pane}` (the agent's number among its type) and `{role}` (its short type), and nhm's own names (`NHM_LAUNCH_ID`, `NHM_AGENT`, `AGENT_MAIL_AGENT`) win over it; `--verify-boot` waits 30 s (or `--ready-timeout`) even with no prompt and fails with ntm's words (`TIMEOUT` under `--json`); nhm runs no command hooks, so `--no-hooks` changes nothing. `--privacy` and `--allow-persist` are ntm's (nhm-2p6.4.16, the operator's ruling of 2026-10-24): spawn records them for the session before any agent starts, and a record that fails aborts the spawn with ntm's `recording privacy settings for session S: …`; nhm keeps them in the instance's store, keyed by the herdr workspace id (ntm: an option on the tmux session; herdr's metadata tokens do not outlive a herdr restart), and a new workspace never keeps an old record of its id; nhm's `[privacy]` keys are ntm's (`enabled`, `disable_prompt_history`, `disable_event_logs`, `disable_checkpoints`, `disable_scrollback_capture`, `require_explicit_persist`), with ntm's defaults, and a `[privacy]` that does not load refuses the command; a send that reaches an agent of a private session writes no prompt history line, unless `--allow-persist`. nhm's own records of a private session's data follow ntm's operations: spawn's stored request keeps the prompts as their digests (the operation's fingerprint hash still covers them, so a rerun of the id is a replay); the enrichment injections keep no query and no labels, only the items' keys; the agent's conversation is not copied by recycle, retire or kill (ntm makes no archive), and so is not indexed in CASS; receipts, bindings and send's stored request hold no prompt text. `--allow-persist` lifts each. `--auto-restart` is nhm-2p6.4.15 (the operator's ruling of 2026-10-24), an unknown flag until then. The specs are ntm's (nhm-2p6.4.1), and `-l` and `--safety` are ntm's (nhm-2p6.4.5): `--safety` refuses a session that exists, also one that appears while the spawn runs, with ntm's `session 'S' already exists (--safety mode prevents reuse; use 'nhm kill S' first)`, and `add` has no `--safety`. `--create-dir` is ntm's too (nhm-2p6.4.5), on `--project`'s folder (the cwd's work tree always exists): `--create-dir` or `--json` makes a missing folder; without a terminal, or with `NHM_NONINTERACTIVE` (ntm's `NTM_NONINTERACTIVE`), the spawn refuses with ntm's `project directory D does not exist; pass --create-dir to create it, or scaffold with 'nhm quick S'`; on a terminal it asks `Create it?`, and a no prints `Aborted.` and exits 0; nhm's `--dry-run` makes nothing; `add` has no `--create-dir`. The text and the JSON are ntm's (nhm-2p6.4.8; ACFS's readers: acfs-9hkh.108): nhm's result, scope, operation id and warnings go under `_nhm`, and nhm's agent lines to stderr; a refused admission is `RESOURCE_BUSY` with the admission; there is no `recovery`, `profile_set` or `seat_skips`, and no pane size or command; the step lines print when the run ends, not as it goes, and without ntm's "Waiting for panes to initialize" (tmux's `pane_init_delay_ms`; herdr has none); a dry run and a replay (nhm's `--dry-run` and `--op-id`) print nhm's text; with `--assign` the text is ntm's too, its phase steps (`Waiting for agents to become ready`, `Sending init prompt to ready agents`, `Assigning work to agents`) around the assignment's lines, but the JSON keeps nhm's envelope until ntm's `SpawnAssignResult` (nhm-2p6.4.11, after nhm-2p6.9's `AssignOutputEnhanced`). The prompts follow ntm's rule (nhm-2p6.4.6; ACFS: acfs-9hkh.109): an agent gets its kind's `[prompts]` default (`cc_default`, `cod_default`, `agy_default`, `omp_default`, or the `*_default_file`), then `--prompt`, and nothing with neither; with no prompt to send, spawn does not wait for the agents, except with `--assign` or nhm's `--trust-folder`. A `*_default_file` that cannot be read refuses the spawn before it starts; ntm fails it after the launch. The prompt goes alone, as ntm's, with no identity preamble: the agent's Agent Mail name is in Agent Mail's identity file for its pane (the operator, 2026-10-24; ACFS's kickoff texts that say "register": acfs-9hkh.110). `--assign` sends `--prompt` only, without the `[prompts]` defaults. nhm's spawn wizard keeps its "ACFS's default_new_agent prompt" choice (`KickoffTemplate` in core/spawn). `--cass-context Q` turns CASS on with Q as its query, as typed, in place of the prompt's keywords; `--cass-context-days N` and `--cass-context-limit N` bound its hits (above 0), and `--no-cass-context` wins over them; without them nhm's `[cass]` settings hold, not ntm's 3 sessions over 30 days. With a query and no prompt, ntm sends the CASS context alone; nhm sends nothing. Not yet: the recovery prompt and `--no-recovery` (nhm-2p6.4.13). `--with-agent-name` is ntm's (nhm-2p6.4.11): it changes only `--init-prompt` with `--assign` (that row). The stagger is ntm's (nhm-2p6.4.14): `--stagger-mode` none and `--stagger-delay` 30s by default, the `[spawn]` keys beneath them, and `--stagger[=D]` (90s bare, 0 off) paces the prompts in mode `legacy` while the mode is none, and counts as choosing the mode. With no agent flag, the counts are the `[defaults] agents` of nhm's project config, the nearest `.nhm/config.toml` (ntm's is `.ntm/config.toml`, which nhm does not read: the operator, 2026-10-24), with ntm's `Using default configuration: 2 cc, 1 cod`; a type nhm has no kind for (gmi, cursor, windsurf, aider, ollama) refuses. nhm bounds every ready wait with `--ready-timeout` (ntm's bounds only the `--assign` wait; its prompt wait is a fixed 30 s), refuses at admission (exit 1). Three differences stay by the operator's rulings of 2026-10-24: nhm always adds new panes, also to a session that exists (ntm launches into its panes, and nhm keeps no user pane to spare); after a failure it closes the panes it made that hold no running agent and retires the identities it registered, while the failure response still lists every pane it made (`partial_mutation`, `affected_pane_ids`; each target's `cleanup` under `_nhm`); and it runs in the cwd's work tree or `--project`, never in ntm's `projects_base/<session>` (no `~/ntm_Dev`, no `NTM_PROJECTS_BASE`). **On herdr:** `workspace.create`, `tab.create`, `pane.split` and `agent.start` in core/spawn; `--pane-env` is the `env` of `pane.split` and `tab.create`; `--verify-boot` is nhm's ready wait; `--auto-restart` is recycle under `nhm maintain`; a new kind needs an adapter | `internal/cli/spawn.go`, `internal/cli/spawn_output.go`, `internal/cli/spawn_output_ntm.go`, `internal/core/spawn`, `internal/core/privacy` | `TestSpawnJSONIsSpawnResponse`, `TestSpawnText`, `TestSpawnFailureResponse`, `TestSpawnSafety`, `TestSpawnPromptsDefault`, `TestKickoffIsThePromptAlone`, `TestSpawnNoKickoffWithoutPrompt`, `TestPromptsResolveForType`, `TestSpawnRequestPrompts`, `TestSpawnStaggerDefaults`, `TestSpawnProjectDefaults`, `TestSpawnProjectDefaultsNote`, `TestSpawnPaneEnv`, `TestSpawnPaneFlags`, `TestSpawnCreateDir`, `TestSpawnPrivacyFlags`, `TestSpawnRecordsPrivacy`, `TestSendSkipsHistoryInPrivacyMode`, `TestCanPersist`, `TestRecordAndLookup`, `TestLoadConfig`, `TestWouldBlock`, `TestPrivateSpawnStoresPromptDigests`, `TestPrivateInjectionsKeepNoText`, `TestPreserveSkipsAPrivateSession`, `TestSpawnVerifyBoot`, `TestSpawnCassContextFlags`, `TestCASSQueryDaysAndLimit` | deferred: nhm-2p6.4 (spawn and add) |
| `status <session> [--tag T] [--assignments [--status S] [--agent T] [--pane N] [--summary]] [-w\|--watch] [--interval MS]` | — | ntm needs the session. It shows boxed sections: the folder, the panes, context usage, agent counts, Agent Mail with its file reservations, the handoff, and quick actions (`ntm send S --all …`, `ntm view S`, `ntm zoom S <n>`). `--tag` filters the panes. `--assignments` adds the bead assignments, filtered by `--status`, `--agent` and `--pane`; `--summary` shows their stats only. `-w` refreshes every `--interval` milliseconds (2000). JSON: `StatusResponse{generated_at, session, exists, attached, working_directory, panes[], agent_counts, agent_mail, handoff, assignments, assignment_stats, assignment_filters, assignment_summary}`. A missing session: `session 'S' not found`, exit 1. **Gaps:** `--tag`, `--assignments`, `--status`, `--agent`, `--pane`, `--summary` and `-w` are unknown flags. `--interval` is a Go duration (30s), so `--interval 2000` is refused. The text is a table `SESSION SLOT NAME KIND MODEL STATE BEAD MAIL GUARDS PANE`; the JSON is nhm's envelope (`generated_at, workspaces, agents, absent_bindings, displaced_bindings, sources`). Without a session nhm lists every workspace. **On herdr:** core/snapshot has the bindings, beads, mail and guards; `--assignments` reads core/assignment's ledger; tags need pane tokens; the reservations come from the agentmail integration; context usage needs a source that herdr does not give | `internal/cli/status.go` | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.3 (session reads) |
| `summary [session] [--since D] [--format F] [--all] [--recent] [--regenerate]` | — | ntm's summary writes a session summary from the agents' output. Live: it reads 500 lines per agent pane, cuts them to `--since` (30m) by its archive's times, adds git's changed, new and deleted files, and renders ntm's summary. `--format` takes text, brief, json, markdown (md), detailed or handoff (`json` forces JSON). `--all` lists the saved summaries (`Available summaries (fmt):` or `No summaries found.`; JSON a list of `{session, timestamp, path}`, `[]` when none). `--recent` shows the latest saved one. `--regenerate` builds it again from ntm's archive and writes a summary file; `--recent` with `--regenerate` is refused. A session with a saved summary shows that one. Text is the rendered summary. JSON `SessionSummary {session, generated_at, format, accomplishments?, changes?, files?, pending?, errors?, decisions?, thread_summaries?, token_estimate, text, handoff?}`. Errors exit 1 (`no summaries found`, `invalid --format …`, `no archived output found …`). ntm's hidden monitor daemon writes the archive and the saved summaries. **On herdr:** `pane.read` recent 500 per agent; git by exec; port ntm's summary generator (it decides the text). `--robot-summary` is separate code. **Rulings:** nhm has no source for ntm's archive or saved summaries: what do `--since`, `--recent`, `--all` and `--regenerate` read (nhm's kept transcripts and CASS are the nearest)? | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.8 (pane reads) |
| `support-bundle -o PATH [--session S] [--panes 1,2] [--lines N] [--include-sessions] [--since D] [--max-size B] [--no-redact] [--include-events]` | — | Writes a zip (`-o` is required; `.zip` is added) with `versions.json` (ntm, go, `tmux -V`, and the `--version` of bv, br, cm, cass, ubs and dcg), `doctor.json` (the `doctor` report), `config_effective.toml` (redacted), `events.jsonl` (ntm's analytics events, `--since`; `--include-events` is on by default), with `--include-sessions` (off by default) a `sessions/<s>/snapshot.json` and pane captures (`--session`, `--panes`, `--lines 200`), and `manifest.json` (each file's sha256, an overall checksum, the platform). Redaction is on unless `--no-redact`. A bundle larger than `--max-size` (52428800 bytes) is deleted and refused. Text: `Support bundle created: <path>`, `Size: N bytes`, `Files: N`, `Redacted: true` or `false`; `--json` `BundleResult {success, path, size, files, redacted, error}`; a failure exits 1. `--robot-support-bundle` is a separate path with other defaults. **On herdr:** `tmux -V` becomes herdr's version and protocol (`internal/herdr`); sessions and captures become herdr workspaces and pane reads (as `read` and `logs`); `doctor.json` is nhm's `doctor --json`; the config is nhm's `config.toml`; the events come from nhm's store history (`internal/store/history`); redaction is `internal/redaction` (ntm's patterns) | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.10 (config and setup) |
| `swarm [--scan-dir D] [--projects P] [--dry-run] [--sessions-per-type N] [--panes-per-session N] [--output F] [--prompt T\|--prompt-file F] [--wait-ready] [--ready-timeout S] [--remote U@H]`, `swarm plan\|status\|stop` | — | ntm's launch over many projects. It scans `--scan-dir` (`/dp`, else `[swarm] default_scan_dir`), counts open beads per project with br and plans by tier (at least 400, at least 100, else: cc/cod/gmi 4/4/2, 3/3/2, 1/1/1) into sessions `{type}_agents_{n}` that mix projects. A real launch needs `[swarm] enabled = true`. It creates the sessions, starts each agent with `cd <project> && <cmd>`, types the prompt (300 ms stagger) and, with `--wait-ready`, polls every 500 ms. `plan` is the dry run. `status` checks each session named `^(cc\|cod\|gmi\|agy)_agents_[0-9]+$`. `stop [pattern...]` (`--force`, `--timeout 5s`, `-y`) exits each agent with its keys and kills the session; with `--json` it needs `--yes` or `--force`. Flags of every swarm subcommand: `--auto-rotate-accounts`, `--force-global-auth-clobber`. `stop` also takes `--sessions` (name patterns). Text: the plan, then the launch lines. `--json`: `SwarmPlanOutput` (`total_*`, `allocations[]`, `sessions[]`, `dry_run`, `execution{}`, `account_rotation`); status `{checked_at, sessions[], summary, overall_status}`; stop `ShutdownResult`. Codes `INVALID_FLAG`, `TIMEOUT`, `NOT_FOUND`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`; exit 1. **On herdr:** a session per type is a workspace; `core/spawn`, `core/send`, `core/wait`, `core/snapshot`, and `core/lifecycle` with the kinds' exit keys. **Rulings:** mixed-project workspaces (nhm groups agents per project per tab and warns on a mix): one tab per project, or another layout; ntm's swarm registers no Agent Mail identity, nhm's spawn does; caam rotation, the Claude model snapshot and `--remote` have no nhm or herdr part | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.27 (swarm) |
| `switch <agent-name> [--session S]` | — | ntm's switch focuses the pane of an Agent Mail agent. It searches `--session`, or every tmux session, in ntm's agent registry and takes the first exact match. Inside tmux it switches the client and selects the pane: `Switched to pane %ID (Name, pane N) in session S`. Outside tmux it prints where the agent is and the tmux commands to run. A registry that fails to load warns on stderr. JSON `{action, agent_name, session, pane_index, pane_id, pane_title}` (`action` is `selected` or `instructions`). Not found: `no agent named "X" found in any session`, exit 1, plain text also with `--json`. **On herdr:** `core/bindings` is the registry; the focus is `workspace.focus`, `tab.focus` and `pane.focus` (or `agent.focus`; nhm's herdr client has no wrapper yet). herdr has no attach, so nhm always selects. `pane_index` is the slot's M. ntm compares names exactly; nhm's bindings ignore case | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.8 (pane reads) |
| `timeline [list] [-n N]`, `timeline show <session> [-e]\|delete <session> [-f]\|cleanup [-f]\|export <session> [-f jsonl\|svg\|png] [-o F]\|stats` | — | ntm reads the agent-state timelines that its resident `ntm monitor` writes: one file per session under `$XDG_DATA_HOME/ntm/timelines/` (gzip after 24 h, 30 kept). Each line is an `AgentEvent` (`agent_id` such as `cc_1`, `agent_type`, `session_id`, `state`: idle, working, waiting, error or stopped, `previous_state`, `timestamp`, `duration`, …). The monitor records each pane's observed state and saves every 5 minutes and at exit; the dashboard records too. `list` (`-n` 20): `TimelineListResult{timelines[], total_count, showing}`. `show` (`-e` adds the events): `{info, events, stats}`. `delete` and `cleanup` ask `[y/N]` unless `-f`; cleanup gives `{deleted, remaining, compressed}`. `stats`: `{total_timelines, total_events, total_agents, total_size_bytes, compressed_count, oldest_timeline, newest_timeline}`. `export` writes jsonl, svg or png (`--since`, `--until`, `-w` 1200, `-s` 1 to 3, `--light`, `--no-legend`, `--no-metadata`; default file `<session>_timeline.<fmt>`) and prints `✓ Exported N events to F`. Prompt markers of send and replay stay in the sending process and are lost. Errors exit 1. **On herdr:** a recorder on herdr's agent-state events (`events.subscribe`) in `nhm maintain --loop` or the board: working is working, idle and done are idle, blocked is waiting, a closed pane is stopped; agent ids are ntm's `<type>_<n>`. Port the file store and the svg and png export | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.19 (history and analytics) |
| `unlock <session> [patterns...] [--all] [--pane N] [--task-id T]` | — | ntm releases the session identity's Agent Mail reservations (`release_file_reservations`): the given patterns, or all with `--all`; neither is refused ("specify patterns to release or use --all"). Text with `--all`: `Released N reservation(s) for <agent>` or `No active reservations to release for <agent>`. With patterns: `Released N reservation(s)`, then `[_] <pattern>` per requested pattern (not per released one); 0 released fails "released 0 reservations for N requested pattern(s)", exit 1. `--json`: `UnlockResult` {success, session, agent, released, released_at, error, receipt}. `--pane` with `--task-id` adds the `receipt` {paths, session, pane, task_id, released, already_released, release_count, timestamp, conflict}; 0 released then gives `success: false` with `already_released: true`. **On herdr:** as `lock`. **Rulings:** the identity, as for `lock`. | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.16 (Agent Mail and files) |
| `upgrade [--check] [-f\|--force] [-y\|--yes] [--strict] [-v] [--release-tag TAG]`, `update` | — | ntm's self-update from its GitHub releases (the latest, or `--release-tag`; `GITHUB_TOKEN` as bearer). It picks the `ntm_<ver>_<os>_<arch>.tar.gz` asset (other names too, unless `--strict`), asks y/N (`-y` skips it), downloads, checks `checksums.txt` (a missing file is only a warning), extracts, replaces the running binary and keeps a `.old` backup, runs `<new> version --short` (on a mismatch it offers to restore), then offers to change `ntm init` to `ntm shell` in the shell rc files. `--force` installs the same version again. Text: `🔄 NTM Upgrade`, the versions, platform and asset, `✓ You're already on the latest version!` or `⬆ New version available` (with `--check`), progress, `✓ Successfully upgraded to X!`. `--json` needs `--check` (else `INVALID_FLAG`, exit 1): `upgradeCheckJSONOutput {success, current_version, latest_version, update_available, release_tag, release_url, asset_name, asset_size, match_strategy, strict, tried_names, …}`. A failed release lookup exits 0 without `--strict` or `--release-tag`. **On herdr:** no herdr part. nhm's releases differ: `nhm-<ver>-linux-{amd64,arm64}.tar.zst` with a required `SHA256SUMS`, each with `nhm`, `nhm-hook` and the herdr plugin; `install.sh` unpacks to a versioned release directory, swaps a `current` link and keeps no `.old`. A port runs `install.sh`'s steps and checks with `version --short`. Never test it on the development host (rule 10). **Rulings:** ACFS pins nhm's version and archive SHA-256 and updates its tools with `acfs update`: refuse when pinned, hand over to `acfs update`, or run `install.sh`'s steps? | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.10 (config and setup) |
| `wait [session] [--until idle\|complete\|generating\|healthy[,…]] [--timeout D] [--poll D] [--pane N] [--type T] [--any [--count N]] [--exit-on-error]` | — | ntm polls the agent panes and reads each screen: waiting, generating, thinking, error or stalled. It skips user and unknown panes. `idle` is waiting; `complete` is waiting with 5 s of no output (at once for an agent with no output yet); `generating` is generating; `healthy` is not error or stalled; in a comma list all must hold. Every agent must match, or `--count` (1) of them with `--any`. `--pane` is an index (-1: all); `--type` takes ntm's names (`cc` is claude); `--timeout` 5m, `--poll` 2s; `--exit-on-error` stops at an agent in error. Text only: `⏳ Waiting for 'S' until idle (timeout: 5m0s)...`, then `✓ Condition 'idle' met after 1.2s` with a line per pane, `✗ Timeout after …` or `! No matching panes found`. ntm has no JSON form: `--json` prints the text, and an error as ntm's JSON error. Exit 0 or 1; the help names 2 and 3, but ntm maps them to 1. **Gaps:** `--exit-on-error` is an unknown flag; a comma list is refused (exit 2). `--until complete` waits for a new turn, so an agent that is idle already times out or ends ambiguous (exit 4). `--type cc` matches no agent; `--pane 2` matches no slot (exit 2). The text is a line per agent (`Name (slot): idle`) and `idle: met after 1.2s`; nhm also writes JSON (its envelope). **On herdr:** core/wait on herdr's states; `--exit-on-error` is `--robot-wait`'s fail-fast; `complete` is idle or done, as in `--robot-wait`; a list is an AND of core conditions; blocked is the nearest to error and stalled | `internal/cli/wait.go`, `internal/core/wait` | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.5 (send family) |
| `work triage\|alerts\|search\|impact\|next\|queue-dry\|commit-ready\|history\|forecast\|graph\|label-health\|label-flow\|burndown`, `work commit-readiness` | — | ntm's bv front end for the cwd's project; no session, no tmux. Bare `work` prints help, exit 0. Each subcommand has flags of its own (for example `alerts --critical-only --label --type`, `queue-dry --commits --force --parent`, `commit-ready --sync-lag-minutes`): the tree at 9d5ed977 lists them. triage: bv's triage (`-n` 10, `--quick`, `--health`, `--format json\|markdown\|auto`, `--compact`); `--by-label` or `--by-track` print bv's raw grouped JSON. next: the top pick (`next --json` with none prints text). alerts, search `<query>` (`--mode text\|hybrid`), impact `<paths...>`, history (`--since 30d`), forecast `[issue-id]`, graph (`--format json\|dot\|mermaid`), label-health, label-flow and burndown `<sprint>` run bv's robot modes and render them; `--json` prints bv's JSON. queue-dry tells if the queue is dry: bv's summary and in-progress beads, the `.beads` sync age, the git log, reservations and quiescence; `--ideate` proposes work, and `--create-beads --yes` creates it with br. JSON `QueueDryResponse` {success, project, queue_dry, evidence, quiescence, ideation, recommendations, recipes, warnings, errors}. commit-ready tells if a commit is safe: git status, the `.beads` sync lag, reservations and urgent mail for `--agent` (`AGENT_NAME`), a commit-message lint. JSON `CommitReadyResponse` {success, project, agent, safe_to_commit, summary, findings, notes, evidence, warnings, errors}. Both exit 0 when unsafe or dry. **On herdr:** no herdr call. nhm's bv integration has ntm's triage and next. New: the bv robot-mode wrappers and their renders (one engine with nhm-2p6.13's `--robot-triage`, `--robot-graph` and the like), queue-dry and commit-ready. | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.21 (work) |
| `work-snapshot [--project P] [-n N] [--refresh] [--timeout D] [--watch] [--wait-ready N] [--interval D] [--wait-timeout D] [--require-reservations]` | — | ntm prints a project's durable work view, always as JSON: ready, blocked and in-progress beads, a summary, triage and graph, checked against the checkout, the tracker, the `[assign.work_source]` policy and live reservations. A per-project cache holds it 45 s; `--refresh` skips the cache. `--limit/-n` 10 (1 to 100000); `--timeout` 10s per read (at most 1 minute). `--watch` prints one object per observation; `--wait-ready N` waits for N ready beads (`--wait-timeout` 5m, at most 24 h); `--interval` 2s (100 ms to 30 s). `--watch` and `--wait-ready` exclude each other; `--interval` and `--require-reservations` need one of them; `--wait-timeout` needs `--wait-ready`. JSON: ntm's robot response fields (success, timestamp, version, output_format, _meta, error, error_code, hint, structured_error), then `project`, `work` {ready[], blocked[], in_progress[], summary, triage, graph, available, reason, verification} and `observation`. Codes WORK_SNAPSHOT_UNAVAILABLE, CANCELLED, TIMEOUT and the work source's stale code; hint "Inspect the source and retry with --refresh". A failure prints JSON on stdout and `Error:` on stderr, exit 1. **On herdr:** no herdr call. nhm has ntm's work source and bv ported. New: ntm's robot work adapters and a project-scoped cache table (nhm's store is per herdr instance). The same engine feeds `--robot-snapshot`'s `work` section. | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.21 (work) |
| `workflows list\|show\|run\|status\|recover [-s S] [--var k=v]`, `workflow`, `wf` | — | ntm's TOML workflow templates (not pipeline files): builtins `red-green`, `review-pipeline`, `specialist-team`, `parallel-explore`, then `~/.config/ntm/workflows/`, then `.ntm/workflows/` (the project wins). `list`: `Available Workflow Templates` by source, `Total: N template(s)`; `--json` `{workflows[{name, description, source, coordination, agent_count}], total}`. `show <name>`. `run <name-or-path>` (a path when it has a separator or ends in `.toml`; `--project-root`, `--max-transitions 8`, `--interval 2s`, `--timeout 15m`, `--fire-manual`, `--trigger-timeout 0`, `--resume` or `--restart`) gives the roles to the session's agent panes in pane order. At each interval it reads 200 lines per pane, checks the triggers (file, command, `agent_says`, `all_agents_idle`, time; `manual` only with `--fire-manual`), sends the stage prompt by `ntm send`'s path and handles faults (`restart_agent`, `pause`, `skip_stage`, `abort`, `notify`, `retry`). Checkpoint: `.ntm/workflows/state/<session>.json`. `status -s S` and `recover --session --pane --token --outcome delivered\|not-sent` read or fix it and never send. `run --json`: `{success, workflow, session, agents[{role, pane}], stages, transitions, completed, resumed, faults[], restarts, reason, error}`. Errors: `{success:false, error}`, exit 1. **On herdr:** `core/send` sends (its gate and `--if-generation` replace the pane-PID check); a herdr pane read feeds `agent_says`; herdr's state gives `all_agents_idle` and the faults; `core/lifecycle` recycle restarts; `core/watcher` gives file triggers | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.25 (pipeline and workflows) |
| `worktree list [-o table\|json]`, `worktree provision <agent> <session-id>`, `worktree remove <agent> <session-id>`, `worktree cleanup [--max-age D] [--dry-run]`, `worktree sync <path>`, `worktree auto-provision <session>`, `worktree status [session]`, `worktree clean-session <session>` | — | ntm's per-agent git worktrees beside the repository: `<repo>/../agent-…-<agent>-session-…-<session>` on branch `agent/<agent>/<session>` (not the layout of `worktrees`). The cwd must be in a git repository. Bare `worktree` prints help, exit 0. list: a table `AGENT BRANCH PATH COMMIT LAST USED` or `No agent worktrees found.`; JSON an array of {path, branch, commit, agent, created_at, last_used} (`--json` wins over `-o`). provision: `✓ Worktree created successfully!` with the path, branch and commit. remove and clean-session remove worktrees; cleanup removes those unused for `--max-age` (168h). sync: `git fetch origin` and a merge of the default branch into that worktree. auto-provision: a worktree for each agent pane of the session, then Ctrl-C and a typed `cd <path>` in each pane; it lists provisioned, skipped and failed, then `Processing completed in D`. status takes the session or the current tmux session ("session name required (not in tmux session)"). **On herdr:** the git steps need no herdr (nhm's lifecycle facts already read `git worktree list`). **Rulings:** auto-provision types Ctrl-C and `cd` into a running agent. On herdr that interrupts the agent and sends `cd …` as a prompt: another design (set the pane's cwd before the agent starts) or a waiver? | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.17 (git and worktrees) |
| `worktrees list`, `worktrees merge <agent> [--force]`, `worktrees clean [--session S] [--force]`, `worktrees remove <agent> [--force]` | — | ntm's session worktrees, the layout that `spawn --worktrees` makes: `<project>/.ntm/worktrees/<session>/<agent>` on branch `ntm/<session>/<agent>`. The scope is an explicit session's project, else the cwd's project and the current session (the current tmux session, the cwd, else the project folder's name). Bare `worktrees` prints help, exit 0. list: `Worktrees for session: S`, then per agent `Agent:`, `Path:`, `Branch:` and `Status: ✓ Active` or `✗ <error>`; JSON {session, worktrees[], total}. merge: checks out the default branch in the main tree and merges the agent's branch with `--no-ff` (`--force` merges despite worktree errors); `Successfully merged agent A's work from branch B`. clean: a `(y/N)` prompt on stdin unless `--force`, then `Successfully cleaned up N worktrees for session: S`. remove: shows the removal risk in a confirm (TUI) unless `--force` (which `--json` needs), then deletes the worktree and force-deletes its branch. **On herdr:** git only; the confirm is an nhm popup or a stdin prompt. It is useful only when nhm's spawn makes this layout (`spawn --worktrees`, the same bead). | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.17 (git and worktrees) |
| `zoom [session-name] [pane-index]`, `z` | — | ntm's form of zoom: it zooms one pane and attaches. The session resolves as ntm's verbs do (current, cwd, only session, picker; prefix). The pane is an index, else an agent type (its first pane). With no pane: `--json` errors `pane index is required for JSON output`; with no terminal, `non-interactive environment: pane index is required for zoom`; on a terminal a numbered picker asks (`Select pane to zoom:`; q or empty cancels, exit 0). Text: `✓ Zoomed pane N in 'S'`. JSON `SuccessResponse {success, message}`; a failure is `ErrorResponse`, exit 1 (`pane N not found in session 'S'`). **On herdr:** nhm's zoom (`core/zoom`). Gaps: the alias `z`; an optional session (the calling workspace) and pane (nhm's picker popup or ntm's numbered list); the prefix match; nhm's text and envelope in place of `SuccessResponse`; nhm exits 2 for an unknown session or pane and for a missing argument; a pane index is refused by design (the grammar bead decides indexes) | `internal/cli/attach.go`, `internal/core/zoom` | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.3 (session reads) |

## The other robot flags

| Form | Callers | Output | nhm | Test | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `--robot-account-status [--account-status-provider=P\|--provider=P]` | — | ntm asks caam for the AI accounts and their usage. It runs `caam robot status --compact`, `caam cost sessions --json` and, for claude and openai only, `caam limits <provider> --format json --rank earliest-reset-headroom`. The provider filter is canonical: `cc` and `anthropic` are `claude`; `cod`, `codex` and `chatgpt` are `openai`; `gmi` and `google` are `gemini`. JSON `AccountStatusOutput`: `accounts`, a map from provider to `{current, usage_percent, limit_reset, available_accounts, rate_limited, tier, resets_at, governing_window, recommended_seat, recommended_persona, seats[], limits_error}`. A provider that is asked for but absent is listed with zero values. A limits failure fills `limits_error`; it is not an error. No caam: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING`, with `accounts: {}`. A caam status failure: `INTERNAL_ERROR`. Exit 1 on error. **On herdr:** no tmux part. nhm's quota integration runs only `caam status` today; it needs ntm's three caam calls. A codex account is keyed `openai`, not `codex` | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.18 (accounts, quota and context) |
| `--robot-accounts-list [--accounts-list-provider=P\|--provider=P]` | — | ntm lists every caam account. It runs `caam robot status --compact` (cached for the process) and `caam cost sessions --json` for the cost. The provider filter is canonical, as for `--robot-account-status`. JSON `AccountsListOutput`: `accounts[] {provider, id, email, name, current, usage_percent, rate_limited, cost_cents, cooldown}`. `accounts` is `[]` on every path, never null. ntm declares `usage_percent` but never fills it. No caam: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING`. A caam failure: `INTERNAL_ERROR`. Exit 1 on error. **On herdr:** no tmux part. The caam calls go into nhm's quota integration, with those of `--robot-account-status` | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.18 (accounts, quota and context) |
| `--robot-acfs-status` | — | ntm reports ACFS and the tools it needs. It finds each tool on PATH and runs its version flag: tmux (`-V`, required), br, bv, claude, codex, gemini and git, then `acfs --version`. It touches no session. No modifiers. ntm's `--robot-setup` is an alias: the same branch and output. JSON `ACFSStatusOutput`: `acfs_available, acfs_version, acfs_path, tools`, a map keyed `tmux, br, bv, cc, cod, gmi, git` of `{installed, version, path, hint, required}`. No acfs: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING`, and the tools map is still filled. Exit 1 on error. ntm's list has no `agy`. **On herdr:** no tmux part. The probes of `nhm deps` cover most of these tools. **Rulings:** the required `tmux` key has no meaning on herdr. Report herdr under ntm's `tmux` key, or add a `herdr` key? | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.15 (ops tools) |
| `--robot-agent-health=S [--panes=P] [--lines=N] [--no-caut] [--verbose]` | — | ntm scores the health of each agent in a live session. It reads the session observer (working, idle, rate limit, context low), caut's usage per provider (cached 5 minutes; `--no-caut` skips it) and the process_triage (`pt`) states, then computes a score, a grade, issues and a recommendation. `--panes` takes N, W.P or %N. `--lines` is 100 in the code (the registry says 20). `--verbose` (hidden old name `--agent-health-verbose`) adds a raw sample. JSON `AgentHealthOutput`: `session, query, caut_available, pt_available, pt_status, pt_observation_mode, panes` (a map: `agent_type, local_state, provider_usage, pt_health, health_score, health_grade, issues[], recommendation, recommendation_reason, raw_sample`), `non_agent_panes, provider_summary, pt_summary, fleet_health`. Codes: `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `INVALID_FLAG` or `PANE_NOT_FOUND` (selectors), `TIMEOUT`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`; `success: false` exits 1. Not the same surface as `--robot-health=S`. **On herdr:** herdr's agent state gives working and idle; a screen read, as `--robot-health=S` does it, gives the limit and context indicators; nhm's quota integration replaces caut. pt has no herdr part: run the host's `pt`, or report `pt_status` disabled. Port ntm's score, grade and recommendation | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.18 (accounts, quota and context) |
| `--robot-agent-names=S [--spawn-names=a,b]` | — | ntm computes a name for each pane of a session from the pane order. A name is `<type>-<nato>`, with one NATO counter over all panes, the user pane too (`user-alpha`, then `claude-bravo`). Names from `--spawn-names` come first for the agent panes. After 26 names a `-2` suffix follows. `pane` is always `0.<index>`, sorted as a string (`0.10` before `0.2`). `agent_type` is the long name (`claude`, `codex`, `gemini`). The session is passed as given, not resolved. It reads tmux only: no store, no Agent Mail. JSON `AgentNamesOutput`: `session, agents[] {name, pane, agent_type}, count`; `agents` is never null. Codes: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (no tmux), `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`. **On herdr:** herdr's panes of the workspace and nhm's bindings. These names are not Agent Mail names; a caller can confuse them. **Rulings:** nhm keeps no user pane, so its first agent is `alpha` where ntm's is `bravo`. Keep ntm's count, or start at `alpha`? | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.8 (pane reads) |
| `--robot-alerts [--severity=S] [--alerts-type=T] [--session=S]` | — | ntm generates the active alerts of every tmux session and lists them. It captures each pane for crash, error, rate-limit and context-warning lines, checks the free disk space, and asks bv and br for stale in-progress beads and dependency cycles. Types: `agent_crashed, agent_error, rate_limit, context_warning, disk_low, bead_stale, dependency_cycle`. `--severity` and `--session` have the hidden old names `--alerts-severity` and `--alerts-session`. JSON `TUIAlertsOutput`: `session, count, alerts[] {id, type, severity, session, pane, message, created_at, age_seconds, dismissible}, dismissed, _agent_hints`. The only error is `SESSION_NOT_FOUND` (the filter). The alert tracker lives in one process, so each call makes every alert again: `age_seconds` is about 0, every alert is dismissible, and a dismissal by another process is not seen. **On herdr:** herdr's states (blocked, unknown), a screen read per agent for error and limit lines (nhm's limit recogniser) and bv for the beads; `snapshot.Attention` can feed it. Port ntm's alert ids, types and severities | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.22 (attention and alerts) |
| `--robot-answer-dialog=S --panes=P --choice=C` | — | ntm answers the dialog on one agent pane of a live session. `--panes` must resolve to exactly one pane. `--choice` is `decline`, `extra-usage`, `dismiss` or `option-K`. ntm captures the visible screen, classifies the dialog, maps the choice to keys and sends them 150 ms apart; it captures a cursor dialog again before Enter, and the screen again after 700 ms to check. Classes: `none, trust_prompt, rate_limit_options, usage_overlay, paste_limbo, destructive_confirm, unknown_dialog`. JSON `AnswerDialogOutput`: `session, pane, target, before {class, options[], evidence, input_mode}, choice, keys_sent[], after, resolved, follow_up_dialog`. Codes: `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`; `INVALID_FLAG` (no pane or several, an unknown choice, no dialog, an accept on a destructive confirm, whose message starts `POLICY_REFUSED:`); `PANE_NOT_FOUND`; `TIMEOUT`; `INTERNAL_ERROR` (capture or send failed, the dialog stays, a follow-up dialog). **On herdr:** herdr's blocked state shows a dialog; a herdr screen read and herdr keys replace capture-pane and send-keys. The keys go through the send core. nhm's kinds know some trust and exit dialogs. Port ntm's classifier and option parser. `--panes` takes the slot `N.M` or a herdr pane id | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.12 (agent screens) |
| `--robot-attention [--attention-cursor=N] [--attention-session=S] [--attention-timeout=D] [--attention-poll=D] [--attention-condition=C] [--profile=P]` | — | ntm blocks until its attention feed has an event that meets a condition, or until the timeout (5m, a Go duration; poll 1s). It replays the journal in ntm's state store from the cursor (`--attention-cursor`, else `--since-cursor`). Conditions: `attention` (default), `action_required, mail_pending, mail_ack_required, context_hot, reservation_conflict, file_conflict, session_changed, pane_changed`. Profiles: operator, debug, minimal, alerts. Other ntm processes write the events (send, spawn, serve, monitors). JSON `AttentionResponse`: `wake_reason` (attention, timeout, cursor_expired, error), `matched_condition, trigger_event, waited_seconds, digest, cursor_info {start_cursor, end_cursor, oldest_cursor, next_command}, replay_window`. Exit 0 on attention. Exit 1 with `TIMEOUT`, `CURSOR_EXPIRED`, `FEED_UNAVAILABLE` (not a standard code), `INVALID_FLAG` (a bad timeout or poll interval), `SESSION_NOT_FOUND` or `INTERNAL_ERROR`. **On herdr:** nhm has no event journal. It needs one in its store, numbered by cursor and fed by herdr's event subscription and nhm's own sources (receipts, mail, quota), and ntm's event, digest, condition and profile types. `snapshot.Attention` lists only current items. It ports with `--robot-events` and `--robot-digest` | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.22 (attention and alerts) |
| `--robot-bead-claim=ID [--bead-assignee=A]` | — | ntm claims a bead with br in the cwd's project: `br update <id> --claim --actor <actor> --json`. The actor is `--bead-assignee`, else `$AGENT_NAME`, else `ntm-robot`. It uses br's plain `--claim`, which in br 0.2.x also reopens a closed bead. JSON `BeadClaimOutput`: `bead_id, title, prev_status, new_status` (`in_progress`), `claimed, actor, _agent_hints`. ntm declares `prev_status` but never fills it. Codes: `INVALID_FLAG` (a blank id), `RESOURCE_BUSY` ("already assigned to"), `INTERNAL_ERROR`. Exit 1 on error. **On herdr:** no tmux part. nhm's bv integration runs br (`RunBd`) and has a compare-and-set claim for assignments | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.13 (br and bv wrappers) |
| `--robot-bead-close=ID [--bead-close-reason=R]` | — | ntm closes a bead with br: `br show <id> --json`, then `br close <id> --json [--reason R]`. JSON `BeadCloseOutput`: `bead_id, title, prev_status, new_status, closed, reason, _agent_hints`. A bead that is already closed is a success with `closed: false` and a warning. The hint tells the agent to run `br sync --flush-only`. Codes: `INVALID_FLAG` (a blank id, or `br show` failed: "not found"), `INTERNAL_ERROR` (parse or close failed). Exit 1 on error. **On herdr:** no tmux part. nhm's bv integration runs br (`RunBd`) | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.13 (br and bv wrappers) |
| `--robot-bead-create --bead-title=T [--bead-type=T] [--bead-priority=N] [--bead-description=D] [--bead-labels=a,b] [--bead-depends-on=a,b]` | — | ntm creates a bead with br: `br create --json --type T --priority P --title X [--description D] [--labels L]`, then `br dep add <new> <dep>` for each dependency. `--bead-title` is required. Defaults: type `task`, priority 2; a priority outside 0 to 4 silently becomes 2. JSON `BeadCreateOutput`: `bead_id, title, type, priority` (as `P2`), `description, labels, created, _agent_hints`. A failed `dep add` is a warning in `_agent_hints`, not an error. The hint suggests `br update <id> --status=in_progress`. Codes: `INVALID_FLAG` (no title), `INTERNAL_ERROR`. Exit 1 on error. **On herdr:** no tmux part. nhm's bv integration runs br (`RunBd`) | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.13 (br and bv wrappers) |
| `--robot-bead-show=ID` | — | ntm shows one bead: `br show <id> --json`. No modifiers. JSON `BeadShowOutput`: `bead_id, title, status, type, priority, assignee, description, labels, created_at, updated_at, depends_on, blocks, comments, _agent_hints`. No flag asks for comments, so `comments` stays empty. Codes: `INVALID_FLAG` (a blank id; a br failure is reported as "not found"), `INTERNAL_ERROR` (parse). Exit 1 on error. **On herdr:** no tmux part. nhm's bv integration runs br (`RunBd`) | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.13 (br and bv wrappers) |
| `--robot-beads-list [--status=S] [--priority=P] [--assignee=A] [--beads-type=T] [--limit=N]` | — | ntm lists beads: `br list --json` with the status, priority (0 to 4 or P0 to P4), assignee and type filters. `--status`, `--priority` and `--assignee` share their values with the hidden old names `--beads-status`, `--beads-priority` and `--beads-assignee`. The limit is the old `--beads-limit` when it is set, else the shared `--limit`, else 20. JSON `BeadsListOutput`: `beads[] {id, title, status, priority, type, assignee, labels, blocked_by, created_at, updated_at, is_ready, is_blocked, description}, total, filtered, summary {open, in_progress, blocked, closed, ready}, _agent_hints`. The summary counts all rows; the list stops at the limit. `ready` counts open beads with no dependency check; `blocked_by` is never filled. No br: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING`. br not set up: a success with an empty list and a hint. Other failures: `INTERNAL_ERROR`. **On herdr:** no tmux part. nhm's bv integration (`RunBd`, `UnmarshalBdList`) | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.13 (br and bv wrappers) |
| `--robot-bulk-assign=S (--from-bv\|--allocation=JSON) [--strategy=impact\|ready\|stale\|balanced] [--skip=P] [--template=F] [--bulk-parallel] [--bulk-stagger=D] [--reserve-files] [--reservation-paths=G] [--require-reservation] [--with-cass\|--no-cass] [--with-memory] [--dry-run]` | named in ACFS's `docs/planning/ROOT_AGENTS_MD_PLAN.md` (`--from-bv`); no script calls it | ntm gives one bead to each idle agent pane of a live session and sends the prompt. The plan comes from bv (`--from-bv`: plan, triage, in-progress beads, stale-work recovery) or from `--allocation`, a JSON map from pane to bead; one is required. The strategy is `impact` unless `--strategy` (or `--bulk-strategy`) is set. For each item ntm claims the bead in br, reserves files in Agent Mail (on by default), sends the prompt with CASS and CM context, and records the assignment. Config `[assign] prompt_template(_file)` sets the prompt. JSON `BulkAssignOutput`: `session, strategy, assignments[], summary {total_panes, assigned, skipped, failed}, unassigned_beads, unassigned_panes, dry_run, allocation_source, work_source_mismatch`. Codes: `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `INVALID_FLAG`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`, `DEPENDENCY_MISSING`, `STALE_WORK_COORDINATION`, `TIMEOUT`, `PANE_NOT_FOUND`, `NOT_IMPLEMENTED` (grok), `DISPATCH_UNKNOWN`, `RESERVATION_REQUIRED`, `ASSIGNMENT_FAILED`. **On herdr:** the send core's delivery gate finds idle agents. nhm's spawn-assign, assignment ledger, send core and bv integration already give one bead to each agent for `--robot-spawn --spawn-assign-work`. Missing: the allocation JSON, ntm's bulk strategies, stale recovery, parallel and stagger, and per-bead templates. **Rulings:** nhm reserves no files for agents. `--reserve-files` (on by default) and `--require-reservation` need a ruling, as `--spawn-require-reservation` did | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.9 (work distribution) |
| `--robot-cass-context=QUERY` | — | ntm asks CASS for past sessions that fit a query: `cass search --json --limit=3 -- <query>`. It takes no `--cass-*` modifiers. JSON `CASSContextOutput`: `query, relevant_sessions[] {summary, key_points, source, agent, when}, suggested_context`. `when` is a date (YYYY-MM-DD). ntm never fills `key_points`. Codes: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (no cass, or no index), `INTERNAL_ERROR`. Exit 1 on error. **On herdr:** no tmux part. nhm's CASS integration runs `cass search --robot`, not `--json`: check its fields against ntm's | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.14 (knowledge tools) |
| `--robot-cass-insights [--cass-since=D\|--since=D]` | — | ntm asks CASS for counts per agent and per workspace: `cass search --json --limit=0 --since=<window> --aggregate=agent,workspace -- '*'`. The window is `--cass-since`, else the shared `--since`, default `7d`. It is a CASS duration here, where `--robot-snapshot` reads `--since` as RFC 3339. JSON `CASSInsightsOutput`: `period, agents[], workspaces[]` (CASS's aggregation buckets); the lists are never null. Codes: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`. Exit 1 on error. **On herdr:** no tmux part. nhm's CASS integration needs an aggregate search | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.14 (knowledge tools) |
| `--robot-cass-search=QUERY [--agent=A] [--workspace=W] [--since=D] [--limit=N]` | — | ntm searches CASS: `cass search --json --limit=N --agent=A --workspace=W --since=D -- <query>`. Each modifier has a `--cass-` twin (`--cass-agent`, `--cass-workspace`, `--cass-since`, `--cass-limit`). The limit is 10, one value for both names. JSON `CASSSearchOutput`: `query, count, total_matches, hits[] {source_path, agent, title, score, snippet, created_at}`; `created_at` is in ms. Codes: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (no cass, or no index), `INTERNAL_ERROR`. Exit 1 on error. ntm's capabilities list only the query. **On herdr:** no tmux part. nhm's CASS client has `--workspace` and `--days`, not `--agent` or `--since` | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.14 (knowledge tools) |
| `--robot-cass-status` | — | ntm reports the CASS index: `cass status --json`. No modifiers. JSON `CASSStatusOutput`: `cass_available, healthy, index {exists, fresh, last_indexed_at, conversations, messages, counts_skipped}`; `last_indexed_at` is in ms. Codes: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (no cass, or no index), `INTERNAL_ERROR`. Exit 1 on error. **On herdr:** no tmux part. nhm's CASS integration has no status call yet; `nhm deps` only finds cass. Twin of `cass status` | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.14 (knowledge tools) |
| `--robot-causality=S [--causality-project=D] [--causality-agent=A] [--causality-bead=ID] [--causality-pane=P] [--causality-type=T] [--causality-chain=C] [--causality-since=T] [--causality-until=T] [--causality-limit=N]` | — | ntm builds one time line of causes and effects for a session, also after the session stops. It reads four sources: ntm's audit log, Agent Mail (the project's reservations and the inbox of `--causality-agent`), the session time line, and pipeline state under the project's `.ntm/pipelines`. Then it filters, removes duplicates, sorts, and cuts at `--causality-limit` (200). The window takes a duration or RFC 3339. The project is `--causality-project`, else the session's project, else the cwd. JSON `CausalityOutput`: `query, events[] {id, source, type, timestamp, session, pane, agent, bead_id, chain_id, run_id, summary, details}, sources[] {name, available, events, error}, total, available, filtered, truncated, warnings`. A missing source shows in `sources`; it is not an error. Codes: `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `INVALID_FLAG` (a bad window), `INTERNAL_ERROR`. **On herdr:** no tmux part. nhm's sources are its store (receipts, operations, history), the assignment ledger and Agent Mail. nhm keeps no audit log, time line or pipelines yet, so those sources report unavailable. Keep ntm's source and type names | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.19 (history and analytics) |
| `--robot-context=S [--lines=N]` | — | ntm estimates how full the context window of each agent in a live session is. For each agent pane (not user, service or unknown panes) it captures the scrollback (`--lines`; 1000 here when not given or 0 or less), finds the model, estimates the tokens (chars/4, times 2.5 for overhead) against the model's window, and replaces the estimate with a true value when it finds one: omp's status bar, or the agent CLI's transcript matched by cwd. It adds the pending rotations. `--panes` is refused with `INVALID_FLAG`. JSON `ContextOutput`: `session, captured_at, agents[] {pane, pane_idx, agent_type, model, estimated_tokens, with_overhead, context_limit, usage_percent, usage_level, confidence, state, source, transcript_*}, summary {total_agents, high_usage_count, avg_usage}, pending_rotations[], _agent_hints`. `source` is `scrollback_estimate`, `status_bar` or `transcript`. A failed capture gives a low estimate, not an error. Codes: `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`. **On herdr:** a herdr pane read for the estimate, herdr's agent state for `state`, and a new transcript reader per kind (nhm's quota integration reads rate windows, not context). Port ntm's model window table and usage levels. nhm has no rotation store, so `pending_rotations` is empty. `pane` is the slot `N.M` | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.18 (accounts, quota and context) |
| `--robot-context-inject=S [--inject-files=a,b] [--inject-max-bytes=N] [--inject-pane=N] [--inject-all] [--inject-dry-run]` | — | ntm types project files into the agent panes of a live session. It reads `--inject-files` (default `AGENTS.md,README.md,.claude/project_context.md`), joins them as `### <path>` blocks with `---`, cuts at `--inject-max-bytes` (0: no limit) with `...(truncated)`, and types the text and Enter into every pane above index 0, or into `--inject-pane`. `--inject-all` adds pane 0, the user pane. A path must stay inside the project; a missing or empty file is skipped. It prints `ContextInjectResult` with no robot envelope: `success, session, injected_files[], total_bytes, truncated, panes_injected[]`. With `--inject-dry-run` nothing is sent and `panes_injected` lists the planned panes, but `dry_run` stays false. No files: a success, nothing sent. Codes, in ntm's envelope: `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `INVALID_FLAG` (a bad path), `PANE_NOT_FOUND`, `NOT_IMPLEMENTED` (an agent type that takes no automated prompt), `INTERNAL_ERROR` (a partial send names the delivered panes). **On herdr:** the send core delivers, one operation per agent (rule 5: no second send path). The targets are the bound agents in slot order; `--inject-pane` maps to a slot; `--inject-all` changes nothing (nhm keeps no user pane). Twin of `context inject` | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.14 (knowledge tools) |
| `--robot-controller-spawn=S [--controller-agent-type=T] [--controller-prompt=F] [--controller-no-prompt]` | — | ntm adds a controller agent to a session. It renders the agent command from its config (`--controller-agent-type`, default `cc`), gives Claude its own credential folder, and reuses pane 1 of the first window only when it is a bare shell, else splits a new pane. It sets the pane's identity and launch spec, sends the launch command, waits a fixed 2 s, then sends the prompt: `--controller-prompt` names a Go template file (`.Session`, `.AgentList`), else ntm's default text; `--controller-no-prompt` skips it. JSON `ControllerResponse`, with no `success` field: `generated_at, session, pane_id, pane_index, agent_type, prompt_used, agent_count, agent_list`. Every failure, an unknown agent type too, is `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, exit 1. **On herdr:** nhm's spawn core adds the agent (quota admission, Agent Mail identity, binding), herdr splits the pane, and the send core sends the kickoff from ntm's template. nhm keeps no shell pane, so it needs no reuse rule. nhm waits until the agent is ready, where ntm waits 2 s: record that difference. Twin of `controller` | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.11 (pane lifecycle) |
| `--robot-dashboard [--json]` | — | ntm prints a fleet dashboard of the whole tmux server, in markdown unless the root `--json` is given. It reads every session and pane (the state is always `unknown`), bv's beads summary and progress, the alert generator, ntm's file-change and conflict tracker, Agent Mail for the cwd, and the attention feed. The markdown starts `# NTM Fleet Dashboard: <cwd base>` and has System, Summary, Attention, Sessions, Alerts, Beads, Conflicts, File changes and Agent Mail tables, with row caps of 10 to 50. JSON `DashboardOutput`: `generated_at, fleet, agents[], metrics, system, summary, beads, progress, alerts[], alert_summary, conflicts[], file_changes[], agent_mail, attention`. `fleet` is the cwd's base name. Each source is best effort, so errors are rare. **On herdr:** nhm's snapshot model (workspaces as sessions, herdr's states, `snapshot.Attention`), bv and Agent Mail. Port ntm's markdown renderer. nhm has no alert generator or file-change tracker yet, so those sections stay empty. **Rulings:** ntm shows every agent as `unknown`; nhm can show herdr's state. Keep ntm's `unknown`? | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.22 (attention and alerts) |
| `--robot-dcg-check --command=CMD [--context=T] [--cwd=D]` | — | ntm asks dcg whether a shell command is safe. It runs `dcg --version`, then `dcg --robot test --format json -- <command>` in `--cwd` (default the cwd), with a 30 s timeout; it unwraps an `rch exec --` wrapper first. It appends a line to its dcg audit log. `--context` is echoed, never passed to dcg. `--cmd` is the old name of `--command`; `--robot-guard` is the old name of this flag. JSON `DCGCheckOutput`: `command, context, cwd, allowed, severity, rationale, suggestion, rule_matched, dcg_version, binary_path, _agent_hints.requires_confirmation, reason` (a deprecated copy). `severity` is `safe` on an allow; `requires_confirmation` is set for critical and high. A blocked command is a success with `allowed: false`, exit 0. Errors, exit 1: `INVALID_FLAG` (no command), `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (dcg missing or not compatible), `TIMEOUT`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`. **On herdr:** no tmux part. nhm's guards integration runs dcg on stdin (`dcg test --stdin --robot --dialect posix`): map its verdict to ntm's fields, or call dcg as ntm does. nhm keeps no dcg audit log. Twin: `safety check` | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.15 (ops tools) |
| `--robot-dcg-status` | — | ntm reports dcg: its PATH lookup and `dcg --version`, the `[integrations.dcg]` settings of the merged config, and the counts in its dcg audit log. No modifiers. JSON `DCGStatusOutput`: `dcg {enabled, available, version, binary_path, config {audit_log, allow_override, custom_blocklist_count, custom_whitelist_count}, stats {commands_checked, commands_blocked, last_blocked {command, timestamp, pane}}}`. `enabled` means available and compatible; it is not a config switch. No dcg is a success with `available: false`. A failed check is `INTERNAL_ERROR`, exit 1. **On herdr:** no tmux part. nhm's guards integration finds dcg and its version. nhm has no `[integrations.dcg]` config and no audit log, so `config` holds the defaults and `stats` zeros, unless nhm keeps ntm's audit file (see `--robot-dcg-check`) | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.15 (ops tools) |

| `--robot-diagnose=S [--diagnose-fix\|--fix] [--diagnose-brief\|--brief] [--diagnose-pane=N]` | — | ntm diagnoses each agent pane of a live session and says how to fix it. For each pane (user panes only with `--diagnose-pane`) it checks the agent process under the shell, the stall time and the error lines of a capture, and sorts the pane as healthy, degraded, rate_limited, unresponsive, crashed or unknown. Recommendations: `restart` (`ntm --robot-restart-pane`), `interrupt` (`ntm --robot-interrupt`), `investigate`, `wait` (with the limit reset). It also finds stale Agent Mail build-slot leases. JSON `DiagnoseOutput`: `session, overall_health` (healthy, degraded, critical), `summary, panes, recommendations[] {pane, pane_target, status, action, reason, auto_fixable, fix_command}, auto_fix_available, auto_fix_command, build_slots`. `--fix` restarts panes, sends the interrupt key and releases stale slots; it prints `{session, fix_mode, fix_attempts[] {pane, action, success, message}, summary}`. `--brief` prints `DiagnoseBriefOutput {session, overall_health, summary, has_issues, fix_available}`, with a sentence such as `12/16 healthy, 2 rate_limited`. `--brief` with `--fix` or `--diagnose-pane` is `INVALID_FLAG`. Codes: `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `PANE_NOT_FOUND`, `TIMEOUT`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`, `INVALID_FLAG`. **On herdr:** herdr's states and nhm's health reading (as for `--robot-health=S`) sort the panes; `--fix` uses nhm's recycle and interrupt. nhm's Agent Mail client has no build-slot calls. `fix_command` names ntm verbs: nhm names its own, or none, as `--robot-assign` does | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.11 (pane lifecycle) |

JSON `ExitCLIOutput`: `session, results[] {pane, target, agent_type, shell_pid, agent_pids, exited, killed, shell_preserved, relaunched, detail, verification_failed}`. When a pane did not exit, lost its shell or did not relaunch: `INTERNAL_ERROR`, with a hint to use `--robot-kill-agent`. Other codes: `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `INVALID_FLAG`, `INTERNAL_ERROR` (a failed relaunch check, before any exit). **On herdr:** nhm's recycle stop stage exits an agent with its kind's exit and checks for the shell; `--relaunch` is recycle's start stage without the handoff and kickoff. **Rulings:** ntm sends double Ctrl+C to every kind but omp; nhm's kinds exit their own way (`/exit` for Codex and pi). Keep nhm's exits? | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.12 (agent screens) |

The limit is the hidden old `--file-beads-limit` when it is set, else the shared `--limit`, else 20 (the registry says 10). JSON `FileBeadsOutput`: `file_path, available, beads` (bv's `FileBeadsResponse`, as given), `limit, error`. Codes: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (no bv, or no beads workspace, with the hint `br init`), `INTERNAL_ERROR` (bv failed, or bad JSON).
