JSON `FileHotspotsOutput`: `available, hotspots` (bv's `FileHotspotsResponse`, as given), `limit, error`. Codes: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (no bv, or no beads workspace), `INTERNAL_ERROR` (bv failed, or bad JSON). Exit 1 on error. **On herdr:** no tmux part. nhm's bv integration has the `FileHotspotsResponse` type: add the call | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.13 (br and bv wrappers) |

The limit is the hidden old `--relations-limit` when it is set, else the shared `--limit`, else 10. `--threshold` (hidden old name `--relations-threshold`, one value) is 0.5; the registry says 0.0. JSON `FileRelationsOutput`: `file_path, available, relations` (bv's `FileRelationsResponse`, as given), `limit, threshold, error`. Codes: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (no bv, or no beads workspace), `INTERNAL_ERROR`. Exit 1 on error. **On herdr:** no tmux part. nhm's bv integration has the `FileRelationsResponse` type: add the call | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.13 (br and bv wrappers) |
| `--robot-files=S [--window=W] [--limit=N]`, `--robot-files` | — | ntm lists recent file changes and the conflicts between agents. A bare `--robot-files` (or an empty value) covers every session; a name is a filter (an unknown name stays a filter; an ambiguous one is `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`). `--window` (old name `--files-window`) is 15m by default: 5m, 15m, 1h, all or a Go duration; a bad value silently becomes 15m. The limit is the old `--files-limit` when it is set, else `--limit`, else 100. ntm reads only the change list in its process memory, not the lasting change ledger that `ntm internal-monitor` writes, so a one-shot ntm always answers `count: 0` with "No file changes in the last 15m". JSON `FilesOutput`: `session, time_window, count, changes[] {timestamp, path, operation, agents[], session, size_bytes, lines_added, lines_removed}, summary {total_changes, unique_files, by_agent, by_operation, most_active_agent, conflicts[] {path, agents, severity, first_edit, last_edit}}, _agent_hints`. A conflict is critical with 3 or more agents, or with edits less than 10 min apart. **On herdr:** no tmux part. nhm has no change ledger. **Rulings:** answer an empty list as ntm does, or add a git sampler and a store table for a real answer (ntm's `changes` verb reads its ledger)? | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.16 (Agent Mail and files) |
| `--robot-forecast=ID\|all` | — | ntm asks bv for the forecast of one bead, or of all beads: `bv --robot-forecast <target>`, in the cwd. An empty value runs nothing. No modifiers. Codes: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (no bv, or no beads workspace), `INTERNAL_ERROR` (bv failed, or bad JSON). Exit 1 on error. **On herdr:** no tmux part. nhm's bv integration has the `ForecastResponse` type: add the call. | `--robot-giil-fetch=URL` | — | ntm downloads an image from a share URL with giil: `giil <url> --json --quiet`, with a 5 min timeout, into giil's default folder; then it reads the file size. An empty URL is `INVALID_FLAG`. JSON `GIILFetchOutput`: `source_url, path, filename, size_bytes, width, height, platform`. Codes: `INVALID_FLAG`, `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (no giil; the hint gives the install command), `TIMEOUT`, `INTERNAL_ERROR` (giil failed, or its output has no path). Exit 1 on error. **On herdr:** no tmux part. nhm needs a giil wrapper in its integrations | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.14 (knowledge tools) |
| `--robot-graph` | — | ntm builds a graph of beads, agents and mail. It runs `bv --robot-insights`, `bv --robot-priority` and `br stats --json`, then links them: Agent Mail's agents of the cwd's project with their inboxes and thread summaries, br's bead lists, and `br dep tree <id>` up and down for each in-progress bead. No tmux, no modifiers. JSON `GraphOutput`: `generated_at, available, error, insights, priority, health, correlation {generated_at, assignments[] {agent, agent_name, agent_type, program, model, beads[], mail_threads[]}, bead_graph, mail_summary, orphan_beads[], orphan_threads[], errors[]}`. `agent_type` comes from the Agent Mail program name. Codes: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (no bv; the correlation is still built), `INTERNAL_ERROR` or `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` from bv's insights; a priority or health failure only fills `error`. **On herdr:** no tmux part. nhm's bv integration (insights, priority, br lists) and its Agent Mail client; port ntm's correlation builder. It is not `work graph`, which exports bv's graph | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.13 (br and bv wrappers) |
| `--robot-guard --command=CMD [--context=T] [--cwd=D]` | — | ntm's old name of `--robot-dcg-check`: the same flag value, handler and output (`DCGCheckOutput`; the response names `robot-dcg-check`). It is not hidden and prints no warning; only its usage text calls it deprecated. `--cmd`, the old name of `--command`, also prints no warning. ntm's capabilities list both as aliases of `dcg-check`. Codes and exits are those of `--robot-dcg-check`: a blocked command is a success with `allowed: false`, exit 0. **On herdr:** no tmux part. nhm answers it wherever it answers `--robot-dcg-check` (nhm's guards integration runs dcg) | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.15 (ops tools) |
| `--robot-health-oauth=S` | — | ntm checks the OAuth and rate-limit health of each agent pane in a live session. For each agent pane it reads the tmux activity time and the last 50 lines (2 s), finds OAuth trouble by text ("authentication failed", "401", "token expired", "please log in"; "thinking" or "working" mean valid), counts rate-limit hits (1 or 2: warning; 3 or more: limited), reads the session's backoff (process memory, so a one-shot call sees none), counts error lines, and groups the panes by provider. `--panes` is refused with `INVALID_FLAG`. JSON `OAuthHealthOutput`: `session, checked_at, agents[] {pane, agent_type, provider, oauth_status, oauth_error, rate_limit_status, rate_limit_count, cooldown_remaining, last_activity_sec, error_count, throttle_phase, throttle_guidance}, pools, summary, display`. `provider` is claude, openai, gemini or unknown; `oauth_status` is valid, expired, error or unknown. Codes: `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`; `success: false` exits 1. **On herdr:** a herdr screen read of each bound agent, as `--robot-health=S` does; the kinds give the provider; nhm's limit recogniser and quota integration give the limit and `cooldown_remaining`. herdr has no last-activity time, so `last_activity_sec` is 0. Port ntm's text lists as they are | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.18 (accounts, quota and context) |
| `--robot-health-restart-stuck=S [--stuck-threshold=D] [--dry-run]` | — | ntm restarts the agents of a live session that have been idle for the threshold. It runs the `--robot-health=S` reading. A pane is stuck when tmux shows no activity for `--stuck-threshold` (default 5m, at least 30 s, a Go duration), whatever its state; blocked and rate-limited panes are skipped with a reason. `--dry-run` (or `--restore-dry-run`) stops before the restart. Else ntm respawns each stuck pane and relaunches its agent from the saved launch spec. JSON `AutoRestartStuckOutput`: `session, stuck_panes[], restarted[], failed[], skipped[] {pane, pane_target, health, reason}, threshold, dry_run, checked_at`. Codes: `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `INVALID_FLAG`, `NOT_IMPLEMENTED` (exit 2: an agent type that cannot relaunch), `TIMEOUT`, `INTERNAL_ERROR` ("N of M stuck pane restarts failed"). Twin of `health --auto-restart-stuck`. **On herdr:** nhm's recycle restarts in place (stop with the kind's exit, start, keep the binding; it adds handoff and kickoff stages). herdr gives no time since the last output, so no agent would ever be stuck. **Rulings:** what makes an agent stuck on herdr: no screen change over two reads, an event subscription, or a herdr feature? | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.11 (pane lifecycle) |
| `--robot-help` | — | ntm prints a plain-text guide to its robot flags, not JSON: a fixed preamble, one section per flag group built from its robot registry (usage, summary, required parameters), then fixed text on output formats, common modifiers, a quick start and the attention-feed loop. No modifiers. `--robot-format` does not change it. Exit 0. Only a bad `--robot-format` or `--robot-verbosity` fails it first, with a JSON `INVALID_FLAG`. It touches nothing, and loads no config. The text says "ntm (Named Tmux Manager)"; its quick start uses `--robot-send … --track` and TOON. **On herdr:** no tmux part. nhm renders its own guide from its inventory, as `--robot-capabilities` does, in ntm's section layout with nhm's flags. It keeps the `--last` N (0: all), then gives stats (`--stats`) or a page (`--limit` and `--offset`, or `--robot-limit` and `--robot-offset`; a negative value is `INVALID_FLAG`). Each modifier has a hidden old `--history-` name; the specific name wins. JSON `HistoryOutput`: `session, generated_at, entries[] {id, ts, session, targets[], agent_types[], prompt, source, template, success, error, duration_ms}, stats, total, filtered, pagination, _agent_hints {summary, suggested_commands, warnings, next_offset, pages_remaining}`. JSON `IncidentResolveOutput`: `incident_id, previous_status, status, note`. Codes: `INVALID_FLAG` (an empty id, or an unknown id; the hint points at `--robot-snapshot` and `--robot-attention`), `INTERNAL_ERROR` (a store read or write). ntm makes incidents from attention and alert signals in the bv, br and tmux refresh before each robot call. **On herdr:** nhm has no incident store and nothing that makes incidents; its `--robot-snapshot` always answers `active_incidents: []`. | `--robot-is-working=S [--panes=P] [--lines=N] [--verbose] [--semantic] [--semantic-window=D]` | — | ntm tells for each pane of a live session whether its agent is working, idle, rate-limited or low on context, and what to do. It reads each pane (`--lines`, 100 here), parses it with its agent parser, checks the prompt box for unsent input and queued messages, and checks for a dialog. `--panes` takes N, W.P or %N, or `all`. `--verbose` (old name `--is-working-verbose`) adds `raw_sample`. `--semantic` adds progress from `git log` and br over `--semantic-window` (else `[robot.semantic] window_minutes`, else 30m). Other codes: `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `INVALID_FLAG`, `TIMEOUT`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`. **On herdr:** herdr's state: working is `DO_NOT_INTERRUPT`, idle or done `SAFE_TO_RESTART`, blocked `MANUAL_INTERVENTION`, unknown `UNKNOWN`. nhm's limit recogniser gives the rate limit, the send core's delivery gate `safe_to_dispatch`, and herdr's process facts the pid, the command and a dead CLI. herdr gives no context level or queued messages. Each filter has two names that share one value; neither name is marked deprecated. `--category` is also the filter of `--robot-ensemble-modes`. JSON `JFPListOutput`: `count, prompts` (jfp's `prompts` array as given). jfp output with no `prompts` array is `LIST_FAILED`. Codes: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (no jfp, with the npm install hint), `LIST_FAILED`. Exit 1 on error; the error envelope is always JSON. **On herdr:** no tmux part. The jfp bridge in nhm's integrations | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.14 (knowledge tools) |
| `--robot-jfp-search=QUERY` | — | ntm searches jfp's prompts: `jfp search <query> --json`, and takes jfp's `results` array. No modifiers. An empty value runs nothing and falls through to ntm's root help. JSON `JFPSearchOutput`: `query, count, results`. Codes: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (no jfp, with the npm install hint), `SEARCH_FAILED`. Exit 1 on error; the error envelope is always JSON. **On herdr:** no tmux part. The jfp bridge in nhm's integrations | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.14 (knowledge tools) |

No tmux, no ntm store. An empty value does not dispatch: ntm prints the root help. Errors, exit 1: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (no jfp on PATH; hint `npm install -g jeffreysprompts`), `NOT_FOUND` (jfp's error says "not found"), else `SHOW_FAILED`. ntm has no jfp verb. **On herdr:** no herdr part. A new `internal/integrations/jfp` adapter, a port of ntm's jfp adapter, that every `--robot-jfp-*` flag shares; the first jfp flag to land brings it | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.14 (knowledge tools) |

A failed `jfp status` only drops `data`. Errors, exit 1: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (the body still has `jfp_available: false`), `HEALTH_CHECK_FAILED` (hint `jfp doctor`). **On herdr:** no herdr part; the shared jfp adapter | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.14 (knowledge tools) |

An empty value prints the root help. Errors, exit 1: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING`; `SUGGEST_FAILED` when jfp fails or its JSON has no `suggestions` array. **On herdr:** no herdr part; the shared jfp adapter | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.14 (knowledge tools) |
| `--robot-jfp-tags` | — | Runs `jfp tags --json` and prints `JFPTagsOutput`: the envelope plus `count` and `tags` (jfp's JSON as is). `count` is the array length when jfp returns a top-level array, else 0: unlike list, search and suggest, ntm does not unwrap an object here. `--jfp-tag` and `--tag` filter only `--robot-jfp-list`. Errors, exit 1: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING`, `TAGS_FAILED`. **On herdr:** no herdr part; the shared jfp adapter | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.14 (knowledge tools) |
| `--robot-jfp-update` | — | Runs `jfp refresh --json` and prints `JFPUpdateOutput`: the envelope plus `result` (jfp's JSON as is). It writes jfp's own registry cache, not ntm's: the only jfp flag with a side effect. Errors, exit 1: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING`, `UPDATE_FAILED`. **On herdr:** no herdr part; the shared jfp adapter | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.14 (knowledge tools) |
| `--robot-kill-agent=S [--panes=P] [--relaunch]` | — | Kills the agent process in each target pane and keeps the pane's shell. `--panes` takes N, W.P or %N, comma-separated; empty means every agent pane. Any other pane flag is `INVALID_FLAG`. ntm walks the process tree under the shell (16 children per node, depth 5), sends SIGTERM, waits 2 s, sends SIGKILL, waits 1 s, and reads the pane again to prove that the shell survived. `--relaunch` (shared with `--robot-exit-cli`) types the pane's saved launch command again and polls up to 10 s for the CLI. Prints `KillAgentOutput{success, timestamp, session, results[]{pane, target, agent_type, shell_pid, agent_pids, exited, killed, shell_preserved, relaunched, detail, verification_failed}}`. Codes: `INVALID_FLAG` (no session, a bad selector, no agent pane matched, a user pane selected), `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `INTERNAL_ERROR` (tmux, a bad launch spec, or a pane not exited, preserved or relaunched); exit 1. **On herdr:** `pane.process_info` gives `shell_pid` and the foreground processes; nhm kills the tree from `/proc`. Relaunch is `agent.start` in the same pane, from the binding's kind and args, as recycle starts a successor (herdr has no launch-spec option). Engines: core/lifecycle, core/bindings, integrations/kinds. **Rulings:** after a kill without `--relaunch`, is the binding displaced, or still live? | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.11 (pane lifecycle) |

No transcript, identity or bead step; no ntm store. Prints `KillPaneOutput{success, timestamp, session, removed[]{pane, target, title, agent_type}, failed[], remaining_panes}`. Codes: `INVALID_FLAG` (no `--panes`, a bad selector, the user pane without `--force`, or every pane, with the hint "Use 'ntm kill SESSION'"), `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `INTERNAL_ERROR` (tmux, or "one or more panes could not be removed"); exit 1. Twin verb: `kill <session> --pane P`. **On herdr:** `pane.close` for each target; selectors are the slot `N.M` or a herdr pane id; the snapshot model gives the topology. A pane with no agent is a raw close. | `--robot-limit=N` (with `--robot-status`, `--robot-snapshot`, `--robot-history` or `--robot-ensemble-modes`) | — | A global modifier, never a command. Four commands read it: `--robot-status` and `--robot-snapshot` (they page `sessions[]`; not the snapshot's `--since` form), `--robot-history` and `--robot-ensemble-modes`. Other commands (triage, label-attention, search and the like) read `--limit` or a limit of their own, never this flag. Default 0, no limit. `--limit=N` counts only when `--robot-limit` is not given. With limit and offset both 0 the output does not change and has no `pagination` key. Else ntm slices the list and adds `pagination{limit, offset, count, total, has_more, next_cursor}`; status and snapshot page after the summary is built (all sessions count) and add `_agent_hints{next_offset, pages_remaining}`. The shared `--limit` is not read, and `--panes` is `INVALID_FLAG`. ntm captures max(2×limit, 200) lines with a 5 s timeout, strips ANSI, trims trailing blank lines and keeps the last `limit`; a failed capture becomes one line `[error capturing pane output: …]`. The session is inferred only (the current tmux session, or a project dir that matches the cwd); a positional session is `INVALID_FLAG`. | `--robot-pipeline-list` | — | Lists pipeline runs: the in-process registry plus every stored run under the `.ntm/pipelines` found upward from the cwd, so runs of another project are not listed. It reads no `--robot-limit` or `--robot-offset`. Prints `PipelineListOutput` (the pipeline envelope): `pipelines[]{run_id, workflow_id, session, status, started_at, finished_at, progress}`, newest first, `[]` when empty, and `_agent_hints{summary: "N pipelines: R running, C completed, F failed", suggestions}`. Always exit 0. Twin verb: `pipeline list`. **On herdr:** part of the pipeline engine; it reads the engine's run-state store | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.25 (pipeline and workflows) |
| `--robot-pipeline-run=FILE --session=S [--vars=JSON] [--dry-run] [--background] [--pipeline-start-from=STEP] [--pipeline-from-state=RUN_ID]` | — | Runs a workflow file (`.yaml`, `.yml` or `.toml`) in a live session. `--session` is required (a bad one is `INVALID_FLAG`). `--vars` takes a JSON object (its error hint says KEY=VALUE, wrongly). `--dry-run` validates, runs the dry executor and adds `side_effect_manifest`. `--background` starts a detached `__pipeline-worker` and returns at once. `--pipeline-from-state` needs `--pipeline-start-from`. A foreground run blocks to the end (5 min per step, 30 min in all). Steps send prompts to panes chosen by `agent`, `pane` or `route` (the `--robot-route` scorer), wait for idle on ntm's screen classifier, capture output, run `/bin/sh -c` commands, Agent Mail steps (send, inbox check, file reservations) and br `bead_query` steps. State goes to `<project>/.ntm/pipelines/<run-id>.json`. Prints `PipelineRunOutput` (the pipeline envelope; a refusal before the run uses the robot envelope): `run_id, workflow_id, session, status, dry_run, warnings[], progress{…}, side_effect_manifest, _agent_hints{summary, status_cmd, cancel_cmd}`. Exit 0 only for a completed run or a started background run; a failed run is `INTERNAL_ERROR`, exit 1; every input check is `INVALID_FLAG`. Twin verb: `pipeline run <file>`. **On herdr:** a new engine in internal/core: prompts through core/send, waits through core/wait on herdr's states, output through herdr pane reads, pane choice through core/bindings and kinds, Mail steps through integrations/agentmail (nhm already sends mail; a step that reserves files needs the reservation ruling), `bead_query` through br, run state in internal/store or ntm's layout, and nhm's own `__pipeline-worker`. ntm's pipeline package is about 24,500 lines | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.25 (pipeline and workflows) |
| `--robot-plan` | — | Prints ntm's session-onboarding plan plus bv's top 5 priorities. It is not `bv --robot-plan`, though the help says so. ntm lists tmux sessions (name, attached, windows) and runs `bv --robot-priority` in the cwd. Prints `PlanOutput`: the envelope plus `generated_at`, `recommendation`, `actions[]{priority, command, description, args}`, `bead_actions[]` (bv's top 5) and `warnings[]`. Always success: no tmux gives a "brew install tmux" action, and a bv failure is a warning. The actions name ntm verbs (`ntm spawn myproject --cc=2`, `ntm attach S`, `ntm palette`, `ntm dashboard`, `ntm tutorial`). **On herdr:** herdr workspaces replace tmux sessions; "attached" means that a herdr client shows the workspace (snapshot model). The actions name `nhm` for `ntm`, as `--robot-spawn`'s safety sentence does. nhm's bv integration has `PriorityResponse`. Engines: core/snapshot, integrations/bv | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.13 (br and bv wrappers) |
| `--robot-probe=S [--panes=P] [--probe-method=keystroke_echo\|interrupt_test\|wake_ping] [--probe-timeout=MS] [--probe-aggressive]` | — | Tests whether each pane's agent responds. Default panes: all that are not the user pane, a service pane or dead. `keystroke_echo` (default) and `wake_ping` (agent panes only) type Space then Backspace; `interrupt_test` sends the kind's interrupt key (Ctrl-C or Escape). ntm hashes a capture before, checks the pane's identity again before input, and compares a capture after. `--probe-timeout` is 100 to 60000 ms, default 5000. `--probe-aggressive` (keystroke_echo only) escalates to interrupt_test. A bad method or timeout adds `valid_methods`, `min_timeout` and `max_timeout` to the error. Prints `ProbeSessionOutput{success, session, probes[]{pane, pane_ref, selector, pane_id, responsive, probe_method, probe_details{input_sent, output_changed, latency_ms, …}, confidence, recommendation, reasoning, error}, summary{total_probed, responsive, unresponsive, errors}}`; `recommendation` is `healthy`, `likely_stuck`, `definitely_stuck` or `unknown`. Codes: `INVALID_FLAG`, `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `PANE_NOT_FOUND`, `NOT_IMPLEMENTED` (Grok, exit 2), `INTERNAL_ERROR`, and `TIMEOUT` when any probe is unresponsive or fails (exit 1). **On herdr:** herdr `pane.send_keys`, with `pane.read` before and after; the interrupt key from kinds, as `--robot-interrupt` takes it; herdr's agent state is a second signal. The probe types into the composer, so it must hold the send core's delivery gate. Engines: core/wait, the herdr client, kinds | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.12 (agent screens) |
| `--robot-productivity=S [--productivity-window=D]` | — | Decides whether a session's agents still make progress. `--productivity-window` is a duration > 0, default 30m (`--wait-until=converged` uses it too). Evidence per pane: git commits with the trailer `NTM-Pane: <session>/<w>.<p>` that ntm stamps, br claim activity by the pane's label, the `br ready` count, and live build or test processes whose cwd is the pane's directory. Prints `ProductivityOutput{success, timestamp, session, window_seconds, decision, decision_reason, ready_bead_count, ready_bead_delta, panes[]{pane, agent_type, progress, builds[]}, build_processes[]{pid, command}, evidence_complete}`; `decision` is `continue`, `converged` or `unknown`. Codes: `INVALID_FLAG`, `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`; exit 0 or 1. **On herdr:** herdr `pane.process_info` gives each pane's foreground processes with cmdline and cwd; br through integrations. Blocker: the evidence rests on ntm's pane token (the commit trailer and the bead label), which nhm's agents do not stamp; without it every pane reads `unknown`. nhm needs a token of its own (the binding id or the Mail name) or ntm's. Engines: core/snapshot, integrations (worksource, br) | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.19 (history and analytics) |
| `--robot-profile-list` | — | Lists ntm's spawn profiles: `<config dir>/profiles/*.toml` (the config dir from `--config`, default `~/.config/ntm`). Prints `SessionProfileListOutput`: only `success` and `profiles[]` (names, sorted, `[]` when none); no robot envelope, no timestamp. A plain error (an unreadable or symlinked dir) is `INTERNAL_ERROR`, exit 1. Schema name `profile_list`. Twin verb: `profile list`. **On herdr:** no herdr part. nhm has no spawn profiles: port the profile store (TOML keys cc, cod, gmi, agy, ollama, cursor, windsurf, aider, oc, omp, user_pane, prompt, init_file, safety, worktrees) into nhm's config dir. `profile save\|show\|delete` and spawn's `--profile` share the store | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.7 (spawn catalogs) |
| `--robot-profile-show=NAME` | — | Prints one spawn profile, `<config dir>/profiles/<name>.toml`. The name must match `^[a-zA-Z0-9][a-zA-Z0-9_-]*$`. Prints `SessionProfileShowOutput`: `success`, `name` and `profile{cc, cod, gmi, agy, ollama, cursor, windsurf, aider, oc, omp, user_pane, prompt, init_file, safety, worktrees}` (zero fields left out); no robot envelope. A missing profile ("profile %q not found") or a bad name is a plain error, so `INTERNAL_ERROR`, exit 1, not a not-found code. Twin verb: `profile show <name>` (it prints the raw TOML). **On herdr:** no herdr part; the profile store of `--robot-profile-list` | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.7 (spawn catalogs) |
| `--robot-proxy-status` | — | Prints the state of `rust_proxy`. ntm runs `rust_proxy --version` (with a minimum version) and `--help`, then the first status form that answers: `status --json`, `daemon status --json`, `status`; 30 s budget. Prints `ProxyStatusOutput{proxy{daemon_running, version, listen_port, uptime_seconds, routes[]{domain, upstream, active, bytes_in, bytes_out, requests, errors}, failover_events[]{timestamp, domain, from, to, reason}}}`. Errors: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (not installed, or not compatible), `INTERNAL_ERROR`. **On herdr:** no herdr part and no nhm engine. A pure tool wrapper: a new rust_proxy adapter in internal/integrations | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.15 (ops tools) |
| `--robot-quota-check --provider=P` | — | Prints one provider's quota. `--provider` is required (`--quota-check-provider` is deprecated) and is folded: `claude`, `anthropic` or `cc` to `claude`; `openai`, `codex`, `cod` or `chatgpt` to `openai`; `gemini`, `gmi` or `google` to `gemini`. Sources: `caut usage --format json [--provider P]` when ntm's cached poll is stale (25 s), then, for claude and openai, caam's live window (`caam limits <claude\|codex> --format json --rank earliest-reset-headroom`, 30 s). No tmux. Prints `QuotaCheckOutput{provider, quota{usage_percent, requests_used, requests_limit, tokens_used, tokens_limit, cost_usd, reset_at, status}}`; `status` is `ok` below 80, `warning` from 80, `critical` from 95. Errors: `INVALID_FLAG` (no provider), `NOT_IMPLEMENTED` for `omp` (exit 2, `status: unsupported`), `NOT_FOUND` (neither caut nor caam has data). Not the `quota` verb, which reads `/usage` from the agents' screens. **On herdr:** no herdr part. integrations/quota (`nhm quota`, `nhm limits`) reads Claude's statusLine records and Codex's session logs: five-hour and weekly windows and their resets. Map nhm's pool to ntm's provider key (`codex` to `openai`), and give `usage_percent` from the binding window and `reset_at`. Request, token and cost counts have no nhm source (nhm does not read caut). Pools that nhm reads as unknown (agy, pi) answer `NOT_FOUND` | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.18 (accounts, quota and context) |
| `--robot-quota-status` | — | Prints every provider's quota from the sources of `--robot-quota-check`, plus a caut availability probe (5 s); caam's window fills only the fields that caut left empty. Prints `QuotaStatusOutput{quota{last_updated, caut_available, providers{name: ProviderQuota}, total_cost_today_usd, total_cost_month_usd, has_warning, has_critical}}`. No error is fatal: a caut polling error is `error_code: INTERNAL_ERROR` with `success: true`, exit 0. With no caut and no caam, `providers: {}`. **On herdr:** no herdr part. integrations/quota `Collect`, the source of `--robot-health`'s backoff: one provider entry per pool, renamed to ntm's provider keys (`codex` to `openai`). `has_warning` and `has_critical` use ntm's 80 and 95 on the larger window. The cost totals stay 0, as ntm writes them when caut has no data. `nhm quota --json` prints these pools in nhm's envelope | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.18 (accounts, quota and context) |
| `--robot-rano-stats [--panes=1,2] [--rano-window=D]` | — | Prints network connection counts per agent pane from rano's database, across every tmux session. `--panes` takes pane indices only (else `INVALID_FLAG`), and an index matches that index in each session. `--rano-window` defaults to `5m`; config `[integrations.rano] sqlite_path` names the database. ntm runs `rano --version`, `rano status`, then `rano export --format jsonl` for the window, and maps events to panes by each agent pane's PID and its `/proc` tree. Prints `RanoStatsOutput{window, query{panes_requested}, panes{key: {pane_id, window_index, session, pane_index, pane_title, agent_type, ntm_index, pids[], connection_count, last_connection, providers{}}}, total{connection_count}, database, measurement, attribution, unavailable_metrics}` (`connection_events`, `current_process_tree`, and `http_requests, bytes_in, bytes_out` unavailable). Errors: `INVALID_FLAG`, `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (no rano, not compatible, status failed, no database), `PERMISSION_DENIED` (`/proc` unreadable), `INTERNAL_ERROR`. **On herdr:** `pane.process_info` gives `shell_pid` in place of tmux's pane PID; the `/proc` walk stays. Agent panes are the bound agents (core/bindings) across workspaces. herdr has no pane index: take the slot `N.M` or the pane id, as the other robot flags do. A new rano adapter; rano is not in nhm's `deps` list | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.15 (ops tools) |
| `--robot-rch-status` | — | Prints rch's state. ntm runs `rch --version`, `rch help` (a capability probe) and `rch status --json`; 30 s budget. No tmux, no store. Prints `RCHStatusOutput{rch{enabled, available, version, workers{total, healthy, busy}, session_stats{builds_total, builds_remote, builds_local, time_saved_seconds}}}`. Errors: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (not installed, or not compatible), `INTERNAL_ERROR` (the probe or `rch status` failed). **On herdr:** no herdr part. A pure tool wrapper: a new rch adapter. integrations/guards knows only whether a kind's settings load the RCH hook, not rch's daemon state. rch is not in nhm's `deps` list | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.15 (ops tools) |
| `--robot-rch-workers [--worker=NAME]` | — | Lists rch's workers from `rch status --json`. `--worker` takes an exact name; no match is `INVALID_FLAG` "worker 'X' not found". Prints `RCHWorkersOutput{worker, workers[]{name, host, status, current_build, builds_completed, cpu_percent, load, queue, last_seen, available, healthy}}`, sorted by name, `[]` when empty. Errors: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING`, `INVALID_FLAG`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`. **On herdr:** no herdr part; the rch adapter of `--robot-rch-status` | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.15 (ops tools) |
| `--robot-recipes` | — | Lists spawn recipes: the built-ins (quick-claude, full-stack, minimal, codex-heavy, balanced, review-team), `~/.config/ntm/recipes.toml` and `<cwd>/.ntm/recipes.toml`. Prints `RecipesOutput`: the envelope plus `generated_at`, `count` and `recipes[]{name, description, source, total_agents, agents[]{type, count, model, persona}}`; `source` is `builtin`, `user` or `project`. A recipe file that does not load is `INTERNAL_ERROR` (the body has `recipes: []`), exit 1. Twin verb: `recipes list`. The flag's help example `--spawn-preset=standard` names no built-in recipe. **On herdr:** no herdr part. nhm has no recipes: port ntm's recipe loader, with nhm's kinds for `type`. It feeds `--robot-spawn --spawn-preset`, which nhm does not take either (a gap of that row) | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.7 (spawn catalogs) |
| `--robot-replay=S --id=ID [--dry-run]` | — | Sends a history entry's prompt again, to the entry's target panes in the named live session (it replaces the entry's own session). `--id` (or `--replay-id`) names the entry, which must be in the last 100 of ntm's prompt history (`$XDG_DATA_HOME/ntm/history.jsonl`, else `~/.local/share/ntm/history.jsonl`). `--dry-run` (or `--replay-dry-run`) sends nothing. The send is a full robot send, recorded as a replay, with no `--op-id`, so a retry sends again. Prints `ReplayOutput{success, timestamp, history_id, original_command, session, target_panes[], replayed, send_result, _agent_hints}` (`send_result` is a `SendOutput`), in the send's envelope. Codes: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (no history), `INVALID_FLAG` (the id is not in the last 100), and the send's codes. Twin verbs: `replay [index\|id]`, `history replay`. **On herdr:** nhm's history store (internal/store/history, under `$XDG_DATA_HOME/nhm`), which core/send already appends to; the replay goes through core/send (receipts, delivery gate). The targets are the slots or pane ids that nhm stored | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.19 (history and analytics) |
| `--robot-restart-pane=S [--panes=P] [--type=K] [--all] [--dry-run] [--restart-bead=ID] [--restart-prompt=T] [--restart-model=M] [--restart-agent-args=A]` | — | Restarts agents in place: ntm kills each pane's process (`respawn-pane -k`), types the agent's launch command and polls for ready. `--panes` defaults to every agent pane, and each selector is checked before tmux is touched. `--type` filters by kind, `--all` adds the user pane, `--dry-run` (or `--restore-dry-run`) lists `would_affect`. `--restart-bead` needs exactly one agent pane: it shows the bead, checks bv's plan, claims it with br's compare-and-set, records ntm's assignment ledger and sends the bead prompt through ntm's dispatch. `--restart-prompt` wins over the bead template. `--restart-model` (`model` or `model@effort`; claude, codex or gemini) is checked against every target first. `--restart-agent-args` appends raw args. Prints `RestartPaneOutput{success, session, restarted_at, restarted[], failed[]{pane, reason}, dry_run, would_affect[], bead_assigned, prompt_sent, prompt_delivery{}, dispatch_receipt_id, …, pane_shell_pids{before, after}}`. Codes: `INVALID_FLAG`, `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `PANE_NOT_FOUND`, `NOT_IMPLEMENTED` (Grok, exit 2), `TIMEOUT`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`, `RESTART_PROMPT_NOT_DELIVERED`, `PROMPT_SEND_FAILED`. Twin verb: `respawn` (`restart`). **On herdr:** no `respawn-pane -k`. nhm kills the agent's tree from `shell_pid` and runs `agent.start` in the same pane; the shell PID stays, so ntm's check that `after` differs from `before` cannot hold. `--restart-bead` maps to core/spawnassign and core/send. ntm restarts with no handoff; `nhm recycle` adds a handoff and a kickoff, so the two must not share one user-visible path. Engines: core/lifecycle, core/spawnassign, core/send, kinds | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.11 (pane lifecycle) |
| `--robot-restore=NAME [--dry-run]` | — | Rebuilds a saved session's topology; no agent starts. The value is a saved-state name, not a path, though the help and its example say "backup.json": ntm sanitizes it and reads `~/.ntm/sessions/<name>.json`, so `--robot-restore=backup.json` fails with "no saved session named", and a file that `--robot-save --output` wrote cannot be restored. `--dry-run` (or `--restore-dry-run`) prints a preview. ntm creates the session, splits, layout, window names and zoom, and writes the audit log. Prints `RestoreResult{success, timestamp, saved_name, restored_as, dry_run, state, preview{session_name, work_dir, pane_count, agent_count, layout, actions[]}}`. Codes: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (no tmux), `INVALID_FLAG` (no name, or the session exists), `INTERNAL_ERROR` (the load or restore failed). Twin verb: `sessions restore <saved-name>` without `--launch`. **On herdr:** herdr `workspace.create`, `tab.create` and `pane.split` from the saved state, with no agents. Not `nhm admin restore`, which checks bindings again after herdr's own restore. Engines: the herdr client, core/lifecycle. Blocker: the saved-state format of `--robot-save` | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.23 (save and restore) |
| `--robot-route=S [--strategy=STRAT] [--type=K] [--exclude=1,2] [--last-agent=%N] [--msg=M\|--msg-file=F]` | named in ru's automated-review design doc (`--robot-route=<session> --type=cc`); no script calls it | Recommends a pane for the next task. It is advisory and changes no state (round-robin does not advance). `--strategy` (or `--route-strategy`) defaults to least-loaded; the others are first-available, round-robin, round-robin-available, random, sticky, explicit and affinity. `--type` (or `--route-type`) filters by kind; `--exclude` (or `--route-exclude`) takes pane indices; `--last-agent` (%N) serves sticky and round-robin; `--msg` or `--msg-file` gives the text for affinity. ntm scores each pane from a screen capture (activity, context %), Agent Mail file reservations (for affinity, or when `[agent_mail]` is on) and its routing state, read only. Prints `RouteOutput{success, timestamp, session, strategy, recommendation{pane_id, pane_index, agent_type, score, reason, context_usage, state}, candidates[], excluded[]{pane_id, pane_index, agent_type, reason}, fallback_used, _agent_hints}`. Codes: `INVALID_FLAG` (session, strategy, exclude), `INVALID_ARGS` (msg), `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`. Twin: `send --smart` and `send --route=STRAT`. **On herdr:** herdr's state replaces the classifier: idle or done is available, working is busy, blocked is excluded. Affinity can use integrations/agentmail reservations; round-robin needs a table in nhm's store. Context % has no nhm source. Engines: core/snapshot, integrations/agentmail, internal/store | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.9 (work distribution) |
| `--robot-ru-sync [--dry-run]` | — | Runs `ru sync --non-interactive --json [--dry-run]` (5 min timeout) and parses ru's `data.repos[]{name, status}`. It acts: it pulls and clones the repos that ru manages. `--restore-dry-run` does not count here. No tmux. Prints `RUSyncOutput{dry_run, repos{synced[], skipped[]}, conflicts[], failed[], duration_ms, exit_code, stdout, stderr}` and `_meta` with ru's exit code. Errors by ru's exit code: 2 `RU_CONFLICTS`, 1 `RU_PARTIAL_FAILURE`, 3 `DEPENDENCY_MISSING`, 5 `RU_SYNC_INTERRUPTED`; a timeout is `TIMEOUT`, no ru is `DEPENDENCY_MISSING`, else `INTERNAL_ERROR`. Every error exits 1. Twin verb: `repo sync`. **On herdr:** no herdr part. A pure tool wrapper: a new ru adapter. ru is also an nhm caller (its own `--robot-*` forms), not a dependency | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.15 (ops tools) |
| `--robot-safety-simulate [--command=C] [--step=C]…` | — | Checks commands against ntm's policy and runs none of them. `--command` (shared with `--robot-dcg-check`; `--cmd` is deprecated) comes first, then each repeatable `--step`. An empty plan is success, `steps: []`, `safe_to_run: true`. The policy is `~/.ntm/policy.yaml`, else `./.ntm/policy.yaml`, else the built-in default; no dcg, slb or tmux runs. Prints `SafetySimulationOutput{generated_at, commands[], steps[]{index, command, decision, action, would_be_allowed, requires_approval, requires_slb, policy, error, safer_alternatives[], policy_provenance[]}, summary{total_steps, allowed_steps, blocked_steps, approval_steps, invalid_steps, slb_required_steps}, safe_to_run, notes[]}` and `_meta`; `notes` always holds "simulation only; no command was executed". A policy that does not load is `INTERNAL_ERROR`; one that does not compile is success with `safe_to_run: false` and a note. With `--robot-dcg-check` on the same line, dcg-check wins. Twin verb: `safety simulate`. **On herdr:** no herdr part. nhm has no command policy; integrations/guards reports only whether dcg's hook is loaded. Port ntm's policy package (the YAML policy, its block, approve and allow rules, and its SLB marks) | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.20 (safety and policy) |
| `--robot-save=S [--output=F]` | — | Saves a session's state: panes, windows, layout strings, titles and commands, plus the work dir's git branch, remote and commit. ntm writes `~/.ntm/sessions/<sanitized session>.json` (it always overwrites), then a copy at `--output` (`--save-output` is deprecated), and writes the audit log. Prints `SaveResult{success, timestamp, session, saved_as, file_path, state{name, saved_at, cwd, git_branch, git_remote, git_commit, agents{…}, panes[], layout, windows[], …}}`. Codes: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (no tmux), `INVALID_FLAG`, `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`. Twin verb: `sessions save [session]`; `ntm save` is another verb (pane outputs to files). **On herdr:** herdr already keeps workspaces, tabs and panes across a restart, and `nhm admin restore` checks bindings again. ntm's state holds tmux layout strings and pane commands that have no herdr form; herdr `pane.layout` gives rects. core/lifecycle's export already writes bindings to a directory. Engines: core/snapshot, core/lifecycle, core/bindings. Blocker: a herdr layout format, and nhm's place for saved states | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.23 (save and restore) |
| `--robot-schema=TYPE`, `--schema=TYPE` | — | Prints the JSON Schema of one ntm robot type, made by reflection over the Go type; `all` prints every type. `--schema` is the same flag. Schema names are snake_case keys (`status`, `snapshot`, `setup_status`, `acfs_status`, `profile_list`, `pipeline_run`, …): about 134 in ntm's list, plus those that other packages register. Prints `SchemaOutput`: the envelope plus `schema_type` and `schema` (one type) or `schemas[]` (all). An unknown type is `INVALID_FLAG` "unknown schema type", with a hint that lists the types; exit 1. ntm's release build (`-tags ensemble_experimental`) describes the tagged `EnsembleSpawnOutput` for `ensemble_spawn` (`action, session, preset, question, project_dir, assignment, agents{}, modes[], budget, status, warnings[]`), not the stub's `action, session`: nhm pins the release shape. **On herdr:** no herdr part. nhm holds ntm's types only for the forms it implements (internal/robot); a faithful `all` needs every ntm robot type, or a pinned dump of ntm's schemas. Port ntm's schema generator | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.2 (robot grammar) |
| `--robot-search=QUERY [--limit=N]` | — | Searches beads: runs `bv --robot-search --search <q> [--search-limit=N]` in the cwd. `--limit` counts only when given; else bv's own default. Prints `SearchOutput`: the envelope plus `query`, `available`, `results` (bv's `SearchResponse`) and `error`. Errors, exit 1: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`. Twin verb: `work search`, not `search`, which searches CASS. **On herdr:** no herdr part. nhm's bv integration has `SearchResponse`; add the call and the robot wrapper | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.13 (br and bv wrappers) |
| `--robot-sequence=NAME [--sequence-action=create\|next\|advance] [--sequence-steps=JSON] [--sequence-pane=KEY] [--sequence-expected-position=N]` | — | Keeps a named prompt sequence and each pane's position in it. `create` takes `--sequence-steps` (a JSON array of strings). `next` (the default) and `advance` need `--sequence-pane`, an opaque key (a stable pane id or address) that ntm never resolves. `--sequence-expected-position` (an int ≥ 0, advance only) makes a retry a no-op. Files only, under `<cwd>/.ntm/workflows/sequences`; no tmux. Prints `RobotSequenceOutput{success, timestamp, action}` plus `sequence{name, steps[], positions{}, created_at, updated_at}` or `pane{sequence, pane, position, prompt, complete, advanced}`, as plain JSON: the robot format and verbosity do not apply. Every error, store I/O too, is `INVALID_FLAG`; a failed read of the working dir is `INTERNAL_ERROR`. **On herdr:** no herdr part. Port the sequence store, a small file store in the project; nhm's pane key is the herdr pane id or the binding | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.25 (pipeline and workflows) |
| `--robot-setup` | — | An alias of `--robot-acfs-status`. ntm looks up tmux (required), br, bv, claude (cc), codex (cod), gemini (gmi) and git with their versions, and runs `acfs --version`. Prints `ACFSStatusOutput`: the envelope plus `acfs_available`, `acfs_version`, `acfs_path` and `tools{tmux, br, bv, cc, cod, gmi, git: {installed, version, path, hint, required}}`. No acfs on PATH is `DEPENDENCY_MISSING`, exit 1, with `tools` still in the body: on a host without acfs it always fails, even when every tool is installed. Twin verb: `setup status`. **On herdr:** no herdr part; reuse the probes of nhm's `deps`, which already reports herdr as required. **Rulings:** the one of `--robot-acfs-status`: ntm's tool keys (`tmux`, `cc`, `cod`, `gmi`) on herdr | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.15 (ops tools) |
| `--robot-slb-approve=REQUEST_ID` | — | Approves a two-person slb request with the caller's slb session: `slb approve --json --session-id ID --session-key KEY -- REQ` (30 s), with the session from the env variables `SLB_SESSION_ID` and `SLB_SESSION_KEY`. It acts: it signs the approval. No tmux. Prints `SLBActionOutput{request_id, result}` (slb's JSON as is). Errors: `INVALID_FLAG` (an empty id after trim), `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (no slb), `TIMEOUT`, `PERMISSION_DENIED` (no session in the env, your own request, a key mismatch), `NOT_FOUND` ("request not found"), `INTERNAL_ERROR`. **On herdr:** no herdr part and no nhm engine. A new slb adapter in internal/integrations, shared by the three slb flags | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.15 (ops tools) |
| `--robot-slb-deny=REQUEST_ID --reason=R` | — | Rejects an slb request: `slb reject --json --session-id ID --session-key KEY --reason R -- REQ` (the flag says deny; slb's verb is reject). `--reason` is required (empty is `INVALID_FLAG`). The session comes from `SLB_SESSION_ID` and `SLB_SESSION_KEY`. Prints `SLBActionOutput{request_id, result}`. Errors as `--robot-slb-approve`. **On herdr:** no herdr part; the slb adapter | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.15 (ops tools) |
| `--robot-slb-pending` | — | Lists pending slb requests: `slb pending --json` (30 s). Prints `SLBPendingOutput{count, pending}`: `pending` is slb's JSON array (`[]` when slb answers nothing), `count` its length. Errors: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING`, `TIMEOUT`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`. **On herdr:** no herdr part; the slb adapter | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.15 (ops tools) |
| `--robot-smart-restart=S [--panes=P] [--force] [--dry-run] [--prompt=T] [--lines=N] [--verbose] [--hard-kill] [--hard-kill-only]` | — | Restarts agents only when it is safe. A pre-check (the `--robot-is-working` classifier on `--lines` of screen, default 100) decides per pane: a working agent is not restarted without `--force`, a rate-limited one gets `WAITING`, and a `MANUAL_INTERVENTION` gate or an unknown state is `SKIPPED`. ntm types the kind's exit keys (Claude: Ctrl+C twice, 100 ms apart), waits up to 12 s for the shell, kills the tree with `--hard-kill` (or only that, `--hard-kill-only`), types the launch command and polls for ready (at least 10 s). `--prompt` goes after, through ntm's dispatch. `--dry-run` (or `--smart-restart-dry-run`) gives `WOULD_RESTART`. Default panes: all but the control pane. Prints `SmartRestartOutput{success, session, timestamp, dry_run, force, actions{pane: {action, reason, pre_check, restart_sequence, post_state, prompt_outcome, …}}, summary{restarted, skipped, waiting, failed, would_restart, prompt_delivered, prompt_failed, panes_by_action}}`. Codes: `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `PANE_NOT_FOUND`, `INVALID_FLAG`, `NOT_IMPLEMENTED` (Grok), `TIMEOUT`, `SOFT_EXIT_FAILED`, `SHELL_NOT_RETURNED`, `HARD_KILL_FAILED`, `CC_LAUNCH_FAILED`, `PROMPT_SEND_FAILED`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`. **On herdr:** herdr's state replaces the classifier: working is not interrupted, idle or done is safe, blocked is `MANUAL_INTERVENTION`, unknown is skipped. Rate limits come from nhm's limit recogniser and quota, as for `--robot-health`. The restart is the kind's exit (recycle's stop), then `agent.start`; a hard kill uses the `/proc` tree; the prompt goes through core/send. Context-low has no nhm source. Engines: core/snapshot, core/lifecycle, core/send, integrations/quota, kinds | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.18 (accounts, quota and context) |
| `--robot-suggest` | — | Runs `bv --robot-suggest` in the cwd and prints `SuggestOutput`: the envelope plus `available`, `suggestions` (bv's `SuggestionsResponse`) and `error`. Errors, exit 1: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`. No verb twin. **On herdr:** no herdr part. nhm's bv integration has `SuggestionsResponse`; add the call and the robot wrapper | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.13 (br and bv wrappers) |
| `--robot-summary=S [--since=D]` | — | Summarizes a session's agents from their screens. ntm captures the last 500 lines of each agent pane (a fixed count: `--since` sets only `time_range` and the active-time window), finds files, errors and actions in the text by regexp, and runs `git status` in the cwd for conflicts. `--since` (or the deprecated `--summary-since`) defaults to 30m: a duration (minutes when no unit) or a past RFC3339 time. Prints `SessionSummaryResponse{success, timestamp, summary{session, time_range{start, end, duration}, agents[]{pane_id, pane_title, agent_type, active_time, idle_time, output_lines, output_chars, files_modified[], …}, total_files, total_output_lines, conflicts[], timestamp}}`. Codes: `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `INVALID_FLAG` (since), `INTERNAL_ERROR`. Twin verb: `summary [session]`. **On herdr:** herdr `pane.read` (500 recent lines) and herdr's agent state replace the capture and the pane state; port ntm's text extractors; git from the project. Engines: core/snapshot, a ported synthesis package | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.8 (pane reads) |
| `--robot-support-bundle=S [--bundle-output=F] [--bundle-format=zip\|tar.gz\|tgz] [--bundle-since=D] [--bundle-lines=N] [--bundle-max-size=N] [--bundle-redact=MODE] [--all] [--allow-secret]` | — | Writes a diagnostic archive in the cwd. The session is optional, but the flag takes a value: a bare flag takes the next argument, and `--robot-support-bundle=` means no session. A named session must be live. Without a session and without `--all` (every session), the bundle holds only config files. ntm captures `--bundle-lines` (1000) of each pane; a private session gets `PRIVACY_SUPPRESSED.txt` unless `--allow-secret`. It adds `~/.ntm/config.toml`, `palettes.yaml` and `themes.yaml` (not ntm's default config path), and redacts by `--bundle-redact` (`redact`, the default, `off`, `warn` or `block`). Defaults: `ntm-<session>-<YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS>.zip` (or `ntm-bundle-…`), `zip`, 100 MB. Prints `SupportBundleOutput{path, format, file_count, total_size, redaction_summary{mode, total_findings, files_scanned, files_redacted, category_counts}, errors[], warnings[], privacy_mode, privacy_sessions[], content_suppressed}`. Errors: `INVALID_FLAG` (format, since, redact mode), `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`. Twin verb: `support-bundle`, with other flags and defaults (200 lines, 50 MiB). **On herdr:** herdr pane reads (`recent`, N lines) over the workspace's bound agents replace capture-pane. nhm's redaction package is a partial port: no warn or block mode and no summary counts. nhm's config and store files replace ntm's dotfiles. Port ntm's bundle generator | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.10 (config and setup) |
| `--robot-switch-account=PROVIDER[:ACCOUNT]` | — | Switches the host's active account for a provider with caam: `caam activate <claude\|codex\|gemini> <ACCOUNT> --json`, or `caam activate <provider> --auto --json` for the next account (it reads a cooldown); 30 s. The provider is folded as for `--robot-quota-check`. `--pane` is refused: "Account switching is global per provider". Running agents are neither restarted nor told, and `panes_affected` is never filled. Prints `SwitchAccountOutput{switch{success, provider, previous_account, new_account, panes_affected, cooldown_seconds, error}}`. Errors: `INVALID_FLAG` (no provider, `--pane`), `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (no caam), `INTERNAL_ERROR` (caam failed). Not `rotate`, which rotates one pane's account and restarts that agent. **On herdr:** no herdr part. nhm only reads caam (integrations/quota's credential reader). A switch invalidates that pool's records (nhm-kyt), so `nhm quota` reads the pool as unknown until a record of the new credential comes. **Rulings:** this would be nhm's first write to caam, on host-wide credentials: may nhm do it? | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.18 (accounts, quota and context) |
| `--robot-terse` | — | Prints one text line, not JSON, built from the full snapshot. Per session: `S:<name>\|A:<active>/<total>\|W:<working>\|I:<idle>\|E:<errors>\|C:<ctx%>\|B:R<ready>/I<in-progress>/B<blocked>\|M:<unread>\|^:<Na,Ni or 0>\|!:<Nc,Nw or 0>`, with sessions joined by `;`. ntm adds `\|T:feed:unavail` when the attention feed is down; with no sessions it prints `S:-`. Mail, bead and alert counts are global, repeated on each session's line. No session filter. A failed snapshot prints `TerseOutput{states[], terse_lines[], attention_hint}` as the JSON error, exit 1. **On herdr:** built on nhm's snapshot model: A, W and I from herdr's agent states; herdr has no error state, so E counts blocked or gone agents; M from Agent Mail. C (context %) has no herdr source and reads 0. B needs bv's ready, in-progress and blocked counts, and ^ needs an attention feed. Engines: core/snapshot, integrations/bv, integrations/agentmail | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.22 (attention and alerts) |
| `--robot-tokens [--days=N] [--since=D] [--group-by=agent\|model\|day\|week\|month] [--session=S] [--agent=A]` | — | Prints token use, estimated from prompt length, from ntm's analytics log (`~/.config/ntm/analytics/events.jsonl`: spawn, add and prompt-send events). `--days` defaults to 30; `--since` (RFC3339 or `YYYY-MM-DD`) wins over it; `--group-by` defaults to `agent`. `--session` also filters on a session that is not live: only an invalid name or an ambiguous prefix fails. The `--tokens-*` forms are deprecated. Prints `TokensOutput`: the envelope plus `period`, `generated_at`, `group_by`, `total_tokens`, `total_prompts`, `total_characters`, `breakdown[]{key, tokens, prompts, characters, percentage}`, `agent_stats{}`, `model_stats{}`, `time_stats[]` and `_agent_hints`. A missing log is success with zeros. Errors, exit 1: `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `INVALID_FLAG` (since, group-by), `INTERNAL_ERROR` (an unreadable log). Twin verb: `analytics` (the same log). **On herdr:** no herdr part. nhm keeps no ntm events log; its store holds send receipts (`payload_bytes`, target, kind) and bindings (spawns, model). The port maps those onto ntm's buckets and estimator: a new read over internal/store | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.19 (history and analytics) |
| `--robot-tools` | — | Lists ntm's 21 tool adapters (bv, bd, am, cm, cass, s2p, jfp, dcg, slb, acfs, ru, ms, xf, giil, ubs, caam, rch, rano, caut, pt, rust_proxy), each with its `--version`, capability and health probe; 30 s in all. Tools that config disables (cass, cm, am, rch, pt, rano, xf, by their `enabled` keys) are marked. tmux is not in the list. Prints `ToolsOutput`: the envelope plus `tools[]{name, installed, version, path, capabilities[], health{healthy, message, error, latency_ms, last_checked}, required, disabled}` (sorted) and `health_report{total, healthy, unhealthy, missing, disabled, tools{}}`. Always success; only bv is `required`. **On herdr:** no herdr part. nhm's `deps` (ntm's dependency check) and `doctor` cover presence and versions, but nhm has no tool registry with per-tool capability and health probes. Port ntm's registry and a probe per tool | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.15 (ops tools) |
| `--robot-triage [--limit=N\|--triage-limit=N]` | — | Runs `bv --robot-triage` in the cwd (behind an in-process 30 s cache) and prints `TriageOutput`: the envelope plus `generated_at`, `available`, `data_hash`, `error`, `quick_ref`, `recommendations[]`, `quick_wins[]`, `blockers_to_clear[]`, `project_health`, `commands{}` and `cache_info{cached, age_ms, ttl_ms}`. The limit (`--limit`, default 10; `--triage-limit` is deprecated) cuts `recommendations` only. In a one-shot CLI the cache is cold, so `cached` is false. Errors, exit 1: `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (no bv, or not initialised), `INTERNAL_ERROR`. Twin verb: `work triage`. **On herdr:** no herdr part. nhm's bv integration already ports `GetTriage`, the cache and the triage types (spawn --assign uses them); only the robot wrapper is new | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.13 (br and bv wrappers) |
| `--robot-verbosity=terse\|default\|debug` (on every robot call) | — | A global modifier, never a command. ntm takes the flag, then env `NTM_ROBOT_VERBOSITY`, then config `robot.verbosity`, else `default`. A bad value in a robot call is `INVALID_FLAG`, with the hint "Use terse, default, or debug". It shapes every successful robot answer and `--robot-capabilities`, but not an error envelope or `--robot-terse` (its own line format). `terse` marshals the payload again, renames keys by ntm's terse key map and drops every `_agent_hints`. `debug` adds `_debug{format, verbosity, go_version, os, arch, payload_type}`; a list payload becomes `{_debug, items}`. `default` changes nothing. **On herdr:** no herdr part. One encoder step in nhm's robot writer (internal/cli) and its copies; port ntm's `TerseKeyMap`, because it decides what the caller reads. nhm today answers `NOT_IMPLEMENTED`, exit 2, and ignores `NTM_ROBOT_VERBOSITY` | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.2 (robot grammar) |
| `--robot-version` | — | Prints `VersionOutput`: the envelope plus `system{version, commit, build_date, go_version, os, arch, tmux_available}`. It reads only build variables and the Go runtime. Always success, exit 0. `tmux_available` is never set here, so it is always `false`. Twin verbs: `version` (`--short`) and `--version`. **On herdr:** no herdr part. nhm answers `version`, `--version` and `version --json` (its own `VersionInfo`), but not this flag. nhm already has ntm's `SystemInfo` and the build info of `--robot-status`: a wrapper only, with `ntm_commit` and the herdr protocol under `_nhm` | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.2 (robot grammar) |
| `--robot-wait-cancel=WAIT_ID` | — | Cancels a running `--robot-wait --wait-id=ID`: ntm marks the handle cancelled in its state store, and the wait, which polls the handle, ends with code `CANCELED`. A blank id (spaces only) is `INVALID_FLAG`; an empty value does not dispatch. Prints `WaitCancelResponse{success, timestamp, wait_id, canceled}`. Codes: `INVALID_FLAG`, `WAIT_NOT_FOUND` (no active wait with that id), `INTERNAL_ERROR` (the store); exit 0 or 1. **On herdr:** no herdr part. A wait-handle table in nhm's SQLite store, and `--wait-id` in `--robot-wait`, which today refuses it with `INVALID_FLAG` (its row; nhm-hir). core/wait watches the handle while it waits. Engines: internal/store, core/wait | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.5 (send family) |
| `--robot-watch-bead=S --bead=ID [--panes=P] [--lines=N] [--interval=D]` | — | Finds mentions of a bead in each pane's recent output, once: it is not a loop, and `--interval` (default 30 s) is only echoed. `--bead` is required. Default panes: all but the user pane; `--lines` defaults to 200. ntm strips ANSI, matches the bead id without case on word boundaries, and reads the status with `br show <id> --json`. Prints `WatchBeadOutput{success, timestamp, session, bead_id, checked_at, interval, panes_scanned, mentions[]{pane, pane_ref, pane_id, agent_type, line, line_num, timestamp}, bead_status, status_error}`; `bead_status` defaults to `unknown`. Codes: `INVALID_FLAG`, `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`. Twin verb: `watch --bead ID` (the streaming form). **On herdr:** `nhm watch --bead` already has ntm's mention regexp and the `br show` status reader; the robot form is one herdr `pane.read` of each agent's recent lines. `--panes` takes the slot `N.M` or a herdr pane id, as the send and wait rows say. Engines: the watch verb's helpers, core/snapshot | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.8 (pane reads) |
| `--robot-xf-search=QUERY [--xf-limit=N] [--xf-mode=lexical\|semantic\|hybrid\|two-tier] [--xf-sort=relevance\|date\|date-desc\|engagement]` | — | Runs `xf search --format json [--limit N] [--mode M] [--sort S] -- <query>` and prints `XFSearchOutput`: the envelope plus `query`, `mode`, `sort`, `count` and `hits[]`. `--xf-limit` defaults to 20 (0 or less is 20); the shared `--limit` is not read. Errors, exit 1: `INVALID_FLAG` (a blank query, or xf says "invalid value" for mode or sort), `DEPENDENCY_MISSING`, `TIMEOUT`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`. No verb twin; ntm's palette offers xf search when `[integrations.xf] enabled`. **On herdr:** no herdr part. nhm already ported xf's search (internal/integrations/xf: detection, search and types) for the palette's xf search; the robot wrapper and the error mapping are new | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.14 (knowledge tools) |
| `--robot-xf-status` | — | Runs `xf --version` and `xf doctor` and prints `XFStatusOutput`: the envelope plus `xf_available`, `healthy`, `version` and `message`. Only `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` (no xf) is an error, exit 1; a failed version or doctor is success with `healthy: false`. **On herdr:** no herdr part. nhm's xf adapter left out ntm's version and health checks; port those two | — | — | deferred: nhm-2p6.14 (knowledge tools) |
